# Save/Load & Versioned Format

> Authoring guidance: .claude/docs/templates/guidance/game-design-document-guide.md (load per-section as you author — do not read entirely).

> **Status**: Designed
> **Author**: user + technical-director, lead-programmer, systems-designer, creative-director (via /design-system, autonomous; orchestrated run 2026-10-26)
> **Last Updated**: 2026-10-27 (consistency pass)
> **Last Verified**: 2026-10-26
> **Implements Pillar**: P5 Deeper Is Richer, Loss Is Not Total (primary — the mechanism half of the failure contract: the PROFILE store that survives a world restore, and a death that costs the minutes since the last autosave and nothing else); **Serves**: P1 The World Never Lies (a save is the true SIM state, never the perception; no thumbnail), P2 Dread Before Chaos (the autosave gate refuses to record a moment the player could not have perceived), P3 The Web Watches (Attention restores exactly; no meter stored for display; no audio cue; no per-write mark that could leak the gate's inputs), P4 Sanity Rewrites Reality (Save/Load never varies by perception and restores every persisted counter and seed exactly; whether perception draws replay identically is Sanity's decision, under a no-reroll-oracle constraint, OQ-37)
> **Creative Director Review (CD-GDD-ALIGN)**: CONCERNS → REVISED 2026-10-26 (5 items applied; skipped: Fifth CD item 'Retained as strengths (no change needed)': SIM-only saves with no thumbnail, no DOWNED/DEAD seat in any file, WORLD-scoped sanity and vitals, honest mechanism-only PROFILE store, the anti-pillar guard-rail table, and the death stub not taking the 2.10 re-wake beat were deliberately left unchanged.; Dormant-section loaded-descriptor filter for the Graph tell is routed, not resolved: LS-19 names no loaded-section predicate, so OQ-38 requests a loaded flag or soonest_warning_remaining(loaded_only) from the Graph owner; until answered a frozen dormant window can defer a save to the cap and tag it RISKY (accepted rare exposure, stated in SL-5 and Edge Cases).; Attention half of the tell condition and breach phase remain provisional (absent = open, GATE_INPUT_ABSENT once per process); not made required because the Attention GDD is undesigned (OQ-24).; Playtest trigger constants (SCOUT_DEATH_WINDOW_TICKS, QUIT_RELAUNCH_WINDOW_S, M1 to M3 thresholds) were not registered as registry entries or data-file knobs; they are provisional playtest-protocol values calibrated at the first outside build.)

## Summary

Save/Load & Versioned Format defines how a campaign of The Synaptic Sea is persisted and restored: a versioned, self-verifying save container (a readable JSON header, binary block payloads and a SHA-256 trailer) written as a rolling ring of autosave generations, the autosave schedule and its fairness gate, Save & Quit, and the MVP death stub that restarts from the last verified autosave. It also owns the PROFILE store that survives a world restore, the load pre-check and its failure classes, and the block-authoring contract every state-owning system must meet. Read it if you design a system that owns persisted state, a death or restart flow, or anything that must survive a quit, a crash or a version bump.

> **Quick reference** — Layer: `Core` · Priority: `MVP` · Key deps: `Command & Simulation Layer (designed; hard upstream); Life-Support Graph (designed; block owner, carries S-1), Player Controller & Input (designed; block owner and the death trigger), Pixel-Perfect Render Pipeline & Camera (designed; consumer of LOADING); Ship Section & Layout Data Model (designed; `layout.state`, the restore_after root); Ship Body & Interior Physics (designed; `body.state`); Emitter State & Light-Exposure Query, Item & Module Database (designed; block owner / header-field provider); Suit & Equipment, Sanity & Hallucination Rules, Attention, Interaction, Modular Ship Assembly, Web Map & Transit, Horror Entities (designed; block owners); Chart Table, HUD & Suit Layer, Debug Overlay (designed; UI and diagnostic consumers)`


## Overview

Save/Load & Versioned Format is the persistence layer of The Synaptic Sea. In one sentence: it is the host-owned writer, verifier and migrator of the world's saved state, and the owner of what happens when the crew dies in the MVP. The Command & Simulation Layer (the kernel) captures the true simulation as an in-memory snapshot at a tick boundary and can restore one; everything that touches a disk is this system's: the container format and its header, the rolling ring of autosave **generations** (complete, self-verifying files), the rules for when an autosave is written and when it is deferred, the pre-check that judges a file before the kernel is ever touched, the orchestration of format and schema migrations, the per-campaign PROFILE store that a world restore never rolls back, and the **death stub** — on crew death, the game restarts from the last autosave.

**How the player meets it.** Almost never, by design. There is no save button, no slot list, no screenshot thumbnail, no "saving" ring, no sound and no death screen. The player meets four things: an autosave they do not perceive, written on a sim-time interval and at moments of consequence (and held back while a crewmate is down, so a save never records a trap); **Save & Quit** on the pause panel; **Continue** on the main menu; and, at the worst moment of a run, the death stub — a breath of black and then control again at the weld, the dock or the door where the last autosave stood, in a world that has not forgiven or forgotten anything. The cost of a death is the sim time since the last autosave and nothing else. Rare plain-spoken strings (a corrupt record, an incompatible build) are the only text this system ever sources; their final copy belongs to UX.

**What the game loses without it.** The index puts it plainly: a no-win-state game that breaks saves on every module change cannot be played long, and versioning is cheapest on day one. Concretely: the kernel's registry contract (every owner registers a versioned `persist` block) would have no host, so the kernel's save-equivalence guarantee (G4) could never be run against a real file; the Graph, the Controller and the Pipeline already point at a death stub that would otherwise be improvised separately by every MVP GDD; the PROFILE scope that carries Pillar 5's "never knowledge" across a world restore would have no store; and a single corrupt file would end a campaign in a game that has no win state to fall back on.

**What it deliberately is not.** It registers **zero state blocks and zero step hooks** (SL-17): the ring's bookkeeping must not roll back with the world it describes. No domain system calls it: the consumer rule stands — a system that wants state to persist registers a block with the kernel and never talks to Save/Load directly. It serialises **data models, never scene trees** (kernel H7, H10), and it never reads, derives or stores perception: a save is the true SIM state (Pillar 1), and it restores every persisted counter and seed exactly (kernel G4, D.10) while never varying by perception; whether perception draws replay identically after a restore is Sanity's decision, not Save/Load's (OQ-37, Pillar 4) *(Amended 2026-10-26 — CD-GDD-ALIGN)*. In the MVP the "never knowledge" half of Pillar 5 is **mechanism only**: the PROFILE store is specified and tested with a synthetic block, but no PROFILE block ships; the failure contract proper (what is kept, what is lost, the cryo-bay re-wake) is Failure Contract & Re-wake's at Vertical Slice A and replaces the death stub through the same restore path.

**MVP slice vs later.** One place to confirm what ships at MVP and what waits.

| Area | MVP | Later |
|---|---|---|
| Autosave triggers | Interval + `QUIT` + `NEW_GAME` initial generation | Event triggers as their owners land (SL-4, OQ-24) |
| Manual save | Save & Quit only | Pinned main-menu slot if playtest demands (OQ-18) |
| PROFILE store | Mechanism + synthetic block; zero shipped blocks | Knowledge Codex, scars (VS, Alpha) (SL-9) |
| Death | Death stub: restart from last autosave | Failure Contract & Re-wake (VS-A) (SL-7) |
| Co-op | Payload separable; `saveload.request_save` reserved | VS-B (SL-17) |
| Compression | None, `codec` 0 | VS-A review (SL-19) |
| API | No `save_now`; no `load(generation)` outside dev builds | Revisit with the Chart Table UX spec (SL-21) |

**Vocabulary (fixed).** The index and the kernel's Interactions row call the stub "reload last save". This GDD fixes the terms **"restart from the last autosave"**, **"death stub"** and **"generation"**; the older wording is a stale token routed to the consistency check (OQ-14). Ownership boundaries: block shapes and intra-block migrations are the owners'; snapshot mechanics, `state_hash`, restore ordering and `FAULTED` are the kernel's; the re-wake, the lights-out beat and the cryo-bay fiction are Failure Contract & Re-wake's; world construction is Layout's, Web Map's and Authoring's; the menu's look is the Chart Table's.

**Design-level only.** Which thread writes the file, which call sequence makes a rename safe on Windows, which hash library computes the file digest and whether payloads are compressed are implementation choices (→ ADR candidates: container format and trailer; atomic write, backup and recovery; hash choice; death-stub handshake). The engine reference holds no file-I/O, serialization or hashing module (its `modules/` folder covers animation, audio, input, navigation, networking, physics, rendering and ui), so every engine behaviour assumed here is tagged **[UNV]** and assigned to the concept's "save round-trip with version" spike. *(Framing: technical infrastructure with a thin but real player surface, selected as the recommended option for a `Core`/`Persistence` system under `autonomous` automation; logged.)*

## Player Fantasy

*Indirect — the player never experiences a save system; they experience what it guarantees: **trust without a safety net**.*

Persistence has no fantasy of its own. The promise comes from Pillar 5 — "losing the home-ship or a crewmate costs ship and body, never knowledge" — and from the user's binding rule for death: "This is like project zomboid, death is a bitch. It happens." The emotion this system must produce is that the player believes, without ever checking, that (1) the ship they welded is still there tomorrow exactly as they left it, (2) a death will cost them the minutes since the last autosave and nothing else, and (3) the game will never trap them in a moment they could not have seen coming. They never feel *saved*; they feel *committed*. Death and quitting never buy back more than the minutes since the last autosave. *(Amended 2026-10-26 — CD-GDD-ALIGN)*

> You never see the save. You see a ship you welded together last week, still breathing, still yours. You go one room deeper than is wise because if it kills you, it costs the minutes since the last autosave — not the hull, not the campaign. When you die, there is no screen: a breath of black, and you are standing where the world last stood still, in a world that has not forgiven or forgotten anything, including the room that killed you. The save never lies about the world and never rescues you from a mistake; it only refuses to trap you in a moment you could not have seen coming.

**The player never...** (each absence is a rule elsewhere in this document):

| The player never... | Because | What they feel instead |
|---|---|---|
| sees a save happen as an event | capture stays inside the kernel's snapshot budget (`SNAPSHOT_BUDGET_MS`, cited from the kernel, not restated) and the write is off the critical path (SL-10) | the world runs continuously; the ship "just exists" |
| chooses which save to load after a death | the death stub restores the newest verified generation (SL-7, SL-8) | death is consequence, not a menu |
| sees a death screen, a thumbnail or a sting | SL-7, SL-21; art bible 7.4 "No death screen" | the loss lands in the body of the game, not in an interface |
| buys back a mistake at will | no save-anywhere in the MVP (SL-4) | weight on every door and every weld |
| wakes into an unwinnable moment | the autosave gate defers while any seat is `DOWNED` or `DEAD`, while a Life-Support Graph warning window is still open, and (if Attention publishes it) outside a calm breach phase (SL-5) | death, when it comes, was *their* death |
| loses the campaign to one bad file | a rolling ring of `AUTOSAVE_GENERATIONS` 3 and a refuse-don't-corrupt pre-check (SL-3, SL-11) | the long tail is safe to invest in |

**Honesty about the MVP (Pillar 5, second half).** "Never knowledge" is **mechanism-only** in the MVP: the PROFILE store exists and is tested with a synthetic block (SL-9), but no PROFILE block ships, and the kernel states that the MVP death stub "is a placeholder, not Pillar 5's failure contract". In the MVP the only knowledge that survives a death lives in the player's head — which, because the world is deterministic (SL-23), is genuinely reusable: the scrubber that failed at minute four fails at minute four again. That is a real, small, honest version of Pillar 5, and this section does not claim more.

**The anchor moment (MVP, solo; numbers from SL-4, SL-5, SL-8 and the Controller's `DOWN_TO_DEATH_TICKS` 1800, at `TICK_RATE` 60).**

1. **T = 432 000** (two hours of sim time). An interval autosave is written (kind `AUTO`). The player sees and hears nothing: there is no per-write mark of any kind (UI-1 retired). If they open the pause panel it shows one SIM fact, the playtime of the newest record (2:00:00), and no countdown to the next write. *(Amended 2026-10-26 — CD-GDD-ALIGN)*
2. **T = 437 400.** The player completes their first weld; an event autosave is written (kind `EVENT`; 5 400 ticks since the last write, above `AUTOSAVE_MIN_GAP_TICKS` 1800). Again invisible.
3. The player pod-hops to a derelict and pushes one room too far. At **T = 446 400** their suit runs out of air mid-channel and `loco_state` becomes `DOWNED`. The next interval autosave falls due at T = 448 200 (437 400 + `AUTOSAVE_INTERVAL_TICKS` 10 800); the gate refuses it: the save system will not record the trap.
4. **T = 448 200** (`DOWN_TO_DEATH_TICKS` 1800 = 30 s after the fall): `DEAD`. On that same tick's boundary the death stub requests a restore of the newest verified generation (the one from T = 437 400). No beat, no lights-out, no screen.
5. The kernel enters `LOADING`; the Pipeline shows layer-1 black (`TRANSIT_HOLD`). The black hold is bounded by `LOADING_TARGET_MS` (1000, provisional); under 120 ms reads as a flash, over about 2 s reads as a crash (Game Feel).
6. The first `RUNNING` frame is a hard camera cut to the avatar standing at the weld they just finished, at the **pre-load zoom** (the Pipeline keeps `k_z` across an in-session load). The new section is welded on. The pod is docked. The derelict still holds the room that killed them, and the physical world's keyed draws (the Graph's, set by step and subject) play out the same way if they walk the same path.
7. **The cost, in one sentence:** 10 800 ticks — three minutes of play — and nothing else. No item loss, no stat penalty, no progression tax.

The anchor emotion is the half-second of black followed by recognition: *I am back at the weld. The ship is exactly as I made it. I know what is in that room now.* That is Zomboid's "death is a bitch" scaled to a three-minute window, with Pillar 5's kernel — the next attempt knows more — carried entirely by the player and the deterministic world. The typical loss is at most one `AUTOSAVE_INTERVAL_TICKS`; the derived ceiling (Formulas, `loss_ticks`) is 19 800 ticks (5.5 minutes), and 23 400 ticks after one conditional step-back. These two numbers are the tuning surface of how much death hurts: playtest knobs, not designs of consequence.

**Design test (Pillar 5, applied).** *A save that lets the player buy back a mistake vs. a save that only refuses to trap the player in a moment they could not perceive → the refusal.* Concept test: "safe farming vs. scarcity that pushes inward → scarcity." Every dispute below resolves the same way:

| Debate | Resolves to | Rule |
|---|---|---|
| Save-anywhere vs. autosave + Save & Quit | autosave + Save & Quit | SL-4 |
| Unconditional step-back after a second quick death vs. the newest verified generation | the newest verified; step back only when it fails verification, or when it is `RISKY` and the death came within `DEATH_LOOP_WINDOW_TICKS` of restoring it | SL-8 |
| Autosave while `DOWNED` vs. defer | defer | SL-5 |
| Let the player pick a generation after a death vs. the stub picks | the stub picks | SL-7, SL-8 |

Secondary test (Pillar 1 applied to persistence): *a restore that re-presents the world as it was vs. one that re-presents what the player saw → as it was.*

**Under hallucination (Pillar 4).** The save is the one artefact in the game that is pure ground truth. That deepens the horror if, and only if, the player can never use it as a free window onto truth. Pillar 4: "No lie is unfalsifiable: the truth is always available … and checking costs time, risk or trust." The rules (all enforced in SL-24 and its criteria):

| # | Rule | Why it deepens rather than breaks the fantasy |
|---|---|---|
| H1 | A save stores SIM truth only: never a `PERCEPTION_OUT` block, never a "what the player saw" field, no thumbnail. | The record of the ship is incorruptible — the dead derelict's flight recorder, not a photograph of your madness. |
| H2 *(Amended 2026-10-26 — CD-GDD-ALIGN)* | Every persisted counter and seed (the owners' `rng_derive` counters, kernel G4 and D.10) is restored exactly, and Save/Load never varies by perception. Whether a **perception** draw (sanity drift, the onset of a false system state) replays identically after a restore or is re-keyed is **Sanity & Hallucination Rules' decision**, not this GDD's (OQ-37); any re-keying must not be steerable by the player, so quit and death offer no reroll oracle. Physical-world determinism (the scrubber that fails at minute four) is unchanged: that is Pillar 1. | Binding perception replay into a Save/Load rule would turn hallucinations into fixed scripts the player can memorise across deaths, flattening dread (P2) and Discovery, which P4's "checking costs time, risk or trust" depends on. Save/Load states only what it owns: exact counters, no perception input, and no restore path that hands out a free reroll. |
| H3 | The sanity level is WORLD-scoped in the MVP and rolls back with the world. | Death is not a sanity cure: a player already low wakes still low; no "die to clear your head" exploit. |
| H4 | No clean frame: the first `RUNNING` frame after `LOADING` must already be presented through the Perception Filter for the restored sanity level; the black hold covers any rebuild. | One unfiltered frame would be a free oracle. This is a **routed request** to Perception Filter, Sanity and Pipeline (all three now designed; the ordering request stands, OQ-11), not Save/Load's to implement. |
| H5 *(Amended 2026-10-26 — CD-GDD-ALIGN)* | Every mark this system sources (the failed-write notice, the pause panel's newest-record line, its strings) is an inviolable channel: it never carries flesh tones or violet, never flickers with sanity and is never faked by a hallucination class. There is **no per-write mark** in the MVP, so nothing on screen tells the player when the game saves or defers a save. | One more thing the player can trust absolutely. A per-write glyph was retired (UI-1): held by the gate, its absence would have signalled that danger was building, a hidden Attention meter against P3. |
| H6 | Save/Load never reads, derives or varies by perception: no sanity-dependent autosave timing, no hallucinated save list. | A lie that reached the save menu would be unfalsifiable inside the session; Pillar 4 requires at least one channel of truth to remain. |

**Reference techniques — adopt / reject.**

| Reference | Technique | Decision | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| Project Zomboid | Continuous autosave; Save & Quit only; no manual slots | **Adopt** | The user's stated touchstone (SL-4) |
| Project Zomboid | Permadeath of the character while the world persists; a new character | **Reject for the MVP** | The user rule is restart from the last autosave; the new-character form is Failure Contract & Re-wake's (VS-A) |
| Darkest Dungeon and roguelikes | No manual save; autosave on meaningful events | **Adopt** (event triggers, names provisional) | Weight on every decision |
| Roguelike "suspend save" (consumed on resume) | A quit-save deleted when resumed | **Reject** | A death after a Continue would lose more than the last autosave; contradicts SL-8 |
| Souls-likes | Authored bonfire-style rest points; a "YOU DIED" screen; a corpse run | **Reject** | Authored spatial rest points violate "no authored story"; a death screen is forbidden (art bible 7.4); a corpse run is a progression penalty |
| Most RPGs and immersive sims | Named manual save-anywhere; quicksave | **Reject** (MVP) | A reload becomes a free hallucination oracle and the cost of death vanishes; the one fallback is a pinned main-menu-only slot (OQ-18) |
| Rogue-lite meta progression | Unlocks earned by dying | **Reject** | Anti-pillar "nothing levels up"; would invert Pillar 5 (death becomes profitable) |
| Pandorum, *Beyond the Aquila Rift* | Waking into wrongness | **Route to Failure Contract & Re-wake** | The re-wake fiction is VS-A; using it in the stub would break SL-7 |
| Satisfactory and factory games | Saves as long-lived investment; patch-safe migrations | **Adopt the principle** | "Versioning is cheapest on day one"; golden saves (SL-14) |
| The save-thumbnail convention | A screenshot in the load list | **Reject** | It would capture the Perception Filter's lie (Pillar 1; SL-21) |

**Anti-pillar guard rails.**

| Anti-pillar / pillar | Guard rail for Save/Load |
|---|---|
| Not a game you win | No "final save", no completion flag, no ending state in the header; saves never close a campaign; `SaveInfo` carries no percentage and no progress meter |
| Not a freeform builder | No ship-blueprint export or import, no "save ship design"; the composite ship is Layout + Graph + Body data only |
| Not intentional PvP | Host-owned saves only; no guest-authoritative block; a friendly-fire death (VS-B) is Failure Contract's, never a save that records blame |
| Not an authored story | No named chapters, acts or authored save locations; generation labels come from SIM facts (the depth label is provisional from Web Map) |
| Not a combat game | The death stub attaches no penalty, no visible deaths counter and no difficulty tier; the cost is the autosave interval, nothing else |
| P2 Dread Before Chaos | The autosave gate is a Pillar 2 obligation, not a convenience: a save inside a `DOWNED` state, inside an open Graph warning window, or (if Attention publishes it) in a non-calm breach phase is deferred |
| P3 The Web Watches | No audio cue for saving (any sound is attributable by the web) unless Audio justifies one; Attention restores exactly; no meter is stored for display |

**Tone and fixed terms.** The few strings this system sources are plain, factual and low-affect, in a ship-log register: never apologetic, never cute, no exclamation marks, no emoji, no colour from the sane/insane palette. Placeholder examples (final copy is UX's and the narrative director's): "Newest record unreadable. Continuing from the one before it." / "This save was made by an incompatible build." Fixed terms: "restart from the last autosave", "death stub", "generation"; player-facing labels "Continue", "Save & Quit", "New campaign". Not used affirmatively anywhere in this document or in a string it sources: "reload last save", "respawn", "save point", "game over", "you died", "progress saved", "quicksave", and "slot" for the (invisible) ring.

*(Framing: indirect — "what it lets you stop worrying about"; selected over the direct "the ship remembers you" framing, which would put infrastructure in the player's face, and over the diegetic cryo-bay framing, which belongs to Failure Contract & Re-wake; logged.)*

## Detailed Design

### Core Rules

**Conventions.** Rules are numbered `SL-n` with names; decisions and routed amendments keep their brief ids (D1–D18, S-1, K-1, K-2, K-3, K-4). Kernel rules are cited by id (CR-8, CR-15, CR-18, H1–H10, D.11, D.12), as are the Graph's (LS-21), the Controller's (PC-17) and the Pipeline's (C5, `TRANSIT_HOLD`). **(provisional — X undesigned/in flight)** marks a name or contract owned by a system whose GDD does not exist or is still being written; it is repeated at every mention, and none of those names is a contract until its owner confirms it. **[UNV]** marks an engine behaviour not verified against Godot 4.7.2 (the engine reference has no file-I/O, serialization or hashing module); every [UNV] is assigned to the concept's "save round-trip with version" spike. "→ ADR candidate" marks an implementation choice deliberately left open. Registry names are used verbatim and never restated differently: `TICK_RATE` 60, `DOWN_TO_DEATH_TICKS` 1800 (Controller), `HOST_PEER_ID` 1, `W_MIN_WARNING_S` 10 (Graph), and the formulas `state_hash` (D.12) and `rng_derive` (D.10). **Kernel GDD values (Tuning Knobs / D.11), not registry entries — kernel registry candidates:** `SNAPSHOT_BUDGET_MS` 16.7 (K:515), `MAX_BLOCKS` 128 (K:722) and the `snapshot_bytes` formula (K:501); they are cited by name and never restated with another value. All durations are integer ticks read from `kernel.clock.T`; wall clock is metadata only (SL-23).

**SL-1 — Container and header.** One file holds one **generation** (or, for the PROFILE store, one profile image — SL-9). Layout is the contract:

| Offset | Size | Field | Rule |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | 8 | magic | A fixed 7-byte ASCII prefix followed by one byte, the container-layout id (1 for `FORMAT_VERSION` 1). The layout-id byte names the header/payload encoding only; it never locates the trailer (format law below) |
| 8 | 4 | `header_len` | Unsigned, little-endian; `0 < header_len ≤ MAX_HEADER_BYTES` and `12 + header_len + 32 ≤ file size`, checked **before any allocation** (a corrupt 4 GiB length is never allocated) |
| 12 | `header_len` | JSON header | UTF-8 object (fields below) |
| 12 + `header_len` | rest − 32 | payload region | The block payloads, concatenated in block-table order (SL-2) |
| end − 32 | 32 | trailer | SHA-256 of every preceding byte (magic through the last payload). It lives outside the header because a header cannot contain a hash of itself; a missing or short trailer is truncation, detected for free |

**Format law (every layout, forever).** For every file bearing the 7-byte magic prefix, in every container layout, the last 32 bytes are SHA-256 over all preceding bytes, `[0, size − 32)`. The layout-id byte names the header/payload encoding only. A reader can therefore verify integrity before it judges the layout id, so one flipped bit in the layout-id byte is reported as corruption (and falls back, SL-11) rather than as "made by a newer build" (which must not).

*(Orchestrator ruling: the trailer is additive to the layout in R1 — the file must be self-verifying (R3) and a header cannot hash itself. Payload region and file wrapper stay separable so the same payload can be sent to a guest at VS-B session start (CR-18) without the wrapper.)*

Header fields (closed list for format 1; unknown extra keys are ignored when `min_reader_version` is satisfied, missing required keys refuse):

| Field | Type | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| `format_version` | int ≥ 1 | Container version; `FORMAT_VERSION` 1 today |
| `min_reader_version` | int | Oldest reader that may open the file. In the MVP it equals `format_version` on every write (an older build can never read a newer file's blocks); the field exists so a later additive header change can lower it deliberately |
| `file_kind` | enum | `WORLD` (a generation) or `PROFILE` (SL-9) |
| `game_version`, `build_id` | string | File metadata written by the service; never read by the sim (H9) |
| `catalogue_version` | int | Item/module content epoch — the Item & Module Database's name (designed; IDB-26 `meta.json` key, IDB-27, its Save/Load row; history OQ-13). `catalogue_hash` (hex) is diagnostic only |
| `hash_algo` | int | Names the 64-bit hash the kernel used for `state_hash` and per-block hashes at capture (0 xxHash64, 1 FNV-1a 64, 2 SHA-256-truncated, …), so the kernel's hash ADR can change without a `format_version` bump (SL-16) |
| `codec` | int | 0 = none; 1 reserved (zstd). No compression in the MVP (SL-19) |
| `tick_rate` | int | Must equal the kernel's `TICK_RATE`; a save is bound to the rate that wrote it |
| `T`, `world_seed` | hex u64 | Copied from `kernel.clock`; pre-check asserts they equal the block's values |
| `state_hash` | hex u64 | Kernel `state_hash(PERSIST, T)` of the WORLD blocks at capture (D.12, cited); absent in `PROFILE` files |
| `campaign_id` | hex, 32 chars | 128-bit random; must equal the directory name |
| `generation` | int, < 2^53 | Strictly increasing per campaign; **the ordering truth** (SL-3) |
| `save_kind`, `trigger` | enum, string | `AUTO` / `EVENT` / `QUIT`; `trigger` names the event (names provisional until the registry merge: `POD_ARRIVED`; `ASSEMBLY_WELD` / `ASSEMBLY_CUT` are registered, SL-4); the initial generation is `EVENT` with trigger `NEW_GAME` |
| `risky` | bool | Written while a soft gate condition was closed, including a Save & Quit that bypassed one (SL-5, SL-6) |
| `created_unix` | int | Wall clock, metadata only (SL-23) |
| `seats` | int[] | PlayerIds present, for validation only (SL-20) |
| `crew_count`, `depth_label` | int, string | `SaveInfo` inputs computed from SIM blocks at capture; `depth_label` (provisional — derived from the `ring` of the pod's node in `web.view`; Web Map & Transit publishes a ring, not a depth-label field) |
| `blocks[]` | records | `{block_id, schema_version, persist_scope, offset, length, payload_sha256 (64 hex), block_state_hash (hex, optional)}` per block; entries are contiguous, ascending, non-overlapping and sum exactly to the payload region |

**64-bit rule (S-1).** Every header value that can exceed 2^53 (`T`, `world_seed`, `state_hash`, per-block hashes, `campaign_id`) is an exact-width lowercase hex string (16 characters for 64-bit, 32 for 128-bit), never a JSON number; hex decoding is exact-width — a wrong length, uppercase or non-hex character is a refusal — and the contract is "8 bytes in, 8 bytes out" with no general string-to-int parse. Header numbers that stay numbers (`format_version`, `schema_version`, `generation`, lengths, offsets, counts, `hash_algo`, `codec`, `tick_rate`) arrive as floats from the parser (the Graph GDD's technical-director note, LS-21) and must be integral and below 2^53 before conversion. A `world_seed` ≥ 2^63 would read as a negative signed integer in GDScript [UNV]; it is stored as the hex of its 8 bytes. **File bytes are not canonical:** dictionary iteration is insertion-ordered (kernel D.12), so two saves of identical state can differ byte for byte. State equality is `state_hash` (or the owners' canonical bytes), never file bytes; the file SHA-256 guards *these bytes* against accidental damage; golden saves are immutable artefacts regenerated only deliberately.

**SL-2 — Interchange form and trust model (S-1, K OQ 7, D1).** Each block payload is the owner's `snapshot()` dictionary encoded with `var_to_bytes(dict, allow_objects = false)` [UNV], or the owner's own canonical byte form where it publishes one (the Graph's `canonical_bytes()`, LS-21), as raw bytes inside the container. JSON appears **only** in the header. Decoding uses the object-disallowed decode only; the object-allowing decode, `Resource` and `.tres` paths are forbidden outright (a script path can ride inside a resource; the Graph states "never a `Resource` for saves"). This is the answer to the kernel's Open Question 7. S-1 is carried verbatim: **64-bit integers cannot round-trip JSON text** (Godot's JSON parses numbers as float — the technical director's sourced note in LS-21), so no hashed, 64-bit or packed data ever travels as a JSON number; a payload that stored a packed array as a JSON number list is refused by the Graph's decoder (`GRAPH_RESTORE_BAD_TAG`). A dev-only `dump-save` command renders any block as JSON with 64-bit values as strings; it is diagnostics, never a load path. After decoding, a bounded walk (`MAX_DECODE_DEPTH`, `MAX_DECODE_NODES`; a packed array counts as one node) checks types before any `request_restore`, because `allow_objects = false` stops object instantiation but does not by itself bound allocation or nesting [UNV]: allowed are null, bool, int, float, String, Array, Dictionary and the packed arrays (the H7 set, kernel CR-14); `Vector2i`, float vectors, `Color`, `Rect2`, `Object`, `Callable`, `Signal`, `RID`, `NodePath` and `StringName` are forbidden (kernel H7, K:125, enumerates no types: the allowed set is this GDD's reading of it, and K:839's JSON round trip cannot carry a `Vector2i`, which stringifies, so allowing it would make format 1 depend on K-3 landing); integer vectors travel as two ints or a packed int array (a shape note for the Body author — Ship Body & Interior Physics, designed, whose tables are integer-only (BDY-5); revisited only if K-3 changes K:839, OQ-9); dictionary keys are `String` or `int`; `snapshot()` uses untyped containers until the spike resolves typed-container encoding [UNV]. The kernel text that calls the interchange form "JSON-safe primitives" (CR-8) and its acceptance criterion that round-trips through JSON conflict with S-1 and with 64-bit fields; this GDD does not claim they are already amended — routed amendment **K-3** (OQ-5): the interchange test becomes a `var_to_bytes` round-trip plus a separate JSON-dump assertion that 64-bit values arrive as strings.

**SL-3 — Generation ring.** `AUTOSAVE_GENERATIONS` 3 files (safe 2–5), each complete and self-verifying, at ring positions `gen_0.sav … gen_{N−1}.sav`. **Order is the header `generation` number — never the file name, the modification time or `T`** (after a step-back `T` rolls back while generation numbers keep rising; wall clock is untrusted). A new generation's number is `1 + max(generation seen in the ring, in `quarantine/`, among `.demoted` files and in the manifest)`. It is written into the first ring position that holds no non-demoted verified generation (missing, `.demoted` or quarantined); otherwise into the position of the **oldest** generation; the newest generation's file is never renamed or touched, so a window with no newest file cannot exist. *(Orchestrator ruling: R11 states the old newest is renamed to `.bak` during a swap; the three specialists converged on writing into the oldest position, where `.bak` holds the replaced oldest generation. R11's guarantee — never delete a generation before its successor verified — is kept and strengthened; the sequence is R11's "ADR candidate" and is stated in SL-10.)* To the player the ring is one logical autosave ("Continue"); it exists for corruption fallback and the RISKY rule (SL-8), not for choice, so the MVP API offers **no `load(generation)`** (a dev-build-only call may exist). `campaign.json` is a **hint, never truth**: a rebuildable cache of the ring (order, last write status, `suspect` counters); if missing or corrupt it is rebuilt by scanning generation headers and causes no refusal; the ring's truth is the set of generation headers and file names.

**SL-4 — Autosave triggers and manual save (D3).** Triggers: **interval** (`AUTOSAVE_INTERVAL_TICKS` 10 800 = 3 min of sim time, safe 3 600–36 000, measured from `kernel.clock.T`), **events** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass)* (pod-hop arrival = Web Map & Transit's `POD_ARRIVED{tick, leg_epoch, at}` (`web_events`, provisional names until merge); weld or cut completion = Assembly's shape change, polled through the pure read `last_shape_change_T()` (MSA-33; event names `ASSEMBLY_WELD` / `ASSEMBLY_CUT` registered 2026-10-27, OQ-24); section extraction is Assembly's detach, served by the same `last_shape_change_T()`; a module cut is served by Interaction's pure read `last_salvage_T()` (registry `last_salvage_T`; IA-25; int64 tick or NONE; Boarding & Salvage BS-21), polled under SL-5's gate and `AUTOSAVE_MIN_GAP_TICKS`; dock completion = Web Map & Transit's `BERTHED{tick, derelict_id}` (`web_events`, provisional until merge; `UNBERTHED` is not a trigger); an event trigger activates only when its owner publishes a notification or pure read Save/Load may consume), **Save & Quit** (SL-6) and the **initial generation** after `new_game` (SL-18). Events coalesce (kind only, never queued). A minimum gap `AUTOSAVE_MIN_GAP_TICKS` 1 800 separates any two interval/event generations; QUIT ignores it. An interval save is never due while the kernel is `PAUSED` (`T` cannot advance). The MVP baseline is interval + QUIT + initial only; events join as their owners land. **Manual save is none in the MVP except Save & Quit** from the pause panel: save-anywhere is rejected because it makes every reload a free hallucination oracle (Pillar 4) and erases the cost of death (the user rule). A pinned main-menu-only manual slot is the single playtest fallback and is recorded in OQ-18, never an in-session save. The Chart Table's API (provisional) therefore has no `save_now()` in the MVP; if one ever appears it is marked "(provisional, reserved)" at every mention. The due-computation is Formula `autosave_due` (Formulas).

**SL-5 — Autosave gate.** A due autosave waits for the gate, re-evaluated every `AUTOSAVE_RETRY_TICKS` 300 from the tick it first became due.

| Class | Condition | Source | Overridden by the cap? |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Hard** | Kernel state is `RUNNING` or `PAUSED` | kernel | Never |
| **Hard** | No restore pending; no write in flight | Save/Load | Never |
| **Hard** | No bound seat has `controller.state.loco_state` of `DOWNED` or `DEAD` | Controller (`DOWN_TO_DEATH_TICKS` 1800, cited) | Never |
| **Soft** *(Amended 2026-10-26 — CD-GDD-ALIGN)* | **(a) Graph tell — required at MVP.** Any loaded descriptor of subject kind NODE, EDGE or SEAM (the kinds that carry `warn_since_step` and that the Graph's fairness gate governs; COMPARTMENT and OPENING descriptors such as ATMO_COLD or SEAL_STRAIN open no window, report `warn_elapsed` 0 and never count) with `cause` ENDOGENOUS, `sev` ≥ 1 and `warn_elapsed` < `W_MIN_STEPS` (the Graph's fairness gate is still closed, D.11); the whole open window defers. A WORN descriptor (mode 10) counts: it is the Graph's first warning before an endogenous FAILED, and it is read from the Graph's SIM descriptor, never from the Perception Filter's corruptible module-body channel | Life-Support Graph `read_view.failures()` (LS-19; descriptor fields of LS-14) — **designed and locked**; not provisional, no `GATE_INPUT_ABSENT` | After `AUTOSAVE_MAX_DEFER_TICKS` 7 200 |
| **Soft** *(Amended 2026-10-26 — CD-GDD-ALIGN)* | **(b) Attention tell and breach phase — `attention.view` (designed); optional (absent = open).** An active Attention tell with fewer than `W_MIN_WARNING_S × TICK_RATE` ticks remaining (`gate_tell_mode` FAIR; STRICT defers for the whole tell), or a breach phase that is not calm | Attention SIM read view `attention.view` (designed — Attention System; `tell_active`, `tell_remaining` 0 … `TELL_MAX_TICKS` 1 800, `breach_phase` {CALM, BREACH, SUBSIDING}; `breach_phase` is Save/Load-only (a TELL reads CALM); `tell_remaining` is read by Save/Load, SIM readers and the DIAGNOSTIC Debug Overlay only); an absent view still counts as open, logged once per process as `GATE_INPUT_ABSENT` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass)* | After the cap |
| **Soft** | A bound seat is in a state listed in `GATE_SOFT_STATES` (default `CHANNELLING`, `STAGGERED`, `IN_POD`) | Controller | After the cap |

When a due save has waited `AUTOSAVE_MAX_DEFER_TICKS` and only **soft** conditions are closed, it is written anyway and its header is tagged `RISKY`. A hard condition is never overridden: a `DOWNED` or `DEAD` world is never written by an autosave or by a Save & Quit (SL-6). *(Orchestrator ruling: R4 says the cap "writes anyway"; read literally that would record the very trap the gate exists to avoid, and would write a state the death stub is about to discard. Three specialists proposed three splits; the ruling is that DOWNED and DEAD are hard, the cap overrides soft conditions only, and the derived loss ceiling in Formulas holds. `GATE_SOFT_STATES` is a systems-designer refinement: a restore drops channel progress and turns `STAGGERED` into `IDLE` (PC-17), and a mid-transit state is Web Map's to restore.)* **Consequence stated plainly:** in the solo MVP a downed seat dies after `DOWN_TO_DEATH_TICKS` 1 800, well before the cap, so a `RISKY` generation is reachable only through chained Graph warning windows held past the cap, the provisional Attention view, or a transit/channel state held past the cap or at a Save & Quit (SL-6); none of them involves a downed seat. The conditional step-back of SL-8 is therefore near-dead code in the MVP — cheap to test with an injected gate input, easy to let rot. The gate also exists to avoid a fairness regression the save would otherwise cause: a generation captured mid-tell restores with only the remainder of the warning (the Graph persists `warn_since_step`, so the elapsed part is kept and the player wakes into a fault with less than `W_MIN_WARNING_S` of it left to perceive), so the Graph's guarantee (`W_MIN_WARNING_S`, endogenous transitions only) would be silently shortened by the save system and a death-stub restore would put the player into a fault with a shortened warning. *(Amended 2026-10-26 — CD-GDD-ALIGN)* **The Graph half (a) prevents this at the MVP.** It is built from the designed Graph read view, not from the undesigned Attention system: `failures()` publishes `cause`, `sev` and `warn_elapsed` per descriptor, and `W_MIN_STEPS` (= `W_MIN_WARNING_S × TICK_RATE / GRAPH_STEP_TICKS`, the Graph's, cited and never restated) says when its gate opens, so a due save waits at most one window (at most `W_MIN_WARNING_S × TICK_RATE` ticks, 600 at the defaults) per descriptor. The Attention half (b) reads the designed `attention.view` but stays optional: without the Attention view the gate is Graph + controller + kernel conditions and logs `GATE_INPUT_ABSENT` once per process. `gate_tell_mode` governs half (b) only; for half (a) FAIR and STRICT collapse into one behaviour (defer for the whole open window), because the window *is* the guarantee. Windows may chain (one closes as another opens); the cap bounds the chain, so `AUTOSAVE_MAX_DEFER_TICKS` and `loss_ticks` are unchanged. **Dormant sections:** a dormant section freezes its `warn_since_step` values in place (Graph dormant-section rule), so a frozen mid-window descriptor never closes; LS-19 names no loaded-section predicate, so the filter "loaded descriptors only" is a routed one-line request to the Graph owner (OQ-38). Until it is answered, a frozen dormant window can defer a save to the cap and tag it `RISKY`, an accepted rare exposure (it needs a derelict unloaded within one window of a new tell). The gate is Formula `autosave_gate` (Formulas); its inputs are injectable for tests.

**SL-6 — Save & Quit.** From the pause panel: `request_snapshot` while `PAUSED` (`PAUSED → SAVING → PAUSED`, `T` unchanged), write a generation of kind `QUIT`, and `request_shutdown` only after the write verifies (`WRITTEN`). The call is host-local (CR-15) and is never named a command in the MVP. Quitting is **never deferred** and bypasses the **soft** gate conditions only: if a soft condition was closed at request time the generation is written immediately and tagged `RISKY` (alternatives rejected: refusing to quit traps the player; quitting without writing silently throws away up to one interval). It does **not** bypass the seat-state hard condition: **a `QUIT` requested while any bound seat is `DOWNED` or `DEAD` writes nothing.** `save_and_quit` returns `SL_QUIT_NO_WRITE_DOWNED`, `save_status` is unchanged, and shutdown proceeds after the hold-to-confirm "quit without saving" (art bible HR-14; the look is the Chart Table's); Continue then restores the newest verified generation, which is exactly what the death stub would have done within `DOWN_TO_DEATH_TICKS`. The invariant this buys — **no file on disk ever holds a `DOWNED` or `DEAD` seat** — is testable (Acceptance Criteria). A `QUIT` while a seat is `DEAD` is unreachable in the solo MVP (the stub fires on the `DEAD` tick); the `DEAD` clause is for multi-seat futures. The other hard conditions also apply: the kernel-state condition, and an in-flight write (the `QUIT` waits for it); in `FAULTED` there is no write path (SL-22). If the write fails, the quit is held, `save_status` reads `FAILED`, and the player may retry or quit without saving by hold-to-confirm. The write waits at most `SAVE_QUIT_TIMEOUT_MS`; on timeout the pause panel stays and `save_status` reads `FAILED`. A window-close request while `RUNNING` calls `save_and_quit()` from `RUNNING` (`RUNNING → SAVING → RUNNING`, K:164) and then `request_shutdown`, under the same rules (it saves the *current* state, so it is no rollback and no oracle); an OS kill falls back to the newest generation. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, ASK-CT-11, EC-CT-38)* A window-close request while `PAUSED` under the open Chart Table (SOLO) runs `save_and_quit()` from `PAUSED` (`PAUSED → SAVING → PAUSED`) under the same rules and then `request_shutdown`; the Chart Table calls nothing and is never a second save path. On a boot-flow screen (main menu, UI-8, UI-9: no SIM state exists and the kernel is neither `RUNNING` nor `PAUSED`) Quit and window close write nothing: the process ends through boot code with no kernel call, because the kernel lists `request_shutdown` only from `RUNNING`, `PAUSED` and `FAULTED` (whether a boot-state shutdown call is wanted is the kernel owner's, ASK-CT-11); from `FAULTED`, Quit keeps `request_shutdown` and writes nothing (SL-22). If the state hash equals the newest generation's (same `T`, nothing changed) the write is skipped and shutdown proceeds. *(Orchestrator ruling: R4/R24 are silent on whether the gate applies to QUIT; three specialists agreed it must not block a deliberate quit, and proposed that it bypass every condition; the stage-5 checkpoint made the seat-state hard condition absolute, because a `DOWNED` generation would have broken the Player Fantasy's promise for up to `DOWN_TO_DEATH_TICKS`. Accepted exposure, from the creative director: Save & Quit before a risky door, quit, relaunch and Continue is a manual save in disguise; Zomboid parity — the friction of quit-and-relaunch is the cost, determinism means it buys a retry of the player's inputs and, in a deterministic physical world, a look at one visible unknown for at most the minutes since the last autosave (Discovery is aesthetic priority 1, so this is measured, not waved through: triggers M1 to M3 in Edge Cases and AC-SL-94). The remedy if playtest shows routine quit-scumming is first the interval, then Failure Contract & Re-wake, never deleting the QUIT generation, which would contradict SL-8; OQ-25.)*

**SL-7 — Death stub (the MVP answer to the Graph's Open Question 1).** "Crew death" in the MVP = every bound seat `DEAD` (single player: PlayerId 1); multi-seat semantics are Failure Contract's and Co-op's. Save/Load learns of `DEAD` through the observation channel of SL-13 and, **in the same tick boundary**, waits for any in-flight write to reach `WRITTEN` or `FAILED` (the 30 s `DOWNED` floor makes the case nearly unreachable, and the wait is bounded by the writer's failure path for the rename step (`RENAME_RETRY_COUNT` × `RENAME_RETRY_DELAY_MS`); a write stuck in flush or re-read is unbounded in principle (F-5), is reachable only if a write that started inside the 1 800-tick `DOWNED` window has not settled, and is measured by the spike (AC-SL-30, AC-SL-88)), only then bumps its session epoch (SL-10), so the bump cannot abort the baseline write, selects a generation (SL-8), pre-checks and stages it (SL-11) and only then calls `request_restore(payload)`, which the kernel honours at that tick's boundary services (the Controller's `DEAD` row and its Edge Case "`DEAD` entered during a camera cut" both state this hand-off). Sequence: `DEAD` → (same boundary: epoch++, in-flight write settled, generation chosen by SL-8, pre-checked and staged) → `request_restore(payload)` → kernel `LOADING` (the command queue is flushed, every command `Dropped(RELOAD)`) → the Pipeline shows layer-1 black (`TRANSIT_HOLD`, which the Pipeline lists "kernel `LOADING` entered mid-session (death stub, load)" as an entry condition) → the kernel's restore and the verification window (SL-12) → first `RUNNING` frame is a camera `CUT` at the **pre-load `k_z`**. **No beat, no lights-out, no death screen:** the art bible's 2.10 sequence (sources going dark one by one, then the cryo-bay lamp) is written for the cryo re-wake and belongs to Failure Contract & Re-wake (VS-A); the Pipeline's traceability row that says the sequence is "built once in the death stub" is a routed text fix (OQ-14). *(Orchestrator ruling: the brief proposed that the death stub counts as a re-wake for camera purposes and resets zoom to `k_auto`; the locked Pipeline text classes the death stub with in-session loads and says zoom resets "only on re-wake", so the stub **keeps `k_z`**. If Failure Contract wants the reset at VS-A, that is the Pipeline owner's call then.)* `DEAD` during a camera cut is **handled**, not impossible: the request is made at the tick's boundary, the Presenter holds the down sheet's last frame and no second cut is issued for the death (only `DEAD` during a *cut channel* is impossible, since `DOWNED` precedes `DEAD` by 1 800 ticks and clears the channel). If no candidate passes the load pipeline, the outcome is `SL_LOAD_NO_LOADABLE_SAVE`: Save/Load publishes `SL_LOAD_NO_LOADABLE_SAVE` and `save_status`; the Chart Table shows it as a boot-flow screen — the kernel is not `RUNNING` and no pause is requested (CT-25) — with New campaign or Quit (the message text is UX's; how a running session whose death stub found nothing is torn down to the boot flow is OQ-42) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass)*; the dead world is never autosaved. The stub attaches no penalty beyond the lost interval. Failure Contract & Re-wake replaces this rule at VS-A through the same `request_restore` path and PROFILE store (SL-9). *(Orchestrator ruling: the order inside the `DEAD` boundary is wait for the in-flight write, then bump the epoch, then select and stage — an epoch bump before the wait is exactly what aborts the write the wait exists to protect; the stage-5 checkpoint listed the bump first but states this purpose, so the purpose wins.)*

**SL-8 — Generation selection and demotion.** *Default:* restore the **newest** generation that is not demoted and passes the pre-check (SL-11) — the user's rule, "restart from the last autosave", verbatim. Save/Load restores an *older* generation only when (a) the newest fails the pre-check (fall back newest-first, within `LOAD_MAX_ATTEMPTS`; a reproduction mismatch is never a fallback reason, SL-12), or (b) the **last restored** generation `g_r` is tagged `RISKY`, no newer non-demoted verified generation exists, **and** `T_downed − T(g_r) ≤ DEATH_LOOP_WINDOW_TICKS` 3 600, where `T_downed` is the start of the `DOWNED` episode that ended in `DEAD` (observed through the `loco_state` read SL-13 already grants) and a restore returns `T` to `T(g_r)`. The window bounds the player's *acting* time: measured at death it would shrink by `DOWN_TO_DEATH_TICKS`, whose owner range is 600–3 600, so at the Controller's upper bound the rule could never fire. The "no newer non-demoted verified generation" precondition is the loop test: if one was written after the restore the gate opened and the player got out, so there is no loop and the default already picks the newer file. In case (b) `g_r` is **demoted** (renamed `gen_k.demoted`, file kept, never read as a candidate) and selection proceeds as the default; demotion is what keeps (b) coherent — without it the next death would pick the same RISKY newest again and the ring would oscillate. If nothing non-demoted verifies, a second pass over demoted generations (newest first) runs before `SL_LOAD_NO_LOADABLE_SAVE`; if the only candidate is `RISKY`, restore it and log `SL_STEPBACK_NONE_AVAILABLE`. *(Orchestrator ruling: the brief's unconditional "second death within the window steps back two generations" is not adopted — it reinterprets the user's binding rule. "Step back on a repeated death from a non-RISKY save" is the one open question flagged for the user (OQ-1). Demotion by rename is a systems-designer addition: it extends the file layout, survives the loss of the manifest, and extends R18; a late-landing write from an abandoned timeline is handled separately in SL-10.)* The selection is Formula `generation_select` (Formulas), which takes `T_downed`.

**SL-9 — PROFILE store (K OQ 13, D5).** A per-campaign `profile.sav` beside the generation files, in the same container (`file_kind = PROFILE`; no `state_hash`; the trailer and per-block hashes still apply). The kernel produces one snapshot; Save/Load **splits it by `persist_scope` at write time**: WORLD blocks to the generation, PROFILE blocks to `profile.sav`. An in-session restore (the death stub, a load of the already-open campaign) passes **only WORLD blocks** to `request_restore`, so PROFILE blocks are never rolled back (CR-18); a cold open (process start, Continue) loads each scope whole from its own file, because partial restore within a scope is unsupported. `profile.sav` is rewritten, by the same atomic procedure (SL-10), when a capture's PROFILE per-block hashes differ from those last written (no subscription needed), on QUIT, and on an owner change notification (mechanism provisional, as SL-13), which triggers a profile-only capture with no new generation; a floor `PROFILE_MIN_WRITE_GAP_TICKS` bounds the write rate. `profile.sav` keeps a permanent `.bak` (Pillar 5 makes it more precious than any one world generation). A new campaign starts with an empty profile; the death stub and the VS re-wake ("new crew, same world") leave PROFILE untouched, so knowledge survives death in memory by construction and the disk file matters across process restarts. **Zero PROFILE blocks ship in the MVP; the mechanism is specified and tested now** with a synthetic PROFILE block (the kernel's acceptance criterion for `PROFILE` blocks is the model). What is kept at re-wake is Failure Contract's policy. `scope` (SHARED / PER_PLAYER) and `persist_scope` (WORLD / PROFILE) are orthogonal axes: `suit.state` and the Sanity blocks `sanity.state` and `sanity.ledger` are `PER_PLAYER` × `WORLD` in the MVP and **roll back with the death stub**; only knowledge, lore and scars are PROFILE, and none ships. The routed thread's wording "PER_PLAYER / PROFILE" conflated the two axes and is corrected here; the Sanity row in the kernel's Interactions table gives the level `persist` but no `persist_scope`; the designed Sanity GDD and the registry now state `persist_scope WORLD` for both Sanity blocks (OQ-21 closed), and the boot assertion still catches any such gap mechanically (SL-15).

**SL-10 — Write procedure and session epoch (D9; behaviour only → ADR candidate "atomic write, backup and thread model").** Capture is synchronous inside the kernel's `SAVING` state (the kernel owns capture cost against `SNAPSHOT_BUDGET_MS`, cited; Save/Load adds, on the main thread, only the encoding of the captured plain data and a small hand-off — Formula `write_budget`, bounded by `SAVE_ENCODE_BUDGET_MS` and `WRITE_HANDOFF_MAX_FRACTION`, both logging thresholds); the payload is an immutable byte value handed to a **worker** that never touches the kernel or any block [UNV: worker threads with file access]. The worker writes `gen_k.tmp`, flushes, re-reads and re-hashes it, renames the position's existing file (the oldest generation, if any) to `.bak`, renames `.tmp` into place, verifies again, deletes `.bak` and rewrites `campaign.json` (state table T-4 in States). **Rules:** every `store_*` return is checked (they return `bool` since Godot 4.4 — sourced from the engine reference); any false return, open error, hash mismatch or rename failure leaves every previous generation exactly as it was, reports to diagnostics and to `save_status`, and the next trigger retries with bounded exponential backoff (`AUTOSAVE_FAIL_BACKOFF_MAX_TICKS`; `AUTOSAVE_FAIL_ESCALATE_COUNT` consecutive failures raise the HUD notice). Never overwrite in place; never delete a generation before its successor verified; **never write while the kernel is `FAULTED`**. The design assumes only that a rename of a closed file to an *absent* name is all-or-nothing; atomic replace on Windows, sharing violations from scanners and indexers and OneDrive-redirected folders are [UNV] and are spike items. "Verified" means readable and hash-correct through the operating system's cache — it does not prove the bytes reached the media (whether a flush implies an fsync is [UNV]); the ring and the recovery scan (T-6) are the answer to power loss, not the verify step. **Session epoch:** every `request_restore` increments an in-memory epoch carried by each write job; a job that observes an advanced epoch before its first rename aborts and removes its `.tmp`; a job that had already completed its swap lands a verified generation of the abandoned timeline, which is moved to `quarantine/` as `STALE_EPOCH` and is never a candidate (otherwise a later death would restore the ghost of the previous timeline). Recovery on opening a campaign runs the crash-point scan of T-5: any `*.sav`, `*.bak` or `*.tmp` that verifies is accepted and ordered by `generation`; a file that cannot be proven obsolete is never deleted.

**SL-11 — Load pipeline, pre-check and fallback classes.** All pre-check steps run **before the kernel is touched**, so a bad file never produces `FAULTED`. The pre-check is a pure function of `(bytes, registry view, build constants)` — no clock, no file handle — and is unit-testable over in-memory byte arrays. Pipeline: candidates (ring files plus reconciled leftovers, sorted by header `generation` descending, minus demoted and quarantined) → pre-check (table T-5, a closed `SL_PRECHECK_*` reason set) → stage the decoded WORLD blocks (plus PROFILE on a cold open) → `request_restore` (the kernel orders restore by `restore_after`, runs every `restore()` before any `post_restore()`, CR-8) → verification (SL-12) → `LOADING → RUNNING` with `restore_reason` published (SL-21). **Failure classes decide what happens next:**

| Class | Examples | Outcome |
|---|---|---|
| **CORRUPT** (integrity / structure) | bad magic, truncation, trailer or block hash mismatch, malformed header, decode failure, scope or seat mismatch, caps exceeded, mandatory block missing | The file is a **quarantine candidate** (moved to `quarantine/`, never deleted); the loader falls back to the next candidate automatically |
| **INCOMPATIBLE** (intact, not ours) | `format_version` or layout unknown, `min_reader_version` above ours, `tick_rate` mismatch, unknown non-retired block, unsupported `schema_version` | **The loader stops** and shows "made by an incompatible build"; files stay in place, never quarantined; the campaign directory is **read-only** for the older build (it never writes a generation into a campaign whose newest header has `format_version` above its own). A silent fallback would roll the player back to an older loadable generation and then overwrite newer files |
| **MISSING** | no candidate passes | `SL_LOAD_NO_LOADABLE_SAVE` (SL-7 / menu) |

*(Orchestrator ruling: this reorders R10 and overrides R12's "a generation that fails restore twice moves to `quarantine/`" in two ways. First, the trailer SHA-256 moves ahead of any JSON parse or version judgement so a flipped bit in `format_version` is reported as corruption (and falls back) rather than as "made by a newer build" (which must not). Second, the quarantine rule applies to the CORRUPT class and to `QUARANTINE_AFTER_FAILURES` repeated owner-reported restore failures; a **reproduction** mismatch (SL-12) is a code defect — the file hashed fine — so it marks the generation `suspect` in the manifest and keeps the file, because quarantining healthy player files for our own bug would hide them.)* Owner-level failures reported during `request_restore` (`<BLOCK>_RESTORE_*`, for example `GRAPH_RESTORE_UNKNOWN_DEF`) put the kernel in `FAULTED` with the previous state not intact; the kernel's only exit is an explicit user restore, so the loader's automatic fallback is **limited to pre-check failures**, and after `FAULTED` the menu's Continue re-runs the pipeline on the remaining candidates (SL-22). The pre-check makes that path nearly unreachable by catching everything it can first. Unknown block ids on the build's `RETIRED_BLOCKS` list are stripped with a diagnostics note (a removed block does not strand old saves); any other unknown id refuses, so the kernel's `RESTORE_UNKNOWN_BLOCK` is unreachable — reaching it is a Save/Load defect. A missing block is filled by `default_state()` only when its registration declares `added_in_format` greater than the file's `format_version`; otherwise the file is refused. `LOAD_MAX_ATTEMPTS` (= `AUTOSAVE_GENERATIONS`) bounds one load. For the death stub, the pre-check, decode and staging run **at the `DEAD` tick boundary, before `request_restore(payload)` is issued** (the pre-check is pure and needs no kernel); their main-thread file I/O (a few ms for a ~160 KiB file; cold-cache and scanner latency [UNV]) lands in the `DEAD` tick, where the Presenter holds the down sheet's last frame, and **not** in `LOADING`, which holds only the kernel's restore and the verification window of SL-12. If the spike shows the `DEAD` tick is hitched too long, a hot in-memory copy of the newest verified bytes or a pre-stage during the `DOWNED` window are recorded alternatives (→ ADR candidates, OQ-23), not MVP rules.

**SL-12 — Verification: file stage, reproduction stage, and the verification window.** Verification has two stages (Formula `save_verification`, Formulas). The **file stage** is always on and runs before the kernel is touched: trailer SHA-256, every `payload_sha256`, structure. The **reproduction stage** proves the restore reproduced the snapshot: after the last `post_restore()` and **before the first `RUNNING` tick**, recompute `state_hash(PERSIST, T)` (D.12, cited, never restated) over the restored WORLD blocks and compare it with the header's `state_hash`. **Validity guard (needs no kernel hook):** the compare counts only if `kernel.clock.T` equals the header `T` at compare time; otherwise `SL_VERIFY_WINDOW_MISSED` is logged and **no verdict is recorded** (a hash taken after a tick has run would differ for the wrong reason). Its mode is **EXACT** unless one of five closed **REBASED causes** holds: (1) a migration ran, (2) a retired block was stripped, (3) `default_state()` filled a missing block, (4) the header's `hash_algo` is unsupported by this build (the compare is skipped with `SL_VERIFY_ALGO_SKIPPED`; the state hash is a bug detector, and refusing on it would make a kernel hash change a save-breaking event, contradicting SL-16), or (5) a member of the Save/Load-owned build constant `REBASE_BLOCKS` is present in the file. In REBASED mode the recomputed value is recorded as the generation's baseline **in `campaign.json` only** (a hint — SL-14 forbids writing back to the file) and `SL_VERIFY_REBASED{cause}` is logged; it is not a failure. In EXACT mode a mismatch is `SL_VERIFY_NOT_REPRODUCED{blocks}` (naming the diverging blocks when the kernel exposes per-block hashes): **release** builds mark the generation `suspect` in `campaign.json` and **play on**; **dev and test** builds halt on the first occurrence (it is a K:839 / G4 regression). A reproduction mismatch **never falls back to another generation**: the file stage has already proved the bytes, so a mismatch after a successful restore is deterministic code (`restore(snapshot) ≠ identity`) that reproduces on every generation, and walking the ring would strand the player on an intact ring with `SL_LOAD_NO_LOADABLE_SAVE` for our own defect. `SUSPECT_THRESHOLD` is a diagnostics escalation only. *(Orchestrator ruling — findings the first rulings did not settle, as amended at the stage-5 checkpoint. (1) R10 says the compare runs after every restore in every build; applied literally, the first schema bump would fail every older generation (a migrated block's canonical bytes differ from the stored ones), hence the EXACT/REBASED modes and their closed cause set. (2) **Restore-idempotence is the kernel's rule, not a Save/Load request:** K:839 already requires, for every registered block, that the snapshot → interchange → restore → snapshot round trip yields byte-identical canonical bytes, and G:1096 is the Graph's instance of it; a registration flag that let a block opt out would be a CR-8 field invented by Save/Load and would make verification permanently vacuous, so none exists. The Controller's restore is a non-identity (PC-17: `octant`, `run` and `channel_ref` reset and `CHANNELLING` / `STAGGERED` become `IDLE`; the Controller draft's Body text zeroed `sub` and set `previous := current` in `post_restore`, P:913, while P:285 persists `sub` — answered for `body.state` by Ship Body & Interior Physics BDY-31: `post_restore` preserves `sub`, and `body.state` is restore-idempotent at block level *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass)*), and the Controller's G4 claim (P:1045) cannot hold while the control run carries sub-pixel residue that the restored run zeroes and the live Adapter's resync command is absent from a replayed stream. That is routed to the Controller/Body author as a **CONFLICT against K:839 and G4** naming those three specifics — PC-17 restore normalisation; `sub = 0` and `previous := current` in `post_restore` against a persisted `sub`; the P:1045 replay claim against the residue and the absent resync command (OQ-9) — and the resolution is the owners': express the normalisation in `snapshot()` or move it out of restore. **Interim, Save/Load-owned and provisional (provisional — Controller answered, PC-17 normalises in `snapshot()`; Body answered, BDY-31):** the build constant `REBASE_BLOCKS` (default `[controller.state]` — `body.state` left it when BDY-31 met SL-15 item 10; emptied by Save/Load when AC-SL-67 passes) lists the blocks whose presence selects REBASED mode. A non-empty list is a known kernel-contract debt: while it holds, every real MVP file contains those blocks and so verifies REBASED, which makes the reproduction stage diagnostic-only in release for real saves and exact only for files without them. The through-disk G4 criteria therefore run on the kernel's stand-in blocks and `graph.state` (real and restore-idempotent, G:1096), and the real Controller/Body criteria are (review) and blocked on OQ-9. (3) **K-4** (provisional): the kernel text gives Save/Load no hook between the last `post_restore()` and the first `RUNNING` tick. The recommended mechanism is that `request_restore(payload, expected_state_hash)` is compared inside `LOADING` and reported to the requester as a **non-fatal** verdict (not a `FAULTED` transition, because release plays on); the alternative is a restore-complete callback to the requester; restoring into `PAUSED`, verifying and resuming is rejected (it conflicts with the Pipeline's `LOADING → RUNNING` `CUT`, with single-player-only pause in CR-16 and with VS-B's reuse of the restore path). Until the kernel owner answers (OQ-6), the reproduction stage runs in the test harness and in dev builds, which drive ticks and can call `state_hash` between restore and tick one, and in release only under the validity guard, diagnostic-only. This GDD states the requirement — the compare happens before tick one — and routes the mechanism.)* Integrity here is against accidental corruption, not tampering (host-owned saves, no PvP, no leaderboard).

**SL-13 — Observation channel for a control-plane caller (provisional — kernel owner).** Save/Load is a host-local control-plane caller (CR-15), not one of the kernel's five subscriber classes (PRESENTATION, INPUT_SURFACE, PROJECTOR, REPLICATION, DIAGNOSTIC). Its reads are limited to: `kernel.clock` (`T`, `world_seed`); `controller.state.loco_state` per bound seat; the Life-Support Graph's `read_view.failures()` for the Graph half of the soft gate (designed, LS-19; required at MVP; a SIM read with no perceived variant, so it is not a `PERCEPTION_OUT` view) and the optional Attention SIM view `attention.view` for the Attention half (designed; absent = open); the event-trigger reads of SL-4 (the `POD_ARRIVED` notification, Assembly's pure read `last_shape_change_T()` and Interaction's pure read `last_salvage_T()`); and the Controller's end-of-tick change notification for `controller.state` (for the death stub) and for any PROFILE block (for SL-9). It **never** reads a `PERCEPTION_OUT` view (CR-12, H5) and **never writes a block** (H3, H4). Which subscriber class or read access a persistence service has is not stated by the kernel's interaction table; it is routed to the kernel owner (OQ-7), and until it is answered every observation is a tick-boundary read-view poll made in the same boundary in which the request will be issued.

**SL-14 — Versioning and migration (D7).** Two levels. **Container** `format_version` (from 1, `FORMAT_VERSION`) is migrated by Save/Load; **block** `schema_version` is migrated by the *owner* inside `restore(data, stored_version)` as a chain v → v+1 → …, each step a registered pure function `(old dictionary) → new dictionary`. **Save/Load never rewrites a block's data itself** — owners are the only writers of their shape. Save/Load verifies, in the pre-check (T-5 step "schema chain"), that the chain is complete for every stored block (a gap is `SL_PRECHECK_SCHEMA_UNSUPPORTED`, never a mid-restore failure). A migrated payload is **not written back** to the old generation file; the next autosave writes the new shape, so a downgrade followed by an upgrade cannot half-migrate a file. A persisted WORLD block is **mandatory** for a file iff its registration's `added_in_format ≤ file.format_version`; the MVP mandatory set (SL-25) is the *expected result* of that rule, not a hand-kept list. An **additive bump** — a new block added after the first outside build — is `format_version + 1`, `min_reader_version` = the new version, container migration = identity, the new block filled by `default_state()`; defining it keeps the friction of adding a block constant. `added_in_format`, `RETIRED_BLOCKS` and the mandatory flag live in one data file cross-checked against the live registry at boot (→ ADR candidate). **Policy:** the machinery exists from format 1 and one **golden save per shipped version** is retained in `tests/fixtures/saves/` from v1 (the Graph's acceptance criterion names the convention); the **obligation** to migrate begins with the first build given to anyone outside the project (the three-person MVP playtest); earlier development saves are disposable and are refused with "made by an incompatible development build". That policy is Save/Load's own; the Graph's citation of a Layout open question ("pre-release saves are disposable") is answered from the Layout side *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass)*: a renamed `room_key` or `template_id` needs a migration step in the owner once the first build has been given outside the project; before that, saves are disposable (this rule; OQ-12 closed). After the policy begins, a rename of a `def_id` or a `room_id` needs a migration step in its owner. The header's `catalogue_version` (designed — Item & Module Database, IDB-26, IDB-27) lets the owner detect a content-epoch change. A header `catalogue_version` above or below the running catalogue's is never a pre-check refusal (it is not a T-5 step): the load proceeds and each holder's `restore()` decides, with `GRAPH_RESTORE_UNKNOWN_DEF` as the model — this answers the Item DB's OQ 7 and its edge case on a save written under a higher `catalogue_version`. An unknown `def_id` at restore stays the owning block's named failure (`GRAPH_RESTORE_UNKNOWN_DEF`), and a `renamed_from` alias honoured only at restore is the Item DB's to offer. `format_epoch` (the brief's separate field) is **dropped**: `format_version`, `min_reader_version` and `added_in_format` cover it.

**SL-15 — Block authoring contract (R23; layered on CR-8, not a kernel amendment).** Save/Load's main deliverable to every block owner. A `persist` block must:

1. carry a namespaced `block_id`; declare `class`, `persist`, an explicit `persist_scope` (WORLD or PROFILE) and `scope`; a `PERCEPTION_OUT` block is never `persist`;
2. declare `schema_version` ≥ 1, bumped on any serialized-shape change, and `supported_versions` (the kernel's pre-check uses the list only "if owners declare"; Save/Load requires it) containing the current version;
3. return from `snapshot()` plain data only (H7: primitives, arrays, dictionaries, packed arrays; no `Vector2i` — integer vectors travel as two ints or a packed int array), in deterministic order, with entity references by stable ID and **no** derived caches, object references, `Resource` or `Node`; untyped containers; no `StringName` keys;
4. encode 64-bit data per S-1 (owner values above 2^53 never become float-typed JSON numbers; they travel inside the binary payload);
5. implement two-phase `restore(data, stored_version)` (decode and validate into scratch, swap on success) returning a value `(ok, reason, detail)` with `reason` from the block's own closed `<BLOCK>_RESTORE_*` set (the Graph's `GRAPH_RESTORE_*` is the model); a non-conforming return (null, wrong type, a reason outside the declared set) is `SL_RESTORE_NONCONFORMING` and is a hard failure — GDScript has no exceptions, so a runtime script error yields a garbage return [UNV];
6. treat out-of-range values as a hard failure, **never clamp** ("a corrupt save must not be fixed into a different world");
7. provide `default_state()` and, for a block added after format 1, `added_in_format`;
8. in `post_restore()` resolve cross-block IDs, rebuild caches, check invariants, and set `previous := current` for every moving read view (Pipeline Interactions row 1, PC-17) so the first frame after a load does not interpolate from a stale pose;
9. track dirty keys explicitly in its mutators — Godot 4.7 packed-array element writes do not call the property setter (sourced) — so any cached hash the block exposes stays honest;
10. be **restore-idempotent**: `snapshot(restore(snapshot()))` equals `snapshot()` in canonical bytes — the kernel's own rule (K:839: the round trip yields byte-identical canonical bytes for every registered block, whose byte-identity assertion K-3 retains while it changes only the transport; the Graph's instance is G:1096). A block whose restore normalises transient fields (the Controller's PC-17) does not conform; that is the routed CONFLICT of OQ-9, and until the owners answer, the build constant `REBASE_BLOCKS` (SL-12) names such blocks;
11. register its migration steps per version and a golden save per shipped version (SL-14);
12. key `PER_PLAYER` data by PlayerId inside its own dictionaries, and pass the generic round-trip test every block has (`snapshot → var_to_bytes → bytes_to_var → restore → snapshot` equal, plus a JSON-dump assertion that 64-bit values arrive as strings — K-3).

Enforcement is by **boot assertions in the Save/Load service**, not by the kernel; a violation fails Save/Load's own boot — the composition root refuses to start, naming the block and the rule — never a runtime surprise. Whether that surfaces as the kernel's `BOOTING → FAULTED` is the composition root's mechanism (routed to the kernel owner; `FAULTED`'s triggers are kernel-owned, K:159):

| Reason | Condition |
|---|---|
| `SL_BOOT_NO_SUPPORTED_VERSIONS` | a `persist` block declares no `supported_versions`, or the list lacks its own current `schema_version` |
| `SL_BOOT_RESTORE_AFTER_UNREGISTERED` | a `restore_after` target is not a registered block id |
| `SL_BOOT_PERCEPTION_PERSIST` | `class PERCEPTION_OUT` with `persist = true` (CR-12) |
| `SL_BOOT_SCOPE_MISSING` | `persist = true` without `persist_scope` (catches the Sanity row gap mechanically) |
| `SL_BOOT_PER_PLAYER_UNKEYED` | a `PER_PLAYER` block whose fixture snapshot does not key its data by PlayerId |
| `SL_BOOT_NO_ADDED_IN_FORMAT` | a persisted block has no `added_in_format` entry in the format data file |
| `SL_BOOT_RETIRED_BUT_REGISTERED` | an id is on `RETIRED_BLOCKS` and also registered |
| `SL_BOOT_STRINGNAME_KEY` / `SL_BOOT_TYPED_CONTAINER` | the fixture snapshot contains a `StringName` key or a typed container |
| `SL_BOOT_SAVELOAD_REGISTERS_BLOCK` | Save/Load itself registered a block or hook (SL-17) |
| `SL_BOOT_KNOB_INVARIANT{id}` | a Tuning Knobs invariant I1–I8 or I10 fails against the data file (I9 is a diagnostic) |

**SL-16 — Hash roles (D8; R10, R15).** Four distinct integrity layers, no overlap. (1) The **file trailer** SHA-256 over every preceding byte and (2) the per-block **`payload_sha256`** over each stored payload — Save/Load's own, fixed, native [UNV], verified before the kernel is touched; they localise damage and guard accidental corruption and truncation, not tampering. (3) The header **`state_hash`** and the optional per-block **`block_state_hash`** are the *kernel's* 64-bit hash over the owners' canonical bytes (D.12), named by `hash_algo`, so the kernel's hash ADR (xxHash64 vs FNV-1a 64 vs a truncated SHA-256) and the per-block cached hash (**K-1**, a kernel ADR candidate — cited, never owned) can change with no `format_version` bump; `block_state_hash` is present only if the kernel exposes a per-block hash, is diagnostic and is never a refusal reason. (4) The post-restore **reproduction compare** of SL-12 detects a restore that does not reproduce the snapshot — a bug, not corruption. The kernel's D.11 worked example assumed the hash cost is a debug-build cost; this design makes it a release cost on every capture and restore, and a 64-bit hash written in GDScript over 100–300 KiB may exceed the 1.5 ms that example allowed [UNV] — K-1 and the spike are the mitigation (OQ-2, OQ-17).

**SL-17 — Zero blocks, zero hooks (D13).** Save/Load registers no state block and no step hook: ring bookkeeping lives in `campaign.json`, because a WORLD block would roll back on restore and desync the file set from the world. The kernel's acceptance criterion that the registry holds exactly one block, `kernel.clock`, stays true with Save/Load booted. The MVP save request is a host-local API call (CR-15), so CR-16's "save-request state" is not needed. A `saveload.request_save` command type (`origin PLAYER`, `allowed_when_paused`, `actor_binding NONE`) for VS-B guests is **reserved and (provisional — Co-op Session & Replication, VS-B)** at every mention; nothing in the MVP depends on it.

**SL-18 — New game entry (D14; routed amendment K-2).** Save/Load owns only `new_game(world_seed)`: create the campaign directory and a random `campaign_id`, set `world_seed` into `kernel.clock`, and write the **initial generation** (kind `EVENT`, trigger `NEW_GAME`) at the first tick boundary after the world is constructed, so a death before the first interval restores a fresh world rather than nothing.
