Who *builds* the world after `default_state()` is a kernel gap: a new game passes through `LOADING` populating blocks from `default_state()`, but `default_state()` yields empty blocks (the Graph's `EMPTY` state has no sections) and the home-ship and the two hand-made derelicts must be constructed through `instantiate_section`, Layout and Body. The recommended `init_world(world_seed)` owner step, run in `LOADING` in `restore_after` order under a WriteScope, is **K-2 (provisional — kernel amendment)** and is written in OQ-4, not as a rule; world content is Web Map's, Layout's and Authoring's. If world construction fails, or the baseline cannot be written after `AUTOSAVE_FAIL_ESCALATE_COUNT` attempts, the empty campaign directory is removed (it holds no verified generation, so the no-deletion rule is intact) and the menu receives `SL_NEW_GAME_NO_BASELINE`: a campaign with no verified generation would make the first death unrecoverable.

**SL-19 — Directory layout, compression, settings (D15–D17).** `user://saves/<campaign_id>/` holds `campaign.json` (the manifest hint), `gen_0.sav … gen_{N−1}.sav`, transient `.tmp`/`.bak`, `gen_k.demoted` files (SL-8), `profile.sav` and its `.bak`, and `quarantine/` (files moved there carry a reason — `CORRUPT`, `RESTORE_FAILED`, `STALE_EPOCH` — and are never deleted automatically; the folder's size is reported in diagnostics against `SL_QUARANTINE_SIZE_ALARM_BYTES`, a diagnostics threshold only; deletion happens only through the explicit campaign delete under hold-to-confirm). `campaign_id` is 128-bit random hex; one campaign exists in the MVP, many are permitted (Alpha host-owned worlds). Starting a new campaign over an existing one is destructive and defaults to retiring the old campaign (renaming it out of the active location); deletion is a separate explicit action under hold-to-confirm (HR-14, art bible; the UI is the Chart Table's). **No compression in the MVP** (typical size ≈ 100–300 KiB, kernel D.11 expectation); header `codec` 0 with 1 reserved; a size alarm at `SAVE_SIZE_ALARM_BYTES` 2 MiB (diagnostics only), re-evaluated at VS-A (600–1 200 Graph nodes). **Settings and key bindings are never in a snapshot**: Settings & Input Rebinding (Alpha) owns a per-client `ConfigFile`; Save/Load may expose only the atomic-write helper. Steam Auto-Cloud is Alpha and constrains the path layout only.

**SL-20 — Identity (D12).** The header `seats` list names the PlayerIds present, **for validation only**; `PER_PLAYER` blocks are keyed by PlayerId inside their own dictionaries; the binding table (`peer_id → PlayerId`) is never saved (kernel CR-7) and a session rebuilds it. Single-player is PlayerId 1 bound to `HOST_PEER_ID` 1. A file whose `PER_PLAYER` keys are not a subset of `seats`, or whose `seats` is empty, duplicated or out of range, is refused (`SL_PRECHECK_SEATS_INVALID`). "Keyed by peer ID" is a stale token; a reconnecting peer with a different transport id changes nothing in a save.

**SL-21 — Published reads, `SaveInfo`, `restore_reason` and the host-local API (R7, R20, D11, D18).** Save/Load publishes **host-local reads**, never commands and never blocks: `save_status` ∈ {`IDLE`, `WRITING`, `WRITTEN`, `FAILED`} (`WRITTEN` is sticky until the next write begins and carries `written_at_T`; no HUD element displays it per write, *(Amended 2026-10-26 — CD-GDD-ALIGN)*); `fail_escalated` (bool) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, HUD OQ-HS-21)* — true from the `AUTOSAVE_FAIL_ESCALATE_COUNT`-th consecutive failed write until the next `WRITTEN`, because `save_status` reads `FAILED` on every failed write and the HUD counts nothing (HS-46); `SaveInfo` for the newest generation — campaign id, generation, kind (`AUTO`/`EVENT`/`QUIT`), `T`, playtime (`T div TICK_RATE`, whole seconds, integer floor — OQ-30), `game_version`, crew count, `RISKY`, depth label (provisional — derived from the node `ring` of `web.view`; Web Map publishes no depth-label field) — **computed from SIM blocks at capture**; and `restore_reason` ∈ {`NEW_GAME`, `LOAD_GAME`, `DEATH_STUB`, `FAULT_RECOVERY`}, valid for the first `RUNNING` frame after `LOADING`. **`restore_reason` is a routed request, not an existing flag:** the Pipeline GDD has no such field (a search finds zero hits), so the brief's statement that the Pipeline was already amended is false; this GDD offers the flag as *provided* and requests it of the Pipeline, HUD & Suit Layer (designed, hud-and-suit-layer.md; the request stands) and Failure Contract & Re-wake (provisional — undesigned); nothing in the MVP depends on a consumer reading it, and no consumer may use `DEATH_STUB` to alter hallucination content (SL-24). **There is no screenshot thumbnail** (it would capture the Perception Filter's lie, Pillar 1), **no audio cue** (any sound is attributable by the web, Pillar 3, unless Audio justifies one) and **no death screen** (art bible 7.4). There is **no per-write autosave mark** in the MVP (UI-1 retired): the HUD & Suit Layer draws only the failed-write notice (UI-10, tied to write health, never to the gate; its shape and placement are the HUD's, HS-46), and the Chart Table's pause panel (`/ux-design`) shows one recency fact, `SaveInfo.playtime` of the newest generation, static while paused, never a countdown or a "time since". *(Amended 2026-10-26 — CD-GDD-ALIGN)* The MVP host-local API (consumer: Chart Table, designed): `new_game(world_seed)`, `continue_campaign()`, `save_and_quit()`, read-only `campaign_info()` (newest `SaveInfo` plus `newest_failed_verification` (bool) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass)*: true when the newest generation by header `generation` failed the file stage at the last ring scan (opening the campaign, T-5) and a load would fall back, so the Chart Table can show UI-7 on the main menu (ASK-CT-5); otherwise the UI-7 line is the HUD's on the first `RUNNING` frame) and `list_saves()`, and `retire_campaign(campaign_id)` / `delete_campaign(campaign_id)` for the destructive actions; no `save_now()` and no `load(generation)` outside a dev build. **Restore timing (ASK-CT-7)** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass)*: the kernel's restore and every `post_restore()` run as one blocking call inside `LOADING`, never chunked across frames; the pre-check and staging run before it (SL-11). Save/Load imposes no minimum black frame of its own and publishes no progress: the black hold is the Pipeline's `TRANSIT_HOLD` (`t_hold_pad` ≥ 120 ms, F-8), and the one presented black frame the Chart Table assumes (CT-42) is the Pipeline's to guarantee.

**SL-22 — `FAULTED` recovery (D10).** The kernel in `FAULTED` never writes or autosaves over an existing save, and a snapshot or autosave in `FAULTED` hands no payload; on a capture error the existing files are untouched. **Release:** `FAULTED` → main menu → "Continue" runs the full load pipeline (SL-11) on the newest verified generation through `request_restore` (the kernel's only exit besides shutdown), with `restore_reason = FAULT_RECOVERY`; the faulting session is never autosaved. A generation whose restore fails `QUARANTINE_AFTER_FAILURES` times (default 2, safe 1–3) because the *owner* reported a restore failure moves to `quarantine/` (never deleted); a reproduction mismatch only marks `suspect` (SL-11). **Dev build:** halt with diagnostics; the optional in-memory pre-restore snapshot the kernel allows is diagnostics only, never an automatic revert. Whether Save/Load may issue the recovery restore automatically in release (rather than on "explicit user action") is a kernel-owner question (K-4; OQ-20).

**SL-23 — Determinism, save-scum and wall clock.** Every persisted counter and seed is restored exactly (the kernel's `rng_derive` with counters inside the owners' blocks, G4 and D.10; the Graph keys its random draws by `(step_no, subject_id)` from `world_seed`), so the **physical** world replays the same way (Pillar 1). Whether a **perception** draw (a false system state's onset, sanity drift) replays identically after a restore, or is re-keyed, is Sanity & Hallucination Rules' decision; Save/Load makes no promise either way, and routes the constraint that any re-keying must not be steerable by the player — no reroll oracle through quit or death (OQ-37). *(Amended 2026-10-26 — CD-GDD-ALIGN)* Save/Load stores true SIM state only (SL-24). Every interval, gap and window in SL-4, SL-5 and SL-8 is in ticks read from `kernel.clock.T`; `created_unix` and `game_version` are file metadata written by the service and never read by the sim (H9), so the tests are deterministic. The Graph's note that "single-slot autosave is Save/Load's and the user's decision" is answered by SL-3's ring; the determinism argument is unaffected. The kernel's `rng_derive` note that a 63-bit owner-defined key is a valid `counter` appears only in OQ-3 as a routed kernel amendment (D.10), not as a rule here. A process kill after a mistake rewinds by at most one interval plus the deferral cap — the same price as dying, minus the death floor — and is an accepted, unpreventable exposure (no write can protect against it), recorded for playtest.

**SL-24 — Truth-only persistence (Pillars 1 and 4).** Save/Load never reads, derives, stores or varies by perception: no `PERCEPTION_OUT` block is ever written (a boot assertion and a pre-check step refuse one), no "what the player saw" field exists, no thumbnail, no sanity-dependent autosave timing, no hallucinated save list (H1, H6). The first `RUNNING` frame after a load must already be presented through the Perception Filter for the restored sanity level — the black hold covers any rebuild (H4); the ordering is a **routed request** to Perception Filter, Sanity and Pipeline (all three designed; the request stands), recorded as a cross-system requirement with no owner yet (OQ-11). The failed-write notice and the pause panel's newest-record line are inviolable channels (H5): their look is UX's, but they never take flesh or violet, never flicker with sanity and are never a hallucination class; there is no per-write glyph *(Amended 2026-10-26 — CD-GDD-ALIGN)*. Because Suit vitals and the Sanity level roll back with the world, a reload is also never a sanity cure (H3).

**SL-25 — What is saved: provisional block inventory and the mandatory set.** The kernel's snapshot covers every `persist` block at one `T`; Save/Load splits it by `persist_scope` (SL-9). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass)* Every owner GDD below is designed and every name is the registered `block_id` (the registry entry name in brackets where it differs); `web.state` keeps its "provisional name until merge" tag:

| `block_id` | Owner | `persist_scope` × `scope` | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| `kernel.clock` | Kernel | WORLD × SHARED, `SIM`; canonical keys exactly `T` and `world_seed` | designed; mandatory |
| `layout.state` (registry: `layout_state_block`) | Ship Section & Layout Data Model | WORLD × SHARED, `repl_hint SHARED_SUMMARY`, schema 1, `restore_after []` | designed (LY-1); mandatory; **the `restore_after` root of `graph.state`, `emitter.state` and `body.state`**: room identity must be stable across versions (a renamed `room_id` breaks Graph restore with `GRAPH_RESTORE_UNKNOWN_ROOM`; migration stance OQ-12) |
| `graph.state` | Life-Support Graph | WORLD × SHARED, `repl_hint SHARED_SUMMARY`, `schema_version` 1, `restore_after [layout.state]` | designed; mandatory; zero floats; largest block (≤ 32 KB at MVP size, per the Graph's cost criterion) |
| `controller.state` | Player Controller & Input | WORLD × PER_PLAYER (the Controller author confirms the scope value) | designed; mandatory; `carry_ref` already saved here |
| `body.state` (registry: `body_state_block`) | Ship Body & Interior Physics (BDY-5) | WORLD × SHARED, `repl_hint SHARED_SUMMARY`, schema 1, `restore_after [layout.state]` | designed; mandatory; restore-idempotent at block level (BDY-31), so not in `REBASE_BLOCKS` |
| `emitter.state` | Emitter State & Light-Exposure Query | WORLD × SHARED, `SHARED_SUMMARY`, schema 1, `restore_after: [layout.state, graph.state, body.state, controller.state]` per EM-1 (designed; supersedes the three-block list in the sibling brief, which omitted `body.state`) | designed (EM-1) |
| `interaction.state` | Interaction System (IA-36) | WORLD × SHARED (per-seat records keyed by PlayerId), `repl_hint SHARED_SUMMARY`, schema 1, `restore_after [layout.state, controller.state, graph.state, emitter.state]` | designed; `post_restore` drops door attempts (`attempts[]`) and in-progress channels and clears `last_result` (IA-36) — a restore normalisation, OQ-39 |
| `assembly.state` | Modular Ship Assembly (MSA-4) | WORLD × SHARED, `repl_hint SHARED_SUMMARY`, schema 1, `restore_after [layout.state, graph.state, interaction.state, body.state]` | designed; per-seat `pending` and `last_result` only, no channel progress and no world history; `post_restore` verifies the as-restored block then clears it (OQ-39) |
| `suit.state` | Suit & Equipment | WORLD × PER_PLAYER (keyed by PlayerId; rolls back on the death stub), `repl_hint SHARED_SUMMARY` | designed; schema 1, `restore_after []` |
| `sanity.state` | Sanity & Hallucination Rules | WORLD × PER_PLAYER (keyed by PlayerId), `repl_hint SHARED_SUMMARY`, schema 1, `restore_after []` | designed; closed `SANITY_RESTORE_*` reasons (SCHEMA_UNSUPPORTED, SHAPE, LEVEL_RANGE, STAGE_RANGE, COUNTER_RANGE, LIE_KIND_UNKNOWN, LIE_TIME_ORDER, SUBJECT_UNRESOLVED) |
| `sanity.ledger` | Sanity & Hallucination Rules | WORLD × PER_PLAYER, `repl_hint HOST_ONLY`, schema 1, `restore_after [sanity.state, graph.state, layout.state]` (+ `horror.state`, provisional) | designed; lie records and RNG counters inside; read by nothing outside sanity/ except the Debug Overlay |
| Sanity perception output | Sanity | `PERCEPTION_OUT`, **never persisted**, rebuilt from the restored `sanity.state` and the persisted `sanity.ledger` (the active lies are SIM state a seed cannot reproduce) | designed |
| `attention.state` | Attention System | WORLD × SHARED, `repl_hint SHARED_SUMMARY`, schema 1, `restore_after [graph.state, emitter.state]` | designed; closed `ATTENTION_RESTORE_*` reasons, never clamps |
| `web.state` (provisional name until merge): derelict records, seeds and deltas, pod and leg state | Web Map & Transit | WORLD × SHARED, `repl_hint SHARED_SUMMARY`, schema 1, `restore_after [layout.state, graph.state, body.state]`; about 2 KB | designed; seeds plus deltas keep it small; the loaded and berthed sets are derived, never persisted; unloaded derelicts persist as data (Graph `dormant` sections) |
| `horror.state` | Horror Entities | WORLD × SHARED, `repl_hint SHARED_SUMMARY`, schema 1, `restore_after [layout.state, graph.state, emitter.state, attention.state]`; RNG counter (`horror.tell`) and the mark ring inside | designed; closed `HORROR_RESTORE_*` reasons |
| PROFILE blocks | Knowledge Codex, Sanity scars, Failure Contract | PROFILE | VS / Alpha; **zero in the MVP** |
| (none) | Save/Load; Failure & Trail; Item & Module Database; 2D Lighting & Occlusion | — | register no block (2D Lighting & Occlusion is a pure renderer, LO-29) |

The MVP **mandatory set** — the expected result of SL-14's derivation, now that those GDDs are designed — is `kernel.clock`, `layout.state`, `graph.state`, `controller.state`, `body.state`, `emitter.state`, `interaction.state`, `assembly.state`, `suit.state`, `sanity.state`, `sanity.ledger`, `attention.state`, `web.state` (provisional name until merge) and `horror.state`: every MVP-registered WORLD block *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass)*. **Not in a snapshot:** the pending command queue (kernel data), the binding table, the command log, diagnostics, any `PERCEPTION_OUT` block, camera and zoom state (the Pipeline's; never saved), the Controller Adapter's local state (`last_acked`, hold timers, run latch, device, pre-play tokens), derived caches of any block, and settings and bindings.

**Reconciliation notes (how the specialists' proposals and the rulings were merged).**

| # | Question | Resolution | Source of the pick |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | File layout | R1 plus a 32-byte SHA-256 trailer; layout id in the magic's last byte | technical-director, lead-programmer; additive to R1 |
| 2 | Ring write sequence | New generation into the oldest ring position; `.bak` = replaced oldest; order by header `generation` | all three specialists; R11's guarantee kept |
| 3 | Gate hard vs soft | `DOWNED`/`DEAD`, kernel state, restore pending and writer busy are hard; tell/breach/transit are soft; cap overrides soft only; QUIT bypasses soft conditions only (refused with `SL_QUIT_NO_WRITE_DOWNED` while any seat is `DOWNED`/`DEAD`) and tags `RISKY` | systems-designer, with QUIT per all three; refines R4 |
| 4 | Death-loop guard | R5 verbatim: default newest verified; step back only on failure or `RISKY` + window; demotion by `.demoted` rename | systems-designer; extends R5 and R18 |
| 5 | Fallback and quarantine | CORRUPT falls back and quarantines; INCOMPATIBLE stops and leaves files; reproduction mismatch marks `suspect` | lead-programmer, systems-designer; overrides R12's "twice-failed" |
| 6 | Pre-check order and names | Trailer SHA before the layout id, JSON and version judgement (SL-1 format law: the layout-id byte is inside the SHA-covered range); one closed `SL_PRECHECK_*` set (22 pre-kernel steps, plus the post-restore compare) | lead-programmer; reorders R10 |
| 7 | Post-restore compare | EXACT/REBASED with a closed five-cause set (including `REBASE_BLOCKS`); a mismatch plays on (release) or halts (dev), never the next candidate; `T == header T` validity guard; restore-idempotence is K:839 (CONFLICT routed, OQ-9); K-4 mechanism provisional (OQ-6) | systems-designer, lead-programmer; rulings landed in OQ-9, SL-12 and OQ-6 (K-4); refines R10 |
| 8 | Timeline-fork rule (quarantine every generation newer than the restored one) | Folded into demotion (RISKY step-back) and `STALE_EPOCH` (late-landing write); no separate rule | orchestrator |
| 9 | Per-block hash | Two families: `payload_sha256` (transport, always) and optional kernel `block_state_hash` (K-1 compatible) | lead-programmer |
| 10 | MVP API | No `save_now()`, no `load(generation)`; `continue_campaign`, `new_game`, `save_and_quit`, reads | lead-programmer, technical-director; the brief's API list narrowed |

### States and Transitions

Save/Load owns no kernel state and no state block (SL-17). It has **host-local** state — `last_save_T`, `pending_event` (kind only; events coalesce), `first_due_T`, `restore_epoch`, the writer's state, `restored_gen_id` (the generation this session began from) and per-generation `suspect` counters in `campaign.json` — all re-derived on process start (`last_save_T := T` of the restored generation, `pending_event := none`, `first_due_T := none`), none of it sim state, none of it able to alter the sim (an autosave-transparency criterion in Acceptance Criteria). The kernel's states and transitions are the kernel's and are cited, never restated; the tables below describe what Save/Load may do in each, the lifecycle of a generation file, the writer, the pre-check, crash recovery and the death-stub run.

**T-1. Kernel-state interplay, from Save/Load's seat.**

| Kernel state | What Save/Load may do | What it must not do | Rule |
|---|---|---|---|
| `BOOTING` | Run the block-contract boot assertions on the frozen registry | Issue any request; read a file as a world | SL-15 |
| `LOADING` | Wait; publish `restore_reason`; hold the epoch; verification window (dev/test; release diagnostic-only per SL-12, K-4 provisional) | Issue a second request (the kernel ignores and reports it); autosave | SL-11, SL-12 |
| `RUNNING` | Poll the gate and triggers; `request_snapshot` when an autosave fires; `request_restore` on crew death | `request_pause` / `request_resume` (not its calls) | SL-4, SL-7 |
| `PAUSED` | Save & Quit `request_snapshot`; `request_restore`: dev-build `load(generation)` only (no MVP caller) | Start an interval autosave (`T` cannot advance, so none is due) | SL-6, SL-11 |
| `SAVING` | Wait. A restore requested now waits for the boundary | Queue a second snapshot | SL-10 |
| `FAULTED` | Show recovery; on explicit user action `request_restore` of the newest verified generation; `request_shutdown` | **Any write**, any autosave, any overwrite | SL-22 |
| `SHUTTING_DOWN` | Nothing — the QUIT write completed (`WRITTEN`) before the request was issued | Begin any write | SL-6 |

**T-2. Autosave scheduler (host-local).**

| State | Entry | Exit | Transitions | Who may cause them |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `IDLE` | Boot; a write ended; a restore reset `last_save_T` | `due` becomes true (interval elapsed, or an event is pending and `AUTOSAVE_MIN_GAP_TICKS` has elapsed) or a QUIT is requested | → `DUE_WAITING`; → `FIRING` (QUIT; soft conditions bypassed; refused with `SL_QUIT_NO_WRITE_DOWNED` while any seat is `DOWNED`/`DEAD`) | Clock `T`; event notifications (provisional); the pause panel |
| `DUE_WAITING` | A save is due; `first_due_T` recorded | Gate open on a retry tick; or `AUTOSAVE_MAX_DEFER_TICKS` elapsed with only soft conditions closed | → `FIRING` (`RISKY` if a soft condition was closed); stays while a hard condition is closed (never expires) | Gate inputs re-read every `AUTOSAVE_RETRY_TICKS` |
| `FIRING` | `request_snapshot` issued at a boundary | Capture handed to the writer (T-4 `CAPTURING`) | → `IDLE` on `WRITTEN`; → `BACKOFF` on `FAILED` | Kernel (`SAVING`); the writer |
| `BACKOFF` | A write failed | The backoff interval elapses (bounded exponential, capped by `AUTOSAVE_FAIL_BACKOFF_MAX_TICKS`) | → `DUE_WAITING` (the save is still due, because `last_save_T` is unchanged by a failed write) | Clock |

**T-3. Generation lifecycle (per file).**

| State | Meaning | Entry | Exit | Transitions | Who may cause them | On disk |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `TMP` | Being written | The worker opens `gen_k.tmp` | Re-read hash verifies, or a failure | → `VERIFIED`; → removed (failure) | Writer | `gen_k.tmp` only; previous generations untouched |
| `VERIFIED` | Complete and self-checked, not yet in place | Re-read and re-hash match | The position swap starts | → `NEWEST` | Writer | the verified `.tmp` |
| `NEWEST` | Highest `generation` among non-discarded files | Swap complete and manifest rewritten | A successor reaches `NEWEST` | → `OLDER`; → `DEMOTED`; → `QUARANTINED` | Writer; SL-8; the load pipeline | `gen_k.sav` |
| `OLDER` | A ring member below the newest | A successor became newest | Chosen as the position for reuse, demoted or quarantined | → overwritten by a successor; → `DEMOTED`; → `QUARANTINED` | Writer; SL-8; the load pipeline | `gen_k.sav` |
| `DEMOTED` | Stepped back from (SL-8); excluded from default selection, retained | A RISKY death inside the window | Its ring position is rewritten | → removed only when the position is reused | SL-8 | `gen_k.demoted` |
| `BAK` | The replaced oldest file during a swap | The position's file is renamed to `.bak` | Manifest verified, then removed; or promoted back on a failed swap | → removed; → `gen_k.sav` | Writer; boot recovery | `gen_k.bak` |
| `QUARANTINED` | Failed as CORRUPT, failed owner restore `QUARANTINE_AFTER_FAILURES` times, or landed `STALE_EPOCH` | The pre-check, the kernel or the epoch rule names it | Never leaves automatically | — | Load pipeline; epoch rule | `quarantine/…` with a reason |
| `SUSPECT` | Passed the file stage but failed the reproduction stage (`SL_VERIFY_NOT_REPRODUCED`) | Verification | A later generation reproduces (counter reset) | stays in the ring (release plays on; dev/test halts on the first occurrence); counter only, and `SUSPECT_THRESHOLD` is a diagnostics escalation | Verification (SL-12) | unchanged; counter in `campaign.json` |

**T-4. Write-procedure states.** A failure at any state leaves every previous generation exactly as it was.

| State | `save_status` | Work (runs where) | Failure outcome on disk |
|---|---|---|---|
| `IDLE` | `IDLE` (or sticky `WRITTEN`/`FAILED`) | none | n/a |
| `CAPTURING` | `WRITING` | Kernel `SAVING`, synchronous snapshot; split by `persist_scope`; `SaveInfo` computed; payload encoded and header assembled into an immutable byte value (main thread, tick halted) | A capture error takes the kernel to `FAULTED`; no file touched |
| `WRITING_TMP` | `WRITING` | Worker writes `gen_k.tmp`; **every `store_*` return is checked**; flush; close | Any false return or open error: `.tmp` removed best-effort, `FAILED` |
| `VERIFYING` | `WRITING` | Worker re-opens the `.tmp` and runs the offline subset of the pre-check (every T-5 step up to and including per-block `payload_sha256`: no registry needed) | Mismatch: `.tmp` removed, `FAILED` |
| `SWAPPING_OUT` | `WRITING` | If the position holds a file — the oldest generation, or a `.demoted` occupant, which is treated exactly like an oldest occupant — rename it to `.bak` (a stale verified `.bak` is deleted first; an unverified one aborts the write, `SL_WRITE_STALE_BAK`); the `.bak` is deleted after `POST_VERIFY` | Rename failure (retry `RENAME_RETRY_COUNT`): `FAILED`, the position's file still in place |
| `SWAPPING_IN` | `WRITING` | Rename `.tmp` → `gen_k.sav` (the target is absent, so no rename-onto-existing; Windows overwrite semantics are never relied on [UNV]) | Failure: roll back by renaming `.bak` back; `FAILED` |
| `POST_VERIFY` | `WRITING` | Hash `gen_k.sav` again; on success delete `.bak` | Mismatch: promote `.bak` back, `FAILED` |
| `MANIFEST` | `WRITING` | Rewrite `campaign.json` by the same temp-and-rename (a hint) | A failure here is a diagnostic, **not** a save failure |
| `WRITTEN` | `WRITTEN` | Main thread observes completion at a tick boundary; `last_save_T` updated | n/a |
| `FAILED` | `FAILED` | Diagnostics entry; the HUD read set; `BACKOFF` | Previous generations intact |
| (epoch bump) | unchanged | Any state; a job that sees a newer epoch before its first rename aborts and removes its `.tmp`; one already past `SWAPPING_IN` lands `STALE_EPOCH` | the late generation is quarantined, nothing else changed |

**T-5. Pre-check: ordered decision list.** Runs entirely before the kernel is touched; stop at the first failing step. `SL_PRECHECK_*` is Save/Load's closed refusal set; every refusal is logged with the generation number and step name. **Class** is the failure class of SL-11. The trailer is checked **before** the layout id is judged (the format law of SL-1), so a flipped bit in the layout-id byte is CORRUPT and falls back, never INCOMPATIBLE.

| # | Check | Refusal reason | Class → next action |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Open and read succeed | `SL_PRECHECK_FILE_UNREADABLE` | unreadable (transient) → next candidate; file left in place |
| 2 | Size floor: size ≥ 12 + 2 + 32 (non-empty, not truncated below the minimum) | `SL_PRECHECK_FILE_TOO_SMALL` | CORRUPT → quarantine candidate; next |
| 3 | Size ≤ `MAX_SAVE_BYTES` | `SL_PRECHECK_FILE_TOO_LARGE` | CORRUPT → quarantine candidate; next |
| 4 | The 7-byte magic prefix matches | `SL_PRECHECK_BAD_MAGIC` | CORRUPT → quarantine candidate; next |
| 5 | Trailer: the last 32 bytes equal SHA-256 over `[0, size − 32)` (format law, SL-1) | `SL_PRECHECK_FILE_SHA_MISMATCH` | CORRUPT → quarantine candidate; next |
| 6 | Layout-id byte known | `SL_PRECHECK_LAYOUT_UNKNOWN` | INCOMPATIBLE → stop |
| 7 | `header_len` within bounds (`0 < header_len ≤ MAX_HEADER_BYTES`, `12 + header_len + 32 ≤ size`) | `SL_PRECHECK_HEADER_LEN_INVALID` | CORRUPT → next |
| 8 | Header parses as a JSON object | `SL_PRECHECK_HEADER_NOT_JSON` | CORRUPT → next |
| 9 | Required keys present, types right, integers integral and below 2^53, hex exact-width | `SL_PRECHECK_HEADER_FIELD_MISSING` / `_TYPE` / `_NOT_INT` / `_HEX_MALFORMED` | CORRUPT → next |
| 10 | `format_version` supported (known, or has a registered container migration) and `codec` implemented (0 in the MVP) | `SL_PRECHECK_FORMAT_UNSUPPORTED` | INCOMPATIBLE → stop ("made by a newer build" / "incompatible development build") |
| 11 | `min_reader_version` ≤ our `FORMAT_VERSION` | `SL_PRECHECK_MIN_READER_EXCEEDED` | INCOMPATIBLE → stop |
| 12 | Header `tick_rate` equals `TICK_RATE` | `SL_PRECHECK_TICK_RATE_MISMATCH` (the kernel's own `TICK_RATE_MISMATCH` is the unreachable backstop) | INCOMPATIBLE → stop |
| 13 | Block table contiguous; Σ lengths equals the payload region; each length ≤ `MAX_BLOCK_BYTES`; count ≤ the kernel's `MAX_BLOCKS` (cited, not restated) | `SL_PRECHECK_BLOCK_TABLE_INVALID` / `_PAYLOAD_LENGTH_MISMATCH` / `_BLOCK_TOO_LARGE` / `_BLOCK_COUNT_EXCEEDS_MAX` | CORRUPT → next |
| 14 | No duplicate `block_id` | `SL_PRECHECK_DUPLICATE_BLOCK_ID` | CORRUPT → next |
| 15 | Each `payload_sha256` matches the stored bytes | `SL_PRECHECK_BLOCK_HASH_MISMATCH` (names the block) | CORRUPT → next |
| 16 | Each `block_id` registered, or on `RETIRED_BLOCKS` (stripped with a note — not a refusal) | `SL_PRECHECK_UNKNOWN_BLOCK`; note `SL_PRECHECK_RETIRED_BLOCK_STRIPPED` | INCOMPATIBLE (a newer or foreign build wrote it) → stop |
| 17 | Scope: no block whose registration is `PERCEPTION_OUT` or non-`persist`; a WORLD file holds no PROFILE-scope block and `profile.sav` holds no WORLD block | `SL_PRECHECK_PERCEPTION_BLOCK_PRESENT` / `SL_PRECHECK_SCOPE_MISMATCH` | CORRUPT (never written by us; presence = defect or tamper) → next |
| 18 | Schema chain: each stored `schema_version` ∈ the owner's `supported_versions`, and the migration chain to current is complete | `SL_PRECHECK_SCHEMA_UNSUPPORTED` (names block and stored version) | INCOMPATIBLE → stop |
| 19 | Decode and type walk: each payload decodes (objects disallowed) to the type its registration declares — a Dictionary, or the owner's published canonical byte form (the Graph's) — and the type walk passes (SL-2) | `SL_PRECHECK_BLOCK_DECODE_FAILED` / `_BLOCK_TYPE_FORBIDDEN` / `_BLOCK_DECODE_LIMITS` | CORRUPT → next |
| 20 | Clock block: `kernel.clock` decodes with exactly the keys `T` and `world_seed`; header `T` and `world_seed` equal them | `SL_PRECHECK_CLOCK_BLOCK_INVALID` / `SL_PRECHECK_HEADER_CLOCK_MISMATCH` | CORRUPT → next |
| 21 | Mandatory set present; a missing non-mandatory block only if its `added_in_format` exceeds the file's `format_version` (then `default_state()` and a note) | `SL_PRECHECK_MANDATORY_MISSING` (names the block) | CORRUPT → next |
| 22 | Seats and campaign: `seats` non-empty, unique, in range; `PER_PLAYER` keys ⊆ `seats`; `campaign_id` equals the directory's | `SL_PRECHECK_SEATS_INVALID` / `SL_PRECHECK_CAMPAIGN_MISMATCH` | CORRUPT → next |
| 23 | *(after `request_restore`, inside `LOADING`; K-4 provisional)* Reproduction compare (SL-12), counted only if `kernel.clock.T` equals the header `T` | `SL_VERIFY_NOT_REPRODUCED` / `SL_VERIFY_WINDOW_MISSED` | suspect mark; play on (release) / halt (dev); never the next candidate; `WINDOW_MISSED` records no verdict |

By name: the **integrity** steps are open/read, size floor, `MAX_SAVE_BYTES`, magic prefix, trailer, and per-block `payload_sha256`; the **compatibility** steps are layout id, `format_version`, `min_reader_version`, `tick_rate`, registered/retired and schema chain; the **structure and meaning** steps are `header_len` bounds, JSON parse, fields, block table, duplicates, scope, decode and type walk, clock block, mandatory set and seats/campaign. The **offline subset** is every step up to and including per-block `payload_sha256`: it needs no registry, and the writer re-runs it in `VERIFYING` (T-4).

**T-6. Crash recovery (what is on disk → what the recovery scan does).** Recovery runs when a campaign is opened, before the first load; it is deterministic and never deletes a file it cannot prove obsolete. Every write step is an injection point for the deterministic fault-injection test harness.

| Crash point | On disk | Recovery |
|---|---|---|
| During `WRITING_TMP` | the position's file (previous generation) intact; a partial `gen_k.tmp` | Delete the `.tmp`; the ring is unchanged |
| After `VERIFYING`, before `SWAPPING_OUT` | the position's file intact; a complete `.tmp` | If the `.tmp` passes its trailer and its `generation` exceeds every generation in the ring, roll forward (run the swap); otherwise delete it |
| Between `SWAPPING_OUT` and `SWAPPING_IN` | `gen_k.bak` (the replaced oldest), a complete `.tmp`, no `gen_k.sav` | `.tmp` valid → promote it into the position and delete the `.bak`; invalid → promote the `.bak` back |
| Between `SWAPPING_IN` and `POST_VERIFY` done | `gen_k.sav` (new) and `gen_k.bak` (replaced oldest) | `gen_k.sav` verifies → delete the `.bak`; else promote the `.bak` |
| During `MANIFEST` | all generations fine; manifest stale or partial | Rebuild the manifest by scanning headers |

Candidates after recovery are every `*.sav` plus `*.bak` leftovers, deduplicated by header `generation` (preferring `.sav`); a `.tmp` is never a candidate except through the roll-forward row. The design assumes only that renaming a closed file to an *absent* name is all-or-nothing [UNV]; the ring (at least two verified generations during any swap, since `AUTOSAVE_GENERATIONS` ≥ 2) is the answer to a failed rename.

**T-7. `save_status` values.**

| Value | Entered | Exit | Consumer |
|---|---|---|---|
| `IDLE` | Boot | Any write starts | Chart Table pause panel, Debug Overlay (designed; `DIAGNOSTIC` P-STATE read); no HUD per-write consumer *(Amended 2026-10-26 — CD-GDD-ALIGN)* |
| `WRITING` | `CAPTURING` begins | `WRITTEN` or `FAILED` | Never drawn for an autosave. The pause panel may show a plain in-panel state only during the player's own Save & Quit (SL-6); diagnostics *(Amended 2026-10-26 — CD-GDD-ALIGN)* |
| `WRITTEN` | Swap and manifest complete | The next write begins (carries `written_at_T`) | Chart Table pause panel (the newest record's playtime, a SIM fact) and Debug Overlay; no HUD display; `STATUS_WRITTEN_HOLD_TICKS` is retired *(Amended 2026-10-26 — CD-GDD-ALIGN)* |
| `FAILED` | Any write-procedure failure | The next write begins | HUD notice on the non-diegetic suit panel, drawn only while `fail_escalated` (SL-21); diagnostics |

**T-8. Death-stub run (single player).** Pre-check, decode and staging run at the `DEAD` boundary, before the kernel is touched (SL-11); `LOADING` holds only the kernel's restore and the verification window.

| Step | Tick boundary | Actor | Result |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `DEAD` entered | Controller | Notifies at the end of the tick (the kernel's notify phase) |
| 2 | Same boundary | Save/Load | Wait for any in-flight write to reach `WRITTEN` or `FAILED` (SL-7; bounded by the writer's failure path for the rename step (`RENAME_RETRY_COUNT` × `RENAME_RETRY_DELAY_MS`); a write stuck in flush or re-read is unbounded in principle (F-5), is reachable only if a write that started inside the 1 800-tick `DOWNED` window has not settled, and is measured by the spike (AC-SL-30, AC-SL-88)), then epoch++ (the bump must not abort that write); `generation_select` (SL-8); pre-check (T-5) and staging, with per-class fallback to the next candidate. Their main-thread file I/O lands in this `DEAD` tick, where the Presenter holds the down sheet's last frame. No candidate passes → `SL_LOAD_NO_LOADABLE_SAVE`, the kernel is never touched (SL-7) |
| 3 | Same boundary | Save/Load | `request_restore(payload)` of the staged WORLD blocks, honoured at the tick's boundary services |
| 4 | The tick's boundary services | Kernel | `RUNNING → LOADING`; queue flushed, each command `Dropped(RELOAD)` |
| 5 | `LOADING` | Pipeline | `TRANSIT_HOLD`: layer-1 black, fade in 120 ms; zoom presses discarded |
| 6 | `LOADING` | Kernel | Restores every block in `restore_after` order, every `restore()` before any `post_restore()`; an owner-reported failure here is `FAULTED` (SL-22) |
| 7 | End of `LOADING` | Save/Load (K-4, provisional) | `restore_reason = DEATH_STUB` published; the epoch held; the verification window (dev/test; release diagnostic-only, SL-12); the kernel enters `RUNNING` |
| 8 | First `RUNNING` frame (`LOADING → RUNNING`) | Pipeline | Camera `CUT` at the pre-load `k_z`; the first frame already passes through the Perception Filter for the restored sanity (H4, routed, OQ-11) |

**T-9. Campaign directory states.**

| State | Entry | Exit | Transitions | Who may cause them |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `EMPTY` | `new_game` created the directory | The initial generation is `WRITTEN`; or world construction / baseline write fails | → `ACTIVE`; → removed (`SL_NEW_GAME_NO_BASELINE`) | `new_game` |
| `ACTIVE` | At least one verified generation | A newer-format header is observed; the user retires or deletes it | → `READ_ONLY`; → `RETIRED`; → deleted | Load pipeline; the user (hold-to-confirm) |
| `READ_ONLY` | An older build found `format_version` above its own in the newest header | A newer build opens it | The older build never writes into it | Load pipeline (SL-11) |
| `RETIRED` | The user started a new campaign over this one | The user deletes it | Renamed out of the active location, files intact | Chart Table (provisional) |

### Interactions with Other Systems

**What Save/Load exposes and consumes, in names fixed elsewhere.** It registers **no hooks, no mutators, no commands (the VS-B `saveload.request_save` is reserved and provisional), no blocks and no dirty keys** (SL-17), and it never writes a block (H3, H4). It calls the kernel's host-local control plane — `request_snapshot`, `request_restore`, `request_shutdown` (CR-15); `request_pause` / `request_resume` are the pause panel's, not its own. It reads `kernel.clock`, `controller.state.loco_state` per bound seat, the Graph's `failures()` read view and an optional Attention SIM view (SL-13). It publishes the host-local reads `save_status`, `SaveInfo` and `restore_reason` and the API of SL-21. All three rules for every consumer in the kernel's Interactions section hold: a system that wants state to persist registers a block and never talks to Save/Load directly. **Every interface with an undesigned or in-flight system below is provisional — it lists only what that system must supply or may rely on, never a guess of its internals.**

Each row quotes the dependency GDD's own Interactions row so the two documents can be checked against each other (bidirectional consistency).

|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Command & Simulation Layer** (hard; designed) | `kernel.clock` (`T`, `world_seed`); per-block snapshots and versions at capture; `state_hash(PERSIST, T)` for the header and verification (D.12, cited); capture time against `SNAPSHOT_BUDGET_MS` (cited); `MAX_BLOCKS` (cited) | Control-plane calls only; WORLD blocks (PROFILE on a cold open) to restore; the registration expectations `supported_versions` and `added_in_format` | Kernel: mechanism (snapshot, hash, restore order, `FAULTED`) · Save/Load: file, header, ring, pre-check, container migration, orchestration of owner migrations | CR-8 registry fields, `restore_after`, `post_restore`, CR-15, CR-18; the kernel's edge cases for missing block (`default_state()`), unknown block (`RESTORE_UNKNOWN_BLOCK`), version mismatch, `TICK_RATE_MISMATCH`, restore during `SAVING`; "in `FAULTED` the kernel never writes or autosaves over an existing save" |
| **Life-Support Graph** (block owner; designed) | `graph.state` snapshot: `SIM`, persist `WORLD`, `scope` `SHARED`, `repl_hint` `SHARED_SUMMARY`, `schema_version` 1, `restore_after: [layout.state]`; zero floats; packed arrays; `canonical_bytes()` (LS-21) | `restore(data, stored_version)` (supports exactly version 1 in the MVP), `post_restore()` | Graph: shape, two-phase restore, the closed `GRAPH_RESTORE_*` reasons (BAD_VERSION, BAD_TAG, BAD_LENGTH, BAD_ENUM, OUT_OF_RANGE, ID_ORDER, COUNTER, BAD_EDGE_ENDPOINT, SEAM_MISMATCH, UNKNOWN_DEF, UNKNOWN_ROOM, INVARIANT) · Save/Load: S-1, the golden-save convention, the harness | S-1; Save/Load never reorders `restore_after` (the kernel's topological order does); Graph cost: snapshot ≤ 0.5 ms and ≤ 32 KB, restore + `post_restore` ≤ 2 ms (cited from the Graph's performance criterion); the soft-gate read: `read_view.failures()` descriptor fields `cause`, `sev`, `warn_elapsed` (LS-14, LS-19) and the constants `W_MIN_STEPS` and `GRAPH_STEP_TICKS` (D.11; cited, never restated) *(Amended 2026-10-26 — CD-GDD-ALIGN)* |

The kernel's own Interactions rows for the first five (rows 4, 5, 8, 10, 13) do not mention Save/Load except through the registry; they are consistent with the table above. **Edge type.** The index lists Save/Load & Versioned Format as a dependency of Suit & Equipment, Modular Ship Assembly, Web Map & Transit, Attention and Sanity (index rows I:82, I:85, I:88, I:89, I:91). One position: each such edge is a **design-time dependency on this GDD's contracts** — the block authoring contract (SL-15), the PROFILE/WORLD scope rule (SL-9), the death-stub semantics (SL-7) and the event/notification contract (SL-4) — and **not a runtime call**: none of them ever calls Save/Load (the consumer rule), which is the sense of the kernel's note that a Graph → Save/Load edge is redundant. The Graph and the Controller were designed before this GDD, so a design-time edge from them is moot and the Graph's S-1 obligation is carried here (SL-2) instead; for the systems not yet designed, the edge is real and is labelled **"design-time (contract)"**. Routed to /map-systems as a label clarification plus a uniformity note — either every undesigned block owner carries the labelled edge or the kernel's registry edge is treated as transitive, the index's call (OQ-15). The ruling-stage suggestion that the index rows for Web Map, Attention and Sanity are wrong is withdrawn: those systems' blocks are written to SL-15, so the edge is correct and only its label was ambiguous.

## Formulas

All sim-time quantities are **integer ticks** read from `kernel.clock.T` and converted to seconds only in examples, at `TICK_RATE` 60 (registry, cited). Anything hashed or stored in a header is an integer or an exact-width hex string (S-1); a millisecond budget below is a float used only for logging thresholds, is never hashed or replicated, and so is outside the kernel's determinism rule. **Cited, never restated:** `state_hash` (kernel D.12, registry), `rng_derive` (kernel D.10, registry), `snapshot_bytes` and `capture_time_ms` (kernel D.11), `SNAPSHOT_BUDGET_MS` and `MAX_BLOCKS` (kernel GDD values, not registry entries — kernel registry candidates), `DOWN_TO_DEATH_TICKS` (registry, Controller), `W_MIN_WARNING_S` (registry, Graph). Owned constants are named in Tuning Knobs. Ten formulas follow; the first six are the registry candidates of the rulings (R29, with `generation_select` and `save_verification` amended at the stage-5 checkpoint), the last four are derived bounds and encodings the criteria need.

### F-1. `autosave_due`

The `autosave_due` formula is defined as:

`elapsed = T − last_save_T; interval_due = elapsed ≥ AUTOSAVE_INTERVAL_TICKS; event_due = event_pending AND elapsed ≥ AUTOSAVE_MIN_GAP_TICKS; due = interval_due OR event_due; kind = EVENT if event_due else AUTO`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `T` | int | 0 … 2^63 − 1 | `kernel.clock` (read at the tick boundary) | Current sim tick |
| `last_save_T` | int | 0 … `T` | Save/Load session memory: the capture `T` of the newest durable generation; reset to the restored generation's `T` on every restore | Basis of the loss window; unchanged by a failed write, so a failed save stays due |
| `event_pending` | bool | {0, 1} | Owner notifications and pure reads (SL-4): `POD_ARRIVED` (`web_events`), `last_shape_change_T()` (Assembly), Interaction's `last_salvage_T()` (BS-21) — `POD_ARRIVED` provisional until merge; coalesced; cleared when a capture at or after the event's tick is `WRITTEN` | At least one autosave-worthy event (pod-hop arrival, weld or cut completion, dock, section extraction) since the last write |
| `AUTOSAVE_INTERVAL_TICKS` ★ | int | 3 600 … 36 000, default 10 800 | Tuning Knobs | Interval trigger (3 min) |
| `AUTOSAVE_MIN_GAP_TICKS` ★ | int | 900 … 5 400, default 1 800 | Tuning Knobs | Floor between any two `AUTO`/`EVENT` generations; strictly below the interval |
| `elapsed` | int | 0 … unbounded | calculated | Sim ticks since the last durable capture |
| `due`, `kind` | bool, enum | {0, 1}; {`AUTO`, `EVENT`} | calculated | Input to F-2; `kind` is stored in the header and `SaveInfo` (an event and the interval due on the same tick produce **one** save, kind `EVENT`) |

**Output Range:** boolean `due` plus `kind`. `T < last_save_T` cannot occur after the reset-on-restore rule; if observed, `elapsed := 0`, `last_save_T := T` and `SL_AUTOSAVE_CLOCK_REGRESSION` is logged once. No interval save is ever due while the kernel is `PAUSED` (`T` cannot advance). The `QUIT` kind is a separate path (SL-6, F-2) and never passes through `due`.

**Example:** `last_save_T = 432 000` (the anchor moment, Player Fantasy). A weld completes at `T = 437 400`: `elapsed = 5 400 ≥ 1 800`, so `event_due`, `due = 1`, `kind = EVENT`. The next interval save is then due at `437 400 + 10 800 = 448 200`. Latch case: a second event at `T = 438 700` has `elapsed = 1 300 < 1 800`, so `event_pending` stays set and `due` becomes true at `T = 439 200`.

### F-2. `autosave_gate`

The `autosave_gate` formula is defined as:

`hard_block = (EXISTS seat IN bound_seats: loco_state(seat) IN {DOWNED, DEAD}) OR kernel_state NOT IN {RUNNING, PAUSED} OR writer_busy OR restore_pending; graph_tell_block = EXISTS d IN graph.failures(): d.subject_kind IN {NODE, EDGE, SEAM} AND d.cause == ENDOGENOUS AND d.sev ≥ 1 AND d.warn_elapsed < W_MIN_STEPS; attention_tell_block = tell_active AND (gate_tell_mode == STRICT OR tell_remaining < W_MIN_WARNING_S × TICK_RATE); tell_block = graph_tell_block OR attention_tell_block; soft_block = tell_block OR breach_phase ≠ CALM OR (EXISTS seat: loco_state(seat) IN GATE_SOFT_STATES); waited = T − due_first_T; fire = due AND waited mod AUTOSAVE_RETRY_TICKS == 0 AND NOT hard_block AND (NOT soft_block OR waited ≥ AUTOSAVE_MAX_DEFER_TICKS); risky = fire AND soft_block; quit_write = kernel_state IN {RUNNING, PAUSED} AND NOT (EXISTS seat: loco_state(seat) IN {DOWNED, DEAD}); quit_risky = quit_write AND soft_block`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `loco_state(seat)` | enum | the nine Controller states (`IDLE`, `WALK`, `RUN`, `CARRY_WALK`, `CHANNELLING`, `STAGGERED`, `DOWNED`, `DEAD`, `IN_POD`) | `controller.state` read per bound seat (SL-13) | `DOWNED` and `DEAD` are **hard** and never in `GATE_SOFT_STATES` |
| `kernel_state` | enum | the seven kernel states | kernel (read) | Capture is valid only in `RUNNING` or `PAUSED` |
| `writer_busy`, `restore_pending` | bool | {0, 1} | Save/Load host-local state | One write in flight at a time; never capture into a world about to be replaced |
| `graph.failures()` descriptors `d` (`subject_kind`, `cause`, `sev`, `warn_elapsed`) | enum, enum, 0 … 4, int steps | `subject_kind` {NODE 1, EDGE 2, COMPARTMENT 3, SEAM 4, OPENING 5}; `cause` {ENDOGENOUS, EXOGENOUS}; `sev` 0 … 4; `warn_elapsed` ≥ 0 | Life-Support Graph `read_view.failures()` (LS-19, LS-14; designed and locked) | *(Amended 2026-10-26 — CD-GDD-ALIGN)* The Graph's published warning state. Only loaded NODE, EDGE and SEAM descriptors (COMPARTMENT and OPENING descriptors carry no `warn_since_step`, so `warn_elapsed` is 0 and counting them would defer on every cold or thin room), endogenous, with `sev` ≥ 1 and a closed fairness gate, count; WORN counts; exogenous causes bypass the Graph's gate and owe no Graph-side warning. The Graph's own window, never restated here |
| `W_MIN_STEPS`, `GRAPH_STEP_TICKS` | int steps, int ticks | 100 at the defaults (`W_MIN_WARNING_S` × `TICK_RATE` / `GRAPH_STEP_TICKS`); 6 | Graph D.11 (cited) | `d.warn_elapsed < W_MIN_STEPS` is "the gate is closed"; `graph_tell_remaining = MIN over the counted d of (W_MIN_STEPS − d.warn_elapsed) × GRAPH_STEP_TICKS`, 0 … `W_MIN_WARNING_S × TICK_RATE` ticks, is a diagnostic and test output and does not enter `fire` |
| `tell_active`, `tell_remaining` | bool, int ticks | {0, 1}; 0 … `TELL_MAX_TICKS` (1 800, Attention's, cited) | Attention SIM read view `attention.view` (designed) | Remaining warning of the soonest active **Attention** tell; an absent view counts as open and logs `GATE_INPUT_ABSENT` once per process (the Graph half is unaffected) |
| `gate_tell_mode` | enum | {`FAIR`, `STRICT`} | Tuning Knobs | Governs the **Attention half only**: `FAIR` defers only while the remaining warning is below the Graph's guarantee; `STRICT` defers for the whole tell. For the Graph half the two collapse into one behaviour, defer for the whole open window, because the window is the guarantee |
| `W_MIN_WARNING_S` | int s | 10 (registry, range 4 … 20) | Graph (cited) | The fairness minimum; `W_MIN_WARNING_S × TICK_RATE` = 600 ticks at the defaults |
| `breach_phase` | enum | {`CALM`, `BREACH`, `SUBSIDING`} (a TELL reads `CALM`) | `attention.view` (designed) | Only `CALM` opens the soft gate |
| `GATE_SOFT_STATES` | set | subset of {`CHANNELLING`, `STAGGERED`, `IN_POD`} | Tuning Knobs | Restores drop channel progress and turn `STAGGERED` into `IDLE` (PC-17); a mid-transit state is Web Map's to restore |
| `due_first_T` | int | 0 … `T` | session memory: the first tick `due` was true since the last fire | Anchor of the deferral clock and the retry grid; **not** reset by gate flicker |
| `AUTOSAVE_RETRY_TICKS` ★, `AUTOSAVE_MAX_DEFER_TICKS` ★ | int | 60 … 900, default 300; 1 800 … (`AUTOSAVE_INTERVAL_TICKS` − `AUTOSAVE_RETRY_TICKS`), default 7 200 | Tuning Knobs | Re-evaluation period and the cap on **soft** deferral; `AUTOSAVE_MAX_DEFER_TICKS mod AUTOSAVE_RETRY_TICKS = 0` so the cap lands on the retry grid |
| `fire`, `risky`, `quit_write`, `quit_risky` | bool | {0, 1} | calculated | `fire` requests `request_snapshot` at this boundary; `risky` is stored in the header; `quit_write = 0` makes `save_and_quit` return `SL_QUIT_NO_WRITE_DOWNED` (SL-6) |

**Output Range:** booleans. A **hard** condition is never overridden by the cap or by a quit: a `DOWNED` or `DEAD` world is never written, so no generation file ever decodes to such a seat. A hard block never expires (a revived crew reopens the gate); only soft conditions are bounded, by `AUTOSAVE_MAX_DEFER_TICKS`. An interval save is never taken while an endogenous Graph warning window is open and the cap has not elapsed, so no unforced generation holds a Graph tell with fewer than `W_MIN_WARNING_S` of it elapsed *(Amended 2026-10-26 — CD-GDD-ALIGN)*. A due save is postponed at most `AUTOSAVE_MAX_DEFER_TICKS` beyond `due_first_T` unless a hard condition holds.

**Example (`FAIR`):** `last_save_T = 0`, `due_first_T = 10 800`. A tell is active with 700 ticks remaining: `700 ≥ 600`, so `tell_block = 0`; the crew is `WALK`, `breach_phase = CALM`; `fire` at `T = 10 800`, kind `AUTO`, not risky. The same state under `STRICT` is blocked at 10 800, 11 100 and 11 400 and fires at 11 700 when the tell ends. **Graph example** *(Amended 2026-10-26 — CD-GDD-ALIGN)*: `W_MIN_STEPS` = 10 × 60 / 6 = 100. A cable descriptor (`subject_kind` EDGE, `cause` ENDOGENOUS, `sev` 1) has `warn_elapsed` = 47 when the interval save falls due at `T = 10 800`: `graph_tell_block = 1`, `graph_tell_remaining = (100 − 47) × 6 = 318` ticks, no fire. At the next retry tick `T = 11 100` the 300 ticks are 50 steps, so `warn_elapsed` = 97 and it is still blocked; at `T = 11 400` it is 147, the window has closed, and the save fires, kind `AUTO`, not risky. A restore from a save taken at 11 400 therefore never lands inside the first 10 s of that tell. **Cap example:** a soft block from 10 800 onward fires at `T = 18 000` (`waited = 7 200`) with `risky = 1`. **Hard example:** the crew goes `DOWNED` at 17 900: at `T = 18 000` the cap has elapsed but `hard_block = 1`, so nothing is written; `DEAD` follows at 19 700 and the stub restores the generation from `last_save_T = 0`.

### F-3. `generation_select`

The `generation_select` formula is defined as:

`C = [g_0, g_1, …] (non-demoted candidates that pass the pre-check, by header generation descending); loop_hit = g_r ≠ none AND g_r.risky AND g_r == g_0 AND (T_downed − g_r.T) ≤ DEATH_LOOP_WINDOW_TICKS; selected = g_1 if (loop_hit AND |C| ≥ 2) else g_0; demote = g_r if (loop_hit AND |C| ≥ 2) else none`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `C` | list | 0 … `AUTOSAVE_GENERATIONS` (plus transient `.bak` leftovers) | header scan after the pre-check (SL-11) | Candidates; a second pass over demoted generations (newest first) runs only if `C` is empty |
| `g_r` | generation or none | — | session memory: the generation the session was last restored from; none on a campaign with no restore yet | The last restored generation |
| `g.risky`, `g.T` | bool, int | {0, 1}; 0 … 2^63 − 1 | header | `RISKY` tag from F-2; capture tick (a restore returns `T` to `g.T`) |
| `T_downed` | int | `g_r.T` … `T_dead` | start tick of the `DOWNED` episode that ended in `DEAD`, observed through the `loco_state` read (SL-13) | Bounds the player's *acting* time after the restore |
| `DEATH_LOOP_WINDOW_TICKS` ★ | int | 1 800 … 10 800, default 3 600 | Tuning Knobs | The window |
| `g_r == g_0` | bool | {0, 1} | calculated | The loop test: if a newer non-demoted verified generation exists, the gate opened after the restore and the player got out; there is no loop |
| `selected`, `demote` | generation or none | `C` or none | calculated | `selected = none` only if `C` is empty and the second pass is empty: `SL_LOAD_NO_LOADABLE_SAVE` |

**Output Range:** `g_0`, `g_1` or none. The result is always the newest non-demoted generation that passes the pre-check, except when `loop_hit` forces `g_1`. A load makes at most `LOAD_MAX_ATTEMPTS` (default `AUTOSAVE_GENERATIONS`) pre-check attempts. If `loop_hit` and `|C| = 1`, the only candidate is restored anyway and `SL_STEPBACK_NONE_AVAILABLE` is logged. A reproduction mismatch (SL-12) is never an input to this formula.

**Example:** ring G7 (`T = 40 000`, `RISKY`), G6 (`T = 29 000`), G5 (`T = 18 000`); the stub restores G7, so `g_r = G7`. The crew is `DOWNED` at `T = 41 200` and `DEAD` at 43 000: `T_downed − g_r.T = 1 200 ≤ 3 600`, `g_r == g_0`, so `loop_hit`; G6 is selected and G7 is renamed `gen_k.demoted`. A second death before any new autosave selects G6 again, never G7 (it is demoted). Variants: `T_downed = 44 000` gives `4 000 > 3 600`, no loop, G7 is restored again; G7 untagged gives no loop at any `T_downed`, G7 is restored every time (the user rule).

### F-4. `save_verification`

The `save_verification` formula is defined as:

`file_ok = SHA256(bytes[0 : size − 32]) == bytes[size − 32 : size] AND all(payload_sha256 match) AND structure_ok; causes = {MIGRATED, RETIRED_STRIPPED, DEFAULT_FILLED, ALGO_UNSUPPORTED} ∩ observed ∪ {REBASE_BLOCK if REBASE_BLOCKS ∩ blocks_in_file ≠ ∅}; mode = EXACT if causes = ∅ else REBASED; window_ok = (kernel.clock.T == header.T) at compare time; verdict = NONE if NOT window_ok, else REBASED_RECORDED if mode == REBASED, else MATCH if state_hash(PERSIST, T) == header.state_hash, else NOT_REPRODUCED`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `bytes`, `size` | bytes, int | 46 … `MAX_SAVE_BYTES` | the candidate file | The whole generation; the last 32 bytes are the trailer (SL-1 format law) |
| `payload_sha256` | 64 hex chars | exact width | header block table | Per-block transport hash, Save/Load's own, native [UNV] |
| `structure_ok` | bool | {0, 1} | pre-check steps (T-5) | Block table contiguous, lengths sum to the payload region |
| `causes` | set | the closed set of five | load pipeline | The five REBASED causes of SL-12: migration ran, retired block stripped, `default_state()` filled, `hash_algo` unsupported, a `REBASE_BLOCKS` member present |
| `REBASE_BLOCKS` ★ | list of `block_id` | default `[controller.state]` (provisional — Controller answered (PC-17 normalises in `snapshot()`); emptied by Save/Load when AC-SL-67 passes; `body.state` left it with BDY-31) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass)* | Tuning Knobs | Interim, known kernel-contract debt (OQ-9) |
| `state_hash(PERSIST, T)` | u64 | 0 … 2^64 − 1 | kernel D.12 (cited, never restated), recomputed over the restored WORLD blocks | The reproduction primitive; `hash_algo` names the 64-bit hash |
| `header.T`, `header.state_hash` | hex u64 | 16 lowercase hex chars | header (S-1) | Capture tick and capture-time hash |
| `verdict` | enum | {`NONE`, `REBASED_RECORDED`, `MATCH`, `NOT_REPRODUCED`} | calculated | `NONE` logs `SL_VERIFY_WINDOW_MISSED` and records nothing; `REBASED_RECORDED` stores the recomputed value as the baseline in `campaign.json` only and logs `SL_VERIFY_REBASED{cause}`; `NOT_REPRODUCED` logs `SL_VERIFY_NOT_REPRODUCED{blocks}` |

**Output Range:** `file_ok` boolean; `verdict` one of four values. `file_ok = 0` is decided before the kernel is touched and sends the loader to the next candidate (class CORRUPT). `NOT_REPRODUCED` never selects another candidate: release marks the generation `suspect` and plays on, dev and test halt on the first occurrence. Undetected accidental damage: `2^−256` at the file stage and `2^−64` at the reproduction stage (the width of D.12's hash). In `REBASED` mode the reproduction stage is vacuous by construction and protection is the file stage plus each owner's closed `<BLOCK>_RESTORE_*` reasons.

**Example:** a 63 284-byte file (F-6) with `REBASE_BLOCKS` empty, no migration: the trailer equals SHA-256 of the first 63 252 bytes (`file_ok`), `mode = EXACT`, `kernel.clock.T = header.T = 437 400`, the recomputed hash `0x9f1c2b770a54e3d1` equals the header's (illustrative): `MATCH`. The same file with the default `REBASE_BLOCKS` holds `controller.state`, so `causes = {REBASE_BLOCK}`: `REBASED_RECORDED`, no failure. A build whose Graph migrates v1 to v2: `causes = {MIGRATED}`, `REBASED_RECORDED`, and the loader does not walk the ring. A flipped bit in the Graph payload: `file_ok = 0` at the `payload_sha256` step, the kernel is never touched, the next candidate is tried.

### F-5. `write_budget`

The `write_budget` formula is defined as:

`main_added_ms = t_encode + t_handoff; main_hitch_ms = capture_time_ms + main_added_ms; worker_ms = t_sha + t_write + t_flush + t_reread + t_resha + t_swap + t_manifest; durable_ms = main_hitch_ms + worker_ms; checks: t_handoff ≤ WRITE_HANDOFF_MAX_FRACTION × SNAPSHOT_BUDGET_MS, t_encode ≤ SAVE_ENCODE_BUDGET_MS, durable_ms ≤ WRITE_DURABLE_ALARM_MS, worker_ms ≤ DOWN_TO_DEATH_TICKS × 1000 / TICK_RATE`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `capture_time_ms` | float ms | within `SNAPSHOT_BUDGET_MS` (kernel GDD value 16.7, logging threshold only) | kernel D.11 (cited; Save/Load neither restates nor adds to it) | Σ `t_snapshot(b)` + `t_hash` inside `SAVING`; with the stored `state_hash`, the hash is a release cost on every capture (OQ-17) |
| `t_encode`, `t_handoff` | float ms | 0 … ~10 [UNV]; 0 … ~2 | Save/Load, main thread, tick halted | Encoding the captured plain data and assembling the header into one immutable byte value; moving it to the worker |
| `t_sha`, `t_resha` | float ms | ~0.3 … 2 at 60–300 KiB [UNV] | worker, native hashing | File integrity at write and at the verify re-read |
| `t_write`, `t_flush`, `t_reread` | float ms | 2 … 100 (AV and indexers add 50 … 500) [UNV] | worker | Disk dependent |
| `t_swap`, `t_manifest` | float ms | 1 … 20 [UNV] | worker | The two renames of the swap, `POST_VERIFY`, and the manifest rewrite |
| `WRITE_HANDOFF_MAX_FRACTION` | float | 0.01 … 0.2, default 0.05 | Tuning Knobs | Share of `SNAPSHOT_BUDGET_MS` the hand-off may take |
| `SAVE_ENCODE_BUDGET_MS`, `WRITE_DURABLE_ALARM_MS` | float ms | 2 … 16, default 4; 200 … 2 000, default 500 | Tuning Knobs | Logging thresholds, never truncating |
| `DOWN_TO_DEATH_TICKS` | int | 1 800 (registry, cited) | Controller | A write that settles inside the `DOWNED` window cannot race a death |

**Output Range:** `main_added_ms` is the only quantity the player can feel as a stutter, and it adds to — it is not part of — the kernel's capture; the target is that the sum stays inside one frame at 60 Hz. `worker_ms` is unbounded in principle (stuck I/O) and bounded in effect by the writer-busy hard block (F-2) and the stub's wait (SL-7). The 30 000 ms ceiling is `1 800 × 1 000 / 60`. All components are [UNV] estimates the spike replaces; every threshold logs and none truncates.

**Example (MVP slice: 8 blocks, 61 440 B payload, SSD):** `capture_time_ms = 3.4` (Graph 0.5 + seven small blocks at 0.2 = 1.9 of snapshots, plus a 1.5 hash), `t_encode = 1.5`, `t_handoff = 0.3` (≤ 0.05 × 16.7 = 0.835 ✓): `main_hitch_ms = 5.2`. `worker_ms = 0.3 + 3 + 4 + 1 + 0.3 + 4 + 3 = 15.6`; `durable_ms = 20.8 ≤ 500` ✓. A slow HDD with a scanner: `worker_ms = 300`, `durable_ms = 305.2` ✓, slack to the death floor `30 000 − 300 = 29 700 ms`.

### F-6. `save_bytes_header`

The `save_bytes_header` formula is defined as:

`header_len = H0 + B × h_b; file_bytes = 8 + 4 + header_len + Σ P_b + 32; alarm = file_bytes > SAVE_SIZE_ALARM_BYTES; refuse = file_bytes > MAX_SAVE_BYTES OR (EXISTS b: P_b > MAX_BLOCK_BYTES) OR header_len > MAX_HEADER_BYTES`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 8, 4, 32 | int B | fixed | the container (SL-1) | Magic with layout id, `u32 header_len`, the SHA-256 trailer |
| `H0` | int B | ~600 (500 … 700) | measured at the spike; the fixed header keys of SL-1 (versions, hex fields, `campaign_id`, `SaveInfo` inputs) | Fixed header size |
| `h_b` | int B | ~150 (100 … 180) | measured | One block-table record (`block_id`, `schema_version`, `persist_scope`, `offset`, `length`, 64-hex `payload_sha256`, optional hex `block_state_hash`) |
| `B` | int | 1 … `MAX_BLOCKS` (kernel GDD value 128, cited) | registry | Blocks in the file |
| `P_b` | int B | ≤ `MAX_BLOCK_BYTES` | the encoded payload | Not the kernel's canonical byte length (`snapshot_bytes`, D.11, cited): the two differ by encoding overhead (≈ 1.0 for packed arrays, 1.0 … 1.6 for dictionary blocks [UNV]) |
| `MAX_SAVE_BYTES` ★, `MAX_BLOCK_BYTES` ★, `MAX_HEADER_BYTES` ★, `SAVE_SIZE_ALARM_BYTES` ★ | int B | defaults 64 MiB, 32 MiB, 64 KiB, 2 MiB | Tuning Knobs | The trust caps and the diagnostics alarm |

**Output Range:** `file_bytes` from ≈ 0.8 KB (only `kernel.clock`, `B = 1`) up to `MAX_SAVE_BYTES`; the MVP expectation is the kernel's 100 … 300 KiB payload (D.11, provisional) plus ≈ 2 KiB of overhead. At `B = 128` the header is `600 + 128 × 150 = 19 800 B` (19.3 KiB), a third of `MAX_HEADER_BYTES`. **`refuse` is a write-side rule:** Save/Load never produces a file its own read side would refuse (`SL_WRITE_TOO_LARGE{block}`, the ring intact, play continues). `alarm` is a diagnostics entry (`SL_SIZE_ALARM`, once per campaign open), not a failure.

**Example:** `B = 8`: Graph 32 768 B (the Graph's cost ceiling, cited) plus seven blocks of 4 096 B = 61 440 B of payload; `header_len = 600 + 8 × 150 = 1 800`; `file_bytes = 8 + 4 + 1 800 + 61 440 + 32 = 63 284 B` (61.8 KiB), header share 2.8 %, far below the 2 MiB alarm. The payload bytes are raw (no base64 inflation; base64 exists only in the dev dump, S-1).

### F-7. `loss_ticks`

The `loss_ticks` formula is defined as:

`loss_ticks = T_dead − T_g; loss_min = DOWN_TO_DEATH_TICKS + 1; loss_max = AUTOSAVE_INTERVAL_TICKS + AUTOSAVE_MAX_DEFER_TICKS + DOWN_TO_DEATH_TICKS; loss_max_stepback = AUTOSAVE_INTERVAL_TICKS + AUTOSAVE_MAX_DEFER_TICKS + DEATH_LOOP_WINDOW_TICKS + DOWN_TO_DEATH_TICKS; loss_mean ≈ AUTOSAVE_INTERVAL_TICKS / 2 + DOWN_TO_DEATH_TICKS + E[defer]`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `T_dead` | int | 0 … 2^63 − 1 | `kernel.clock` at the `DEAD` tick | Death tick |
| `T_g` | int | 0 … `T_dead` | header `T` of the restored generation | Capture tick of the generation the stub restores; the sim time the death discards is `T_dead − T_g` |
| `AUTOSAVE_INTERVAL_TICKS` ★, `AUTOSAVE_MAX_DEFER_TICKS` ★, `DEATH_LOOP_WINDOW_TICKS` ★ | int | knob ranges | Tuning Knobs | Interval, soft-defer cap, death-loop window |
| `DOWN_TO_DEATH_TICKS` | int | 1 800 (registry, owner range 600 … 3 600, cited) | Controller | `DOWNED` to `DEAD` |
| `E[defer]` | float ticks | 0 … `AUTOSAVE_MAX_DEFER_TICKS` | the offline simulation of the systems-designer's plan (a playtest output) | Mean wait between `due_first_T` and the fire |

**Output Range:** `loss_ticks` lies in `[1 801, 19 800]` at the defaults when no event trigger fires (events can only lower it) and in `[1 801, 23 400]` after one conditional step-back. **Derivation of the floor:** the gate hard-blocks capture while any seat is `DOWNED`, and `DEAD` needs `DOWN_TO_DEATH_TICKS` after `T_downed`; the latest possible capture is the boundary of tick `T_downed − 1`, so `loss_ticks ≥ 1 800 + 1`. **Ceiling:** the last durable generation is at `T0`; the next save is due at `T0 + I`; a continuous soft block postpones it to the cap at `T0 + I + D`; the crew enters `DOWNED` on that very tick (a hard block, so the cap does not fire); `DEAD` follows after 1 800 ticks. **Step-back ceiling:** a `RISKY` generation written at the cap is restored, the crew survives for the whole window and goes `DOWNED` at `T(g_r) + W`, dies 1 800 ticks later, and the predecessor at `T0` is restored: `(T0 + I + D + W + 1 800) − T0`. The two coincidences in the defaults (`AUTOSAVE_MIN_GAP_TICKS` = `DOWN_TO_DEATH_TICKS` = 1 800) are coincidences, not derivations, and neither may be computed from the other. At the corners of the safe ranges (`I = 36 000`, `D = 35 700`) `loss_max` reaches 73 500 ticks (20.4 min): the diagnostics constant `LOSS_CEILING_TICKS` (Tuning Knobs) flags any configuration whose `loss_max_stepback` exceeds it.

**Example (defaults, 60 Hz):** `loss_min = 1 801` (30.0 s); `loss_max = 10 800 + 7 200 + 1 800 = 19 800` (5.5 min); `loss_max_stepback = 10 800 + 7 200 + 3 600 + 1 800 = 23 400` (6.5 min); `loss_mean = 5 400 + 1 800 + 0 = 7 200` (120 s). The anchor moment: generation `T_g = 437 400`, `T_dead = 448 200`, `loss_ticks = 10 800` (180 s), inside the range.

### F-8. `load_budget`

The `load_budget` formula is defined as:

`precheck_ms = t_read + t_sha + t_hdr + t_decode + t_walk; loading_ms = Σ_b (t_restore(b) + t_post(b)) + t_verify; restart_ms = LOAD_ATTEMPTS × precheck_ms + max(loading_ms, t_hold_pad); require restart_ms ≤ LOADING_TARGET_MS`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `t_read` | float ms | 1 … 100 [UNV] | disk | Read one candidate file |
| `t_sha` | float ms | 0.3 … 2 [UNV] | native hashing | Trailer SHA-256 and the per-block hashes |
| `t_hdr`, `t_decode`, `t_walk` | float ms | 0.1 … 1; 1 … 10; 0.1 … 2 [UNV] | Save/Load, main thread | Header parse, payload decode with objects disallowed, the bounded type walk (SL-2) |
| `t_restore(b)`, `t_post(b)` | float ms | Graph ≤ 2 combined (the Graph's cost criterion, cited); others 0.1 … 1 | kernel `LOADING`, owner diagnostics | Per-block restore and `post_restore` |
| `t_verify` | float ms | 1.5 (D.11 example) … 20+ [UNV] | kernel D.12 | The reproduction compare, dev/test and the guarded release case (SL-12) |
| `t_hold_pad` | float ms | ≥ 120 | Pipeline (cited: the `TRANSIT_HOLD` fade) | The black hold; load work hides inside it |
| `LOAD_ATTEMPTS` | int | 1 … `LOAD_MAX_ATTEMPTS` (default `AUTOSAVE_GENERATIONS`) | calculated | Candidates whose pre-check ran; the kernel restore happens once, because an owner-reported failure inside `LOADING` is `FAULTED` and not retried in line |
| `LOADING_TARGET_MS` ★ | int ms | 500 … 2 000, default 1 000 (provisional) | Tuning Knobs | A logging threshold, never truncating |

**Output Range:** `restart_ms` is the time the player perceives from `DEAD` (or Continue) to control. For the death stub `precheck_ms` is a hitch inside the `DEAD` tick, where the Presenter holds the down sheet's last frame (SL-11), and `max(loading_ms, t_hold_pad)` is the black hold; in practice the hold dominates. A black hold under 120 ms reads as a flash and over about 2 s as a crash (Game Feel).

**Example:** read 3, SHA 0.5, header 0.2, decode 4, walk 1 → `precheck_ms = 8.7`; Graph restore + post 2, seven other blocks 7 × 0.2, hash 1.5 → `loading_ms = 4.9`; `t_hold_pad = 120`: `restart_ms = 1 × 8.7 + max(4.9, 120) = 128.7 ms` ≤ 1 000 ✓. Worst case, two candidates fail the pre-check before the third passes: `3 × 8.7 + 120 = 146.1 ms`. If an in-script 64-bit hash costs 20 ms [UNV], `loading_ms = 23.4` and the hold still dominates.

### F-9. `ring_position`

The `ring_position` formula is defined as:

`gen_next = 1 + max(generation over the ring, quarantine/, the .demoted files and campaign.json); pos = min{p IN 0 … AUTOSAVE_GENERATIONS − 1 : slot p holds no non-demoted verified generation} if that set is non-empty, else argmin_p generation(slot p)`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `generation` | int | 1 … 2^53 − 1 | header (a JSON number, below 2^53) | Strictly increasing per campaign; the ordering truth, never `T`, never the file name, never modification time |
| `AUTOSAVE_GENERATIONS` ★ | int | 2 … 5, default 3 | Tuning Knobs | Ring size `N` |
| slot `p` | file | `gen_p.sav`, `gen_p.demoted` or empty | the campaign directory | A "free" slot is missing, `.demoted` or quarantined |

**Output Range:** `gen_next` is monotonic over the life of the campaign even after step-backs (which roll `T` back); `pos` is in `[0, N − 1]` and the newest generation's file is never renamed or touched, so a window with no newest file cannot exist.

**Example:** `N = 3`; `gen_0.sav` = G7, `gen_1.demoted` = G6 (stepped back from), `gen_2.sav` = G5, and `quarantine/` holds G9 (`STALE_EPOCH`). `gen_next = 1 + max(7, 6, 5, 9) = 10`; slot 1 is the first free position, so G10 is written to `gen_1.sav` and the `.demoted` occupant is renamed `.bak` and deleted after `POST_VERIFY` (T-4).

### F-10. `hex64`

The `hex64` formula is defined as:

`hex64(x) = lowercase hexadecimal of the 8 bytes of x (two's complement, most significant byte first), exactly 16 characters; unhex64(h) accepts only 16 characters of [0-9a-f] and returns the same 8 bytes`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `x` | 64-bit value | 0 … 2^64 − 1 (a signed GDScript int above 2^63 − 1 reads negative [UNV]) | `T`, `world_seed`, `state_hash`, per-block hashes | Any header value that can exceed 2^53 (S-1) |
| `h` | string | exactly 16 chars (32 for the 128-bit `campaign_id`) | header | Uppercase, a wrong width or a non-hex character is a refusal (`SL_PRECHECK_HEADER_HEX_MALFORMED`); there is no general string-to-int parse |

**Output Range:** a bijection between 8-byte values and 16-character strings; `unhex64(hex64(x)) = x` bit for bit for every `x`, including values a float cannot hold.

**Example:** `world_seed = 2^64 − 1` → `"ffffffffffffffff"` (the signed value is −1 [UNV]); `T = 2^53 + 1 = 9 007 199 254 740 993` → `"0020000000000001"`; `"FFFFFFFFFFFFFFFF"` and `"ffffffffffffffff0"` are refused.

## Edge Cases

Each case names the exact outcome and, where one exists, the diagnostic id. Diagnostics (`SL_*`) are log and `save_status` entries; they are not a second refusal set — the closed refusal set is `SL_PRECHECK_*` (T-5) and the boot set is `SL_BOOT_*` (SL-15). Cases are grouped by theme; a rule id in parentheses names where the behaviour is defined. **Reading rule for every case:** the kernel is never touched by a file the pre-check refuses, and the previous generations are never changed by a failed write.

### Zero, maximum and out-of-range values

- **If `world_seed` is 2^63 or above, or `T` exceeds 2^53**: the header stores each as exactly 16 lowercase hex characters and the round trip is bit-exact (F-10); a signed GDScript read of a `world_seed` above 2^63 − 1 is negative [UNV] and must still round-trip. Any other width, an uppercase letter or a non-hex character is refused (`SL_PRECHECK_HEADER_HEX_MALFORMED`, CORRUPT). Rationale: S-1 — a float parse loses everything above 2^53.
- **If a header number that must stay a number (`generation`, a length, an offset) arrives at or above 2^53 or with a fraction**: the float parse has already rounded it, so the pre-check refuses (`SL_PRECHECK_HEADER_FIELD_NOT_INT`, CORRUPT). Rationale: a silently rounded `generation` would corrupt ring order.
- **If `header_len` is 0, a corrupt 4 GiB, or larger than the file allows**: refused at the `header_len` bounds step before any allocation (`SL_PRECHECK_HEADER_LEN_INVALID`, CORRUPT). Only a re-signed file can reach this step, because the trailer step comes first; the fuzz includes re-signed files for exactly that reason.
- **If the encoded file would exceed `MAX_SAVE_BYTES`, a block exceeds `MAX_BLOCK_BYTES`, or the header exceeds `MAX_HEADER_BYTES` at write time**: no file is produced (`SL_WRITE_TOO_LARGE{block}`), `save_status` reads `FAILED`, the ring is untouched and play continues; nothing is truncated. Rationale: the write side never produces a file its own read side would refuse (F-6).
- **If the size crosses `SAVE_SIZE_ALARM_BYTES`**: the write proceeds and `SL_SIZE_ALARM` is logged once per campaign open. Rationale: it is a prompt to review compression at VS-A, not a failure.
- **If the header `codec` is not 0**: refused at the `format_version` step (`SL_PRECHECK_FORMAT_UNSUPPORTED`, INCOMPATIBLE). Rationale: the field is reserved with no decode path in the MVP, which also rules out decompression bombs.
- **If `T < last_save_T` is observed**: `elapsed := 0`, `last_save_T := T`, and `SL_AUTOSAVE_CLOCK_REGRESSION` is logged once. Rationale: unreachable after the reset-on-restore rule, so it is a defect signal; dev builds assert.
- **If `kernel.clock.T` cannot be read as an integer**: `SL_BAD_CLOCK`, no write and no restore. Rationale: every interval and window is in ticks (SL-23); with no clock, no decision is safe.

### Simultaneous rules and same-tick collisions

- **If a Graph warning window opens on the tick the interval falls due, or a second window opens as the first closes** *(Amended 2026-10-26 — CD-GDD-ALIGN)*: the save waits (`graph_tell_block`, F-2), re-evaluated on the retry grid; chained windows are bounded by `AUTOSAVE_MAX_DEFER_TICKS`, after which the save fires tagged `RISKY`. Rationale: the Graph's `W_MIN_WARNING_S` guarantee must not be shortened by a restore, and the cap keeps `loss_ticks` unchanged.
- **If the only open Graph window belongs to a dormant section**: its `warn_since_step` is frozen (Graph dormant-section rule) so it cannot close; until the Graph owner answers OQ-38 the save can wait to the cap and be tagged `RISKY`. Rationale: a rare, bounded exposure that is recorded rather than hidden.
- **If an event and the interval fall due on the same tick**: one save, kind `EVENT` (F-1). Rationale: the ring must not be flushed by two generations a frame apart.
- **If a due autosave and the `DEAD` tick share a boundary**: no snapshot is taken; `DEAD` is a hard condition and the restore wins (SL-7). Rationale: a snapshot of a dead world is the trap the gate exists to prevent.
- **If no `DOWNED` episode was observed before `DEAD`**: `T_downed := T_dead` for `generation_select`, and the hard condition still closes the gate at that boundary. Rationale: the formula must not depend on an observation Save/Load may not have made.
- **If `request_restore` arrives while the kernel is `SAVING`**: the kernel waits for the boundary (K:651) and the capture completes; the death stub then waits for the in-flight write to reach `WRITTEN` or `FAILED` (SL-7) and selects among verified candidates, the new generation included if it landed. Rationale: the epoch bump must not abort a write the stub could use.
- **If a Save & Quit and an interval autosave are due at the same tick**: the `QUIT` path wins and the pending autosave state is discarded (`due_first_T` is session memory only); only an in-flight write delays it. Rationale: the quit writes the current state anyway.
- **If Continue is pressed while the kernel is `LOADING`**: the second call is ignored (`SL_LOAD_REENTRANT`), as the kernel also ignores and reports a second request. Rationale: one restore at a time, one epoch.
- **If the death stub fires before the initial `NEW_GAME` generation is `WRITTEN`**: the stub waits for `WRITTEN` or `FAILED` (SL-7); on `FAILED` the escalation of SL-18 applies (`SL_NEW_GAME_NO_BASELINE`, the empty directory removed, the menu). Rationale: the 30 s `DOWNED` floor makes the case nearly unreachable, but a campaign with no verified generation must never reach a first death.

### Writing, crashing and recovering (save/restore)

- **If the process is killed during `WRITING_TMP`**: the position's file is intact and a partial `gen_k.tmp` remains; the next open deletes the `.tmp` and the ring is unchanged (T-6). Rationale: an unverified `.tmp` is never a candidate.
- **If the process is killed between `SWAPPING_OUT` and `SWAPPING_IN`**: `gen_k.bak` and a complete `.tmp` exist with no `gen_k.sav`; recovery promotes the `.tmp` if it verifies and deletes the `.bak`, otherwise promotes the `.bak` back (T-6). Rationale: at least two verified generations exist during any swap because `AUTOSAVE_GENERATIONS` is at least 2.
- **If a `store_*` call returns false, a file will not open, or disk is full or locked**: the `.tmp` is removed best-effort, previous generations are untouched, `save_status` reads `FAILED` (`SL_WRITE_FAILED{step}`), `last_save_T` is unchanged so the save stays due, and the retry backs off to at most `AUTOSAVE_FAIL_BACKOFF_MAX_TICKS`; after `AUTOSAVE_FAIL_ESCALATE_COUNT` consecutive failures the HUD notice is raised. Rationale: sourced — `FileAccess.store_*` returns `bool` since 4.4 (breaking-changes), so every return is checked.
- **If a rename fails with a sharing violation (antivirus, indexer)**: the worker retries `RENAME_RETRY_COUNT` times, `RENAME_RETRY_DELAY_MS` apart, then rolls back by renaming the `.bak` back and reports `FAILED`. Rationale: the design assumes only that renaming a closed file to an absent name is all-or-nothing [UNV].
- **If a write job outlives a restore**: a job that sees an advanced epoch before its first rename aborts and removes its `.tmp` (`SL_WRITE_ABORTED_RESTORE`); a job already past `SWAPPING_IN` lands a verified generation of the abandoned timeline, which is moved to `quarantine/` as `STALE_EPOCH` and is never a candidate. Rationale: otherwise a later death would restore the ghost of the previous timeline.
- **If two files carry the same `generation` (a kill between renames)**: prefer `.sav` over `.bak`, then verified over unverified; the loser is removed only after the winner verifies. Rationale: order is the header number, so the choice is deterministic.
- **If `campaign.json` is missing, corrupt or disagrees with the files**: it is rebuilt by scanning headers (`SL_MANIFEST_REBUILT`) and a load is never refused for it; demoted and quarantined generations stay excluded because they are encoded in file names, while `suspect` counters and REBASED baselines are lost. Rationale: the manifest is a hint, never truth (SL-3).
- **If `AUTOSAVE_GENERATIONS` shrinks between builds (3 → 2)**: the writer only uses positions `0 … N − 1`; a file at a position beyond `N` stays on disk as a candidate by generation order and is removed only by the campaign delete. If it grows, new positions fill lazily as the first free position. Rationale: never delete a file that cannot be proven obsolete.
