- **If a restore moves `T` backwards**: `last_save_T := T(g)`, `due_first_T` and `event_pending` are cleared and `restored_gen_id := g`; generation numbers keep rising and the first new autosave is `AUTOSAVE_INTERVAL_TICKS` after `T(g)`. Rationale: timers are sim time and the sim just went back.

### Hostile, damaged and foreign files (pre-check)

- **If a file is empty, shorter than the size floor, or truncated anywhere**: `SL_PRECHECK_FILE_TOO_SMALL` or `SL_PRECHECK_FILE_SHA_MISMATCH` (CORRUPT); the file is a quarantine candidate and the loader falls back. Rationale: a missing or short trailer is truncation, detected for free.
- **If one bit flips in the last byte of the magic (the layout-id byte)**: the trailer no longer matches, so the file is CORRUPT (`SL_PRECHECK_FILE_SHA_MISMATCH`), not INCOMPATIBLE, and the loader falls back on an intact ring. Rationale: the layout id is judged only after the trailer (SL-1 format law); a flipped bit must not stop the loader.
- **If one bit flips in `format_version`**: the same — corruption, falling back — never "made by a newer build". Rationale: the integrity steps precede the compatibility steps.
- **If the newest generation is genuinely newer (a re-signed `format_version` above ours, or `min_reader_version` above ours)**: INCOMPATIBLE — the loader stops with "made by an incompatible build", files stay in place, and the campaign directory is `READ_ONLY` for the older build; New campaign creates a different directory. Rationale: a silent fallback would roll the player back and then overwrite newer files (SL-11).
- **If an older generation is loadable but the newest is INCOMPATIBLE**: no fallback occurs and no older generation is overwritten. Rationale: same-campaign files share the incompatibility in practice, and a rollback is data loss.
- **If a block id is unknown and not on `RETIRED_BLOCKS`**: INCOMPATIBLE, the loader stops (`SL_PRECHECK_UNKNOWN_BLOCK`), so the kernel's `RESTORE_UNKNOWN_BLOCK` is unreachable — reaching it would be a Save/Load defect. If it is on `RETIRED_BLOCKS`, it is stripped with `SL_PRECHECK_RETIRED_BLOCK_STRIPPED` and the mode becomes REBASED (cause 2). Rationale: a removed block must not strand old saves; an unknown one is a newer or foreign build.
- **If a non-mandatory block is missing and its `added_in_format` exceeds the file's `format_version`**: `default_state()` fills it with a note and the mode becomes REBASED (cause 3); otherwise `SL_PRECHECK_MANDATORY_MISSING` (CORRUPT). Rationale: K:645 leaves the decision to Save/Load.
- **If a `PERCEPTION_OUT` block, or a block of the wrong `persist_scope`, appears in a file**: `SL_PRECHECK_PERCEPTION_BLOCK_PRESENT` or `SL_PRECHECK_SCOPE_MISMATCH` (CORRUPT). Rationale: Save/Load never writes one, so presence is a defect or tampering, and a PROFILE block inside a world file could roll knowledge back.
- **If a payload contains an `Object` marker, a forbidden type, excessive nesting or too many nodes**: `SL_PRECHECK_BLOCK_TYPE_FORBIDDEN` or `_BLOCK_DECODE_LIMITS` (CORRUPT); no object is instantiated and the kernel is never touched. Rationale: the object-disallowed decode stops instantiation but does not itself bound allocation (SL-2).
- **If a Graph payload stores a packed array as a JSON-style number list (an S-1 violation that survives the container)**: it passes the container checks, because the pre-check validates declared types, and the Graph's two-phase `restore()` refuses it (`GRAPH_RESTORE_BAD_TAG`) with the live Graph block byte-identical; the kernel enters `FAULTED` (blocks restored earlier in `restore_after` order are not rolled back, K:650), the menu offers Continue on the remaining candidates (SL-22), and the generation's failure counter increments. Rationale: owner-level validation cannot run before the kernel is touched; the 1,000-mutation fuzz (re-signed files) measures how rare this path is.
- **If header `T` or `world_seed` differs from the `kernel.clock` block although every hash is valid (a re-signed file)**: `SL_PRECHECK_HEADER_CLOCK_MISMATCH` (CORRUPT). Rationale: the header copy exists for exactly this cross-check.
- **If a file's `campaign_id` differs from its directory name (a file copied between campaigns, or a directory renamed)**: `SL_PRECHECK_CAMPAIGN_MISMATCH` (CORRUPT), quarantine candidate. Rationale: a copied directory under the same name from another machine is untrusted data under the same caps and loads if it passes; a renamed one does not.
- **If the header `hash_algo` is not implemented by this build**: the file stage still runs (SHA-256 is Save/Load's own), the compare is skipped (`SL_VERIFY_ALGO_SKIPPED`) and the mode is REBASED (cause 4); it is never a refusal. Rationale: a kernel hash change must not break saves (SL-16).
- **If stale `.tmp` or `.bak` files exist when a campaign opens**: the recovery scan of T-6 reconciles them before the first load; nothing it cannot prove obsolete is deleted. Rationale: a crash can leave any of them.
- **If `seats` names a PlayerId the session has not bound**: allowed; the session rebuilds its binding table (CR-7) and the owners' restores decide. A `PER_PLAYER` key outside `seats`, or an empty or duplicated `seats`, is `SL_PRECHECK_SEATS_INVALID`. Rationale: identity is the seat, never the transport peer.

### Death stub and generation selection (degenerate strategies live here too)

- **If the only candidate is `RISKY` and a loop is detected**: it is restored anyway and `SL_STEPBACK_NONE_AVAILABLE` is logged. Rationale: the user rule is restart from the last autosave; there is nothing older.
- **If a `RISKY` generation is restored, the crew dies inside the window, and the predecessor is chosen**: the `RISKY` file is renamed `gen_k.demoted`; a second death before any new autosave restores the predecessor again, never the demoted one (the three-death scripted fixture). Rationale: without demotion the ring would oscillate between a `RISKY` newest and its predecessor.
- **If the newest generation is not `RISKY` and the crew dies repeatedly at the same spot**: the newest is restored every time (the user rule); `deaths_per_restored_gen` is counted in diagnostics for playtest. Rationale: stepping back here is the one question flagged for the user (OQ-1).
- **If every candidate fails the pre-check**: `SL_LOAD_NO_LOADABLE_SAVE` is published before the kernel is touched; the Chart Table shows it as a boot-flow screen (no pause is requested, CT-25; in-session teardown, OQ-42) with New campaign or Quit; no file is deleted and the dead world is never autosaved. Rationale: the game stays inside the player's control and the files stay available for recovery.
- **If only demoted generations remain loadable**: a second pass restores the newest demoted one. Rationale: a stepped-back-from file is still better than nothing.
- **If the stub fires on a campaign with no restore yet (the first life)**: no loop is possible (`g_r` is none) and the newest generation, the `NEW_GAME` baseline at worst, is restored. Rationale: the formula needs a restored generation to detect a loop.
- **If the crew dies within `AUTOSAVE_MIN_GAP_TICKS` of a restore**: the same non-`RISKY` generation is restored again and the loop rule does not apply. Rationale: only a `RISKY` generation is suspected of being unwinnable.
- **If `DEAD` is entered during a camera cut**: the request is made at that tick's boundary, the Presenter holds the down sheet's last frame, and no second cut is issued (the Controller's edge case). Rationale: only `DEAD` during a *cut channel* is impossible, because `DOWNED` precedes it by 1,800 ticks.
- **If an owner's `restore()` or `post_restore()` fails inside `LOADING`**: the kernel enters `FAULTED` with the prior state not intact; the loader does not retry in line; the menu's Continue re-runs the pipeline on the remaining candidates (SL-22). Rationale: K:170 allows only an explicit user restore out of `FAULTED`.
- **If a reproduction mismatch is detected (EXACT mode)**: release marks the generation `suspect` and plays on; dev and test halt; the loader never walks to another generation. Rationale: the bytes are proven, the defect is in the code, and it would reproduce on every generation.
- **If the compare window is missed (`kernel.clock.T` ≠ header `T`)**: `SL_VERIFY_WINDOW_MISSED`, no verdict, play on. Rationale: a hash taken after a tick has run would differ for the wrong reason (SL-12).

### Save & Quit and window close

- **If a Save & Quit is requested while any bound seat is `DOWNED`**: nothing is written — `save_and_quit` returns `SL_QUIT_NO_WRITE_DOWNED`, `save_status` is unchanged, and shutdown proceeds after the hold-to-confirm "quit without saving"; Continue then restores the newest verified generation. Rationale: no file on disk may ever hold a `DOWNED` or `DEAD` seat; the death stub would have restored the same file within `DOWN_TO_DEATH_TICKS`.
- **If a Save & Quit is requested while a seat is `DEAD`**: unreachable in the solo MVP (the stub fires on the `DEAD` tick); the clause exists for multi-seat futures. Rationale: SL-6.
- **If a Save & Quit is requested with only a soft condition closed (a tell, a breach phase, `CHANNELLING`, `STAGGERED`, `IN_POD`)**: the generation is written immediately and tagged `RISKY`. Rationale: refusing to quit traps the player; quitting without writing silently discards up to one interval.
- **If a Save & Quit finds the state hash equal to the newest generation's (same `T`, nothing changed)**: the write is skipped (`SL_QUIT_DEDUPED`) and shutdown proceeds. Rationale: a duplicate generation would only push a useful one out of the ring.
- **If the Save & Quit write fails or times out (`SAVE_QUIT_TIMEOUT_MS`)**: the quit is held, `save_status` reads `FAILED`, the pause panel stays, and the player may retry or quit without saving by hold-to-confirm. Rationale: never silently lose progress; never trap the player.
- **If the window is closed while a write is in progress**: the quit waits up to `SAVE_QUIT_TIMEOUT_MS`, then exits; the next open reconciles by the crash table (T-6). Rationale: an OS kill is the same case.
- **If the window is closed while the kernel is `PAUSED` under the open Chart Table (SOLO)** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass)*: Save/Load's handler runs `save_and_quit()` from `PAUSED` (`PAUSED → SAVING → PAUSED`) under SL-6's rules, then `request_shutdown`; the Chart Table calls nothing (EC-CT-38). **On a boot-flow screen** nothing is written and the process ends through boot code (ASK-CT-11). Rationale: one save path, the same rules everywhere.
- **If a quit is requested while the kernel is `FAULTED`**: no write is attempted (K:151); the quit proceeds to the menu or shutdown. Rationale: the faulting session is never saved over an existing generation.

### PROFILE store

- **If `profile.sav` is absent and zero PROFILE blocks are registered (every MVP build)**: this is valid on a cold open — an empty profile. Rationale: nothing to restore, nothing to refuse.
- **If `profile.sav` is absent and one or more PROFILE blocks are registered**: `SL_PRECHECK_MANDATORY_MISSING` for the profile file (CORRUPT class); `profile.sav.bak` is tried first, and if it is also unusable the world still loads, the profile is reset to `default_state()` with `SL_PROFILE_RESET`, and the damaged file is quarantined, never deleted. Rationale: Pillar 5 says knowledge is not lost with the ship, and a corrupt knowledge file must not strand the world.
- **If a PROFILE write is pending when a world restore occurs**: the profile is flushed first (its file is not part of the restore) and the restore then proceeds; a world restore never reads `profile.sav` into WORLD blocks. Rationale: CR-18 — partial restore within a scope is unsupported, and PROFILE blocks are never rolled back by a world restore.
- **If the process is killed during a PROFILE write**: the crash table (T-6) applies to `profile.sav` and its permanent `.bak`; the ring is unaffected. Rationale: a profile is rewritten by the same atomic procedure.

### Hallucination interplay (Pillars 1 and 4)

- **If a save is captured while a hallucination is active**: the file holds the true SIM state — the sanity level and the owner's counters — and never the perception output (`PERCEPTION_OUT`) or a field describing what the player saw. Rationale: a save is ground truth (H1); the rebuilt output after load derives from level and seed.
- **If the crew reloads after dying to a scrubber that "looked fixed" because of a false system state** *(Amended 2026-10-26 — CD-GDD-ALIGN)*: Save/Load restores every persisted counter and seed exactly (kernel G4, D.10), reads no perception and varies nothing by it; whether the false state's onset replays at the same moment or is re-keyed is Sanity & Hallucination Rules' decision (OQ-37). The physical fact underneath, that the scrubber fails at minute four, is unchanged and is Pillar 1. Rationale: binding perception replay here would let the player memorise hallucinations across deaths (P2, P4); any re-keying must not be steerable by quit or death.
- **If the sanity level was low at death**: the restore returns the generation's sanity level (WORLD-scoped) — death does not cure sanity (H3), so no "die to clear your head" exploit exists. Rationale: Suit vitals and the Sanity level roll back with the world.
- **If the first `RUNNING` frame after `LOADING` is presented unfiltered**: this violates H4; the requirement is routed to the Perception Filter, Sanity and Pipeline (OQ-11), and the black hold covers any rebuild. Rationale: one clean frame would be a free oracle.
- **If an autosave would vary its timing, or a save list its contents, with sanity**: not allowed and not built; Save/Load never reads perception (SL-24). Rationale: a lie that reached the save menu would be unfalsifiable inside the session.

### Co-op hooks and later tiers

- **If a guest later joins (VS-B)**: the snapshot payload is separable from the file wrapper (SL-1) and is the guest's initial transfer (CR-18); a guest's save request is the reserved `saveload.request_save` command (provisional — Co-op Session & Replication), validated on the host; no guest-authoritative block exists. Rationale: host-owned saves only.
- **If one of several seats is `DEAD` while others live (multi-seat futures)**: the hard condition keeps autosave closed until Failure Contract & Re-wake resolves the seat (provisional — Failure Contract undesigned), so the deferral is unbounded in that case. Rationale: recorded as a gap for VS-A/VS-B, not designed here.
- **If a friendly-fire death occurs (VS-B)**: the save records the `DEAD` seat's state like any death and never records blame (Anti-pillar: no PvP). Rationale: blame is Failure Contract's.
- **If a peer reconnects under a different transport id**: nothing in any save changes — blocks are keyed by PlayerId (SL-20). Rationale: "keyed by peer id" is a stale token.
- **If `TICK_RATE` differs between the build that wrote a file and the build reading it**: INCOMPATIBLE at the `tick_rate` step (`SL_PRECHECK_TICK_RATE_MISMATCH`); the kernel's `TICK_RATE_MISMATCH` is the unreachable backstop. Rationale: a save is bound to the rate that wrote it.

### Versioning and migration

- **If a golden v1 save is loaded by a build whose Graph migrates v1 to v2**: the mode is REBASED (cause 1), the load succeeds, the recomputed hash becomes the baseline in `campaign.json`, and the next autosave writes the new shape; the old file is not rewritten. Rationale: a downgrade followed by an upgrade must not half-migrate a file (SL-14).
- **If a save from a development build predates the first build given to anyone outside the project**: it may be refused with "made by an incompatible development build" — migration is an obligation only from that build on (SL-14). Rationale: the policy is Save/Load's own; the Layout open question that the Graph cites is unverified (OQ-12).
- **If a new persisted block is added after the first outside build**: `format_version` increments, `min_reader_version` equals the new version, the container migration is the identity and the new block is filled by `default_state()` (SL-14). Rationale: the friction of adding a block stays constant.

### Degenerate strategies and accepted exposures

- **If the player kills the process (Alt-F4, task manager) after a mistake**: the game falls back to the newest generation, a rewind of at most `AUTOSAVE_INTERVAL_TICKS + AUTOSAVE_MAX_DEFER_TICKS` = 18 000 ticks — the same price as dying, minus the 1,801-tick floor. Accepted and unpreventable (no write can protect against a kill); measured by M3 below (OQ-25). Rationale: both rewinds cost at most the minutes since the last autosave and nothing else.
- **If the player Save & Quits before a risky door, relaunches and Continues** *(Amended 2026-10-26 — CD-GDD-ALIGN)*: it works as a retry of the player's inputs, and because the physical world is deterministic it can also reveal what is behind the door for up to the minutes since the last autosave. Accepted at Zomboid parity (the user's death rule is binding), **but with a measurable trigger, not an open-ended "if playtest shows"**: metric M3 below. The remedy is the interval first, then Failure Contract & Re-wake, never deleting the `QUIT` generation (OQ-25). Rationale: SL-6.
- **If the player dies on purpose to rewind a bad decision or to scout a room** *(Amended 2026-10-26 — CD-GDD-ALIGN)*: the cost is at least 1,801 ticks plus the time back to the last autosave, the restore also returns vitals and sanity, and in a deterministic world the death can reveal one visible unknown (Discovery, aesthetic priority 1) for about 3 to 5.5 minutes of sim time. Accepted at Zomboid parity; metric M2 below is the trigger. Rationale: Pillar 5's design test — safe farming versus scarcity — resolves to scarcity, as long as scouting deaths stay rare.
- **If a save file is edited by hand and re-signed**: it loads if it passes the pre-check; integrity guards accidental damage, not tampering (host-owned, no PvP, no leaderboard). Rationale: documented scope of SL-16; a stricter trust model is a security-audit question before multiplayer.

**Playtest triggers for the accepted exposures** *(Amended 2026-10-26 — CD-GDD-ALIGN)*. Pillar 5's "loss costs ship and body" and Discovery (aesthetic priority 1) pull in opposite directions here, so the exposure is accepted with numbers attached. The constants below are playtest-protocol values, not data-file knobs, and are provisional starting values to be calibrated at the first outside build (AC-SL-94). `T_enter` is, at the MVP, the derelict-level first boarding `FIRST_BOARDED{derelict_id, T}` published by Web Map & Transit (WM-28; the dock room), not a compartment-level first entry, which no designed system tracks; a DIAGNOSTIC-only compartment-entry log (Debug Overlay, from Body section changes; never persisted) is the recorded refinement if M2 proves too coarse (OQ-25) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass)*. Wall-clock gaps come from `created_unix` file metadata and the process-start log, read by the playtest harness only, never by the sim (H9).

| Metric | Definition | Starting threshold | Named response |
|---|---|---|---|
| **M1** `deaths_per_restored_gen` | Deaths whose restored generation is the same `generation`, counted per tester | Median across testers ≥ 3, or any tester ≥ 5 | Players are stuck on the same save, so death is too dear: lower `AUTOSAVE_INTERVAL_TICKS` by up to 3 600 ticks (not below 3 600, OQ-19); if M1 trips again, ask the user OQ-1 (non-`RISKY` step-back) |
| **M2** `scout_death_rate` | Deaths with `T_dead − T_enter` ≤ `SCOUT_DEATH_WINDOW_TICKS` 5 400 (the 1 800-tick `DOWN_TO_DEATH_TICKS` floor plus 3 600 ticks of looking), divided by all deaths; evaluated for a tester with ≥ 4 deaths | More than 0.25 of that tester's deaths, in at least 2 of 3 testers | Death is being used as a free look: raise `AUTOSAVE_INTERVAL_TICKS` by up to 3 600 ticks (not above 36 000); if M2 trips again in the next round, escalate to Failure Contract & Re-wake at VS-A |
| **M3** `quit_relaunch_rate` | Save & Quit followed by Continue within `QUIT_RELAUNCH_WINDOW_S` 120 s of wall time, divided by all `QUIT` generations | More than 0.30 of `QUIT` generations, or 3 or more such pairs inside one 30-minute session | Quit-and-relaunch is a disguised manual save: raise `AUTOSAVE_INTERVAL_TICKS` as for M2; if M3 trips again, escalate to Failure Contract & Re-wake at VS-A; never delete the `QUIT` generation (SL-8) and never add save-anywhere (OQ-18 allows only the pinned main-menu slot, and only for M1) |

If M1 trips together with M2 or M3, the interval cannot repair both and the response goes straight to Failure Contract & Re-wake. The ladder is deliberately short: one retune of the one lever (OQ-19), then the owner of the failure contract.

## Dependencies

**Status at authoring time (2026-10-26, a Glob of `design/gdd/`).** Designed: Command & Simulation Layer, Life-Support Graph, Player Controller & Input, Pixel-Perfect Render Pipeline & Camera, Emitter State & Light-Exposure Query, Item & Module Database. Draft in flight on disk (Status In Design): Failure & Trail System. No file at all: Ship Section & Layout Data Model, Ship Body & Interior Physics, Suit & Equipment, Attention, Modular Ship Assembly, Web Map & Transit, Sanity & Hallucination Rules, Horror Entities, Chart Table, HUD & Suit Layer, Perception Filter, Failure Contract & Re-wake, Co-op Session & Replication. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass)* **Status refreshed 2026-10-27:** every system named above is now designed on disk except Failure Contract & Re-wake and Co-op Session & Replication; Failure & Trail System is Designed (2026-10-26); Interaction System, Boarding & Salvage, 2D Lighting & Occlusion and Debug Overlay are designed as well.

**Upstream (Save/Load depends on):** exactly one system — the Command & Simulation Layer — matching the index (`Depends On: Command & Simulation Layer`; bottleneck, nine dependents).

| System | Direction | Strength | Interface | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Command & Simulation Layer** | Save/Load depends on the kernel | **Hard** | CR-15 control plane (`request_snapshot`, `request_restore`, `request_shutdown`), CR-8 registry contract (`persist`, `persist_scope`, `schema_version`, `restore_after`, `post_restore`, `default_state`), CR-18 snapshot semantics, D.12 `state_hash`, `kernel.clock` | Designed |
| **Life-Support Graph** | Block owner; Save/Load carries its S-1 obligation (no runtime edge for the block; the index's Graph → Save/Load edge was removed). Save/Load also **reads** the Graph's `read_view.failures()` for the Graph half of the autosave tell condition *(Amended 2026-10-26 — CD-GDD-ALIGN)* | **Hard** for MVP load (the largest block); the tell read is **soft-gate input, required at MVP** | `graph.state`, S-1, `restore_after [layout.state]`, `GRAPH_RESTORE_*`, golden-save convention; LS-13, LS-14, LS-19 and D.11 (`cause`, `sev`, `warn_elapsed`, `W_MIN_STEPS`, `GRAPH_STEP_TICKS`) | Designed |
| **Player Controller & Input** | Block owner and the death trigger | **Hard** for the death stub | `controller.state`, `loco_state` / `DEAD`, PC-17 restore rules, the boundary-time hand-off | Designed |
| **Pixel-Perfect Render Pipeline & Camera** | Consumer of kernel states; keeps `k_z`; shows `TRANSIT_HOLD` | Soft | `LOADING → RUNNING` `CUT`; `restore_reason` (a routed request) | Designed |
| **Ship Section & Layout Data Model** (`layout.state`) | Block owner; the `restore_after` root of the Graph | **Hard for load; this GDD's top risk** | `layout.state` (schema 1, `restore_after []`), stable `room_id` | Designed (LY-1, LY-6) |
| **Emitter State & Light-Exposure Query** (`emitter.state`) | Block owner | Hard per block once registered | `restore_after [layout.state, graph.state, body.state, controller.state]` | Designed (EM-1) |
| **Ship Body & Interior Physics** (`body.state`) | Block owner (BDY-5; WORLD × SHARED) | Hard per block | the actors table (`pos_curr`, `sub`, `last_octant`, `odometer`, …) and the bodies table; never `pos_prev`; restore-idempotent at block level (BDY-31, SL-15 item 10) | Designed |
| **Suit & Equipment**; **Sanity & Hallucination Rules**; **Attention System**; **Web Map & Transit**; **Horror Entities**; **Interaction System** | Block owners (design-time dependency on SL-15) | Hard per block once registered | `suit.state`; `sanity.state`, `sanity.ledger`; `attention.state` and the soft-gate read view `attention.view`; `web.state` (provisional name), the `POD_ARRIVED` event and the node `ring` behind the depth label; `horror.state`; `interaction.state` and the pure read `last_salvage_T()` (BS-21) | Designed |
| **Modular Ship Assembly** | Block owner and event source: owns `assembly.state` (pending completion + last outcome per seat; no world history); weld/cut completion polled through `last_shape_change_T()` | Hard per block; soft for the trigger | `assembly.state`; `last_shape_change_T()` (MSA-33; SL-13) | Designed |
| **Item & Module Database** | Provides a content epoch integer and diagnostics for the header | Soft | `catalogue_version`, `catalogue_hash`, `renamed_from[]`, `replaced_by` | Designed (IDB-26, IDB-27) |
| **Failure & Trail System** | None | — | Pure reader and renderer; registers no block; the Graph persists no footprint record | Designed (2026-10-26); no edge |
| **2D Lighting & Occlusion** | None | — | Pure renderer; registers no block (LO-29) | Designed; no edge |
| **Boarding & Salvage** | Names the module-cut autosave trigger; owns no block | Soft | BS-21, through Interaction's `last_salvage_T()` | Designed |
| **Chart Table**; **HUD & Suit Layer** | UI consumers of the host-local API and reads | Soft | SL-21 API, `save_status`, `fail_escalated`, `SaveInfo`, `campaign_info().newest_failed_verification`, `restore_reason` | Designed |
| **Perception Filter** | Read-only consumer; owes the "no clean frame" ordering | Soft (routed request) | SL-24 | Designed (the ordering request stands, OQ-11) |
| **Failure Contract & Re-wake** (VS-A) | Replaces the death stub | Soft | The same `request_restore` path and PROFILE store; `restore_reason` | Undesigned — provisional |
| **Co-op Session & Replication** (VS-B) | Reuses the snapshot payload; a guest save request is a command defined later | Soft | Separable payload (CR-18); `saveload.request_save` (reserved, provisional) | Undesigned — provisional |
| **Godot 4.7.2 file I/O, JSON, `var_to_bytes`, `HashingContext`, threads** | Engine | **Hard** | None covered by the engine reference except two sourced facts: `FileAccess.store_*` return `bool` (since 4.4) and packed-array element writes skip the property setter (4.7) | [UNV] — the concept's spike "save round-trip with version" |

**Downstream (systems that depend on Save/Load):** nine in the index — Suit & Equipment, Modular Ship Assembly, Web Map & Transit, Attention System, Sanity & Hallucination Rules (MVP, designed in this run), and Knowledge Codex & Logs, Failure Contract & Re-wake, Co-op Session & Replication (VS-B), Host-Owned World & Guest Carry-over (later tiers). Every downstream row is a **design-time** dependency on the contracts of this GDD (SL-15, SL-9, SL-7, SL-4) and never a runtime call; for the designed Graph and Controller the edge is moot (S-1 is carried in SL-2, PC-17 in OQ-9), and for the undesigned block owners it is real and labelled "design-time (contract)" (OQ-15). Debug Overlay, Settings & Input Rebinding and the Chart Table consume reads or the host-local API only.

**Bidirectional consistency.** The kernel's Dependencies table lists Save/Load as a **Hard** downstream registrant with "control plane, snapshot/restore semantics, `Dropped(RELOAD)`" on its side and "file format, migrations, death stub" on ours — agreed. The kernel's Interactions row 3, the Graph's Interactions and Dependencies rows, the Controller's `DEAD` row and Interactions row 15, and the Pipeline's Interactions row 17 and traceability row are quoted in Interactions with the places they need a text fix. The designed Item DB's Dependencies row for Save/Load (`catalogue_version`, `catalogue_hash` (diagnostic), stable `def_id` strings, `renamed_from[]`, `replaced_by`) agrees on content and on the field name, `catalogue_version`, which this GDD uses (history: OQ-13). The designed Emitter GDD's EM-1 adds `body.state` to `restore_after` (the sibling brief omitted it); the designed Failure & Trail GDD registers nothing. No sibling draft contradicts a rule here.

**Data Save/Load takes, and what it refuses.** It takes decoded plain data (primitives, arrays, dictionaries, packed arrays), the kernel's snapshot and hash, the Controller's `loco_state`, optional SIM read views and owner notifications. It refuses: object decoding and `Resource` / `.tres` loads in any form; `Vector2i` and the other non-H7 types (SL-2); any `PERCEPTION_OUT` input; any 64-bit value as a JSON number; any write to a block or registration of a block or hook (SL-17); any dependence on a transport peer id (SL-20) or on wall-clock in logic (SL-23); any file that fails the pre-check (SL-11).

## Tuning Knobs

Knobs live in one data file read at boot (never hardcoded, coding standard); the boot invariants below are asserted against it. **★ marks a cross-system fact** — a value or name other GDDs cite or must not contradict — and so a registry candidate for the registry stage. Tick values convert at `TICK_RATE` 60 (cited): 10 800 = 180 s, 7 200 = 120 s, 3 600 = 60 s, 1 800 = 30 s, 300 = 5 s. "Too high" and "too low" are the failure modes a player or a developer sees. Defaults for engine-facing figures are estimates until the "save round-trip with version" spike measures them [UNV].

### Owned knobs

| Knob | Default | Unit | Safe range | Too high | Too low | Interacts with |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ★ `FORMAT_VERSION` | 1 | int | ≥ 1; bumped only on a container change | Every bump needs a container migration and a golden fixture | 0 is invalid | `min_reader_version`, every block's `added_in_format`, SL-14 |
| ★ `AUTOSAVE_GENERATIONS` | 3 | files | 2 – 5 | Disk and quarantine clutter; a longer, slower scan; deeper save-scum history without benefit | 1 forbidden: no safe swap, one bad write loses the campaign; 2 survives exactly one corrupt file | `LOAD_MAX_ATTEMPTS`, step-back depth (F-3), invariant I4 |
| ★ `AUTOSAVE_INTERVAL_TICKS` | 10 800 | ticks | 3 600 – 36 000 | Death is punishing (mean loss `I/2 + 1 800`), a crash rewinds far; at 36 000 the worst case is ~20 min | Death is cheap — "safe farming", Pillar 5 pressure vanishes; capture hitch every minute | `loss_ticks` (F-7), `AUTOSAVE_MAX_DEFER_TICKS`, `AUTOSAVE_MIN_GAP_TICKS`; **the single lever of how much death hurts** (OQ-19) |
| ★ `AUTOSAVE_MIN_GAP_TICKS` | 1 800 | ticks | 900 – 5 400, strictly below the interval | Event saves never matter (effectively interval-only) | Weld and dock bursts write back to back and flush the ring in a minute | `event_due` (F-1), invariant I1; equals `DOWN_TO_DEATH_TICKS` by coincidence only |
| ★ `AUTOSAVE_RETRY_TICKS` | 300 | ticks | 60 – 900 | A recovered crew waits long for its save (up to `R − 1` ticks late) | Gate-polling churn; a tight retry after failure | `fire` grid (F-2), invariant I3 |
| ★ `AUTOSAVE_MAX_DEFER_TICKS` | 7 200 | ticks | 1 800 – (`AUTOSAVE_INTERVAL_TICKS` − `AUTOSAVE_RETRY_TICKS`), a multiple of the retry period | Long unsaved stretches; ceiling `loss_max` grows linearly | Many `RISKY` generations; the step-back rule fires too often | `loss_max` (F-7), invariants I2, I3 |
| ★ `DEATH_LOOP_WINDOW_TICKS` | 3 600 | ticks | 1 800 – 10 800 | An honest later death of a `RISKY` generation is mistaken for a loop | The window cannot contain the player's acting time: the step-back never fires | `generation_select` (F-3), `T_downed`; matters only for `RISKY` generations |
| ★ `MAX_SAVE_BYTES` | 64 MiB | bytes | 4 – 256 MiB | Memory spike on a hostile or runaway file | Legitimate VS-A saves refused | `MAX_BLOCK_BYTES` (≤), invariant I5 |
| ★ `MAX_BLOCK_BYTES` | 32 MiB | bytes | 2 – 128 MiB, ≤ `MAX_SAVE_BYTES` | One block dominates memory on decode | A large block is refused | `MAX_DECODE_NODES`, I5, I6 |
| ★ `MAX_HEADER_BYTES` | 64 KiB | bytes | 16 – 256 KiB, ≥ 600 + `MAX_BLOCKS` × 150 | Large JSON parse on hostile input | Headers refused near 128 blocks (≈ 19 KiB) | F-6, kernel `MAX_BLOCKS` (cited), I7 |
| ★ `SAVE_SIZE_ALARM_BYTES` | 2 MiB | bytes | 1 – 8 MiB, below `MAX_BLOCK_BYTES` | Real growth goes unnoticed before VS-A | Alarm noise | Compression review (`codec`), I6 |
| ★ `LOADING_TARGET_MS` | 1 000 (provisional) | ms | 500 – 2 000, above the Pipeline's fade | The black hold reads as a crash (over ~2 s) | Meaningless below the 120 ms fade; a spurious log | `restart_ms` (F-8), I8; Game Feel |
| ★ `QUARANTINE_AFTER_FAILURES` | 2 | restore failures | 1 – 3 | Repeated `FAULTED` loops on one bad file | One transient fault quarantines a good file | SL-22; counts **owner-reported** restore failures only, never a reproduction mismatch |
| ★ `REBASE_BLOCKS` (provisional — Controller answered (PC-17); emptied by Save/Load when AC-SL-67 passes; Body answered by BDY-31) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass)* | `[controller.state]` | list of `block_id` | empty list … `[controller.state]` | A longer list weakens the reproduction stage | Empty while PC-17 still normalises: every mid-move autosave verifies `NOT_REPRODUCED` and dev builds halt | SL-12, F-4, OQ-9; **emptied by Save/Load when AC-SL-67 passes** (both owners have answered); a non-empty list is a known kernel-contract debt |
| `SL_QUARANTINE_SIZE_ALARM_BYTES` | 16 MiB | bytes | 1 – 256 MiB | Slow disk growth unreported | Alarm noise | Diagnostics threshold **only**; quarantined files are never deleted automatically; deletion is the explicit campaign delete under hold-to-confirm |
| `gate_tell_mode` | `FAIR` | enum | {`FAIR`, `STRICT`} | `STRICT` defers up to a whole Attention tell (≤ `TELL_MAX_TICKS` 1 800 ticks, Attention's, cited) and raises the loss tail | `FAIR` allows a save with exactly the guaranteed warning (600 ticks) left | `attention_tell_block` (F-2), `W_MIN_WARNING_S` (cited); **governs the Attention half only** and needs the Attention read view `attention.view` (designed; absent = open); the Graph half has no mode — it defers for the whole open window — *(Amended 2026-10-26 — CD-GDD-ALIGN)* |
| `GATE_SOFT_STATES` | {`CHANNELLING`, `STAGGERED`, `IN_POD`} | set | any subset; **never** `DOWNED` or `DEAD` | More deferral and more `RISKY` saves | Saves restore mid-transit and lose channel progress (PC-17) | F-2, invariant I10 |
| `AUTOSAVE_FAIL_BACKOFF_MAX_TICKS` | 4 800 | ticks | 600 – 10 800 | Long unsaved span on a failing disk | A capture hitch every few seconds on a dead disk | `AUTOSAVE_RETRY_TICKS`; the retry delay after the n-th consecutive failure is `min(AUTOSAVE_RETRY_TICKS × 2^(n−1), AUTOSAVE_FAIL_BACKOFF_MAX_TICKS)` = 300, 600, 1 200, 2 400, 4 800, 4 800 ticks (orchestrator ruling, OQ-26) |
| ★ `AUTOSAVE_FAIL_ESCALATE_COUNT` | 3 | failures | 1 – 10 | Silent loss of saves | A noisy HUD notice on a transient fault | `save_status` `FAILED`, the published `fail_escalated` (SL-21; the HUD notice, HS-46), SL-18 baseline escalation |
| `WRITE_DURABLE_ALARM_MS` | 500 | ms | 200 – 2 000 | Misses slow disks | Log noise | F-5; log-only |
| `SAVE_ENCODE_BUDGET_MS` | 4 | ms | 2 – 16 | A hitch hidden in the main thread | Log noise | F-5; adds to, is not part of, the kernel capture; log-only, never truncates |
| `WRITE_HANDOFF_MAX_FRACTION` | 0.05 | fraction of `SNAPSHOT_BUDGET_MS` | 0.01 – 0.2 | The hand-off becomes a visible hitch | Unachievable | F-5 |
| `SAVE_QUIT_TIMEOUT_MS` | 5 000 | ms | 2 000 – 15 000 | The quit feels hung | A false "save failed" on a slow disk | SL-6; recovery of T-6 covers an early exit |
| `LOAD_MAX_ATTEMPTS` | `AUTOSAVE_GENERATIONS` | candidates | 1 – 5 | A long walk of failed loads | One bad file blocks Continue | F-3, F-8 |
| `RENAME_RETRY_COUNT` | 3 | retries | 0 – 10 | The worker is busy long on a locked file | An antivirus lock becomes a failed save | `RENAME_RETRY_DELAY_MS`; also bounds the rename step of the stub's wait for an in-flight write (SL-7); a write stuck in flush or re-read is unbounded in principle (F-5) |
| `RENAME_RETRY_DELAY_MS` | 50 | ms | 10 – 250 | A slow failure report | Spinning on a locked file | worker only |
| `MAX_DECODE_DEPTH` | 16 | levels | 8 – 32 | Stack and time on hostile data | A legitimate nested block refused | SL-2 type walk; owners' snapshot depth |
| `MAX_DECODE_NODES` | 250 000 | nodes | 50 000 – 2 000 000 | Decode-walk time on hostile data | A dictionary-heavy block refused (a packed array counts as one node) | SL-2, `MAX_BLOCK_BYTES` |
| `MAX_JSON_DEPTH` | 8 | levels | 4 – 16 | Parse stack risk | None for the flat header | SL-1 header |
| `PROFILE_MIN_WRITE_GAP_TICKS` | 300 | ticks | 60 – 1 800 | Knowledge lost on a kill (≤ the gap) | A write storm on frequent notifications | SL-9; zero PROFILE blocks in the MVP |
| ~~`STATUS_WRITTEN_HOLD_TICKS`~~ **RETIRED** | (was 90) | ticks | — | — | — | Retired with UI-1 *(Amended 2026-10-26 — CD-GDD-ALIGN)*: a per-write glyph held by the gate leaked the gate's inputs to the HUD (P3). The id stays in this table so the registry stage and every reader see the retirement; no consumer may reintroduce it |
| `SUSPECT_THRESHOLD` | 3 | reproduction failures | 2 – 5 | Defects stay silent | False alarms | Diagnostics escalation **only**; dev/test halts on the first occurrence regardless (SL-12) |
| `LOSS_CEILING_TICKS` | 23 400 | ticks | 7 200 – 36 000 | No effect (tolerant) | A boot diagnostic fires for a configuration the team accepts | F-7 `loss_max_stepback`; raising any of the interval, the cap or the window must raise this on purpose |
| `RETIRED_BLOCKS` | empty list | block ids (data) | per-build list | A live block's data is silently dropped | An old save refuses when a block is removed | T-5 registered/retired step; boot assertion `SL_BOOT_RETIRED_BUT_REGISTERED` |
| `EVENT_TRIGGERS` | {`NEW_GAME`} in the MVP baseline; `POD_ARRIVED` (`web_events`), the weld/cut poll of `last_shape_change_T` (`ASSEMBLY_WELD`, `ASSEMBLY_CUT`), the module-cut read `last_salvage_T()` (BS-21) and the dock completion `BERTHED` (`web_events`) as their owners publish them (`POD_ARRIVED` and `BERTHED` provisional until merge; `ASSEMBLY_WELD` / `ASSEMBLY_CUT` registered) | subset | any subset | Write churn (bounded by the minimum gap) | Interval-only saves | SL-4, F-1 |
| `hash_algo` (written) | the kernel's | enum | follows the kernel hash ADR | — | — | SL-16; header; REBASED cause 4 |
| `codec` | 0 | enum | 0 (1 reserved) | Enabling it without decode support refuses files | — | SL-19, VS-A size review |
| Dev-build `FAULTED` behaviour | halt with diagnostics | enum | {halt, menu} | — | The menu in a dev build loses the diff | SL-22 |
| `FUZZ_MUTATION_COUNT` (test) | 1 000 | mutations | ≥ 1 000 | Slow CI | A weak fuzz | Acceptance Criteria (fuzz) |

### Invariants (asserted at boot against the data file)

A violation fails Save/Load's own boot naming the invariant (`SL_BOOT_KNOB_INVARIANT{id}`, composition-root mechanism as in SL-15); I9 is a diagnostic, not a refusal.

| Id | Invariant | Why |
|---|---|---|
| I1 | `AUTOSAVE_MIN_GAP_TICKS` < `AUTOSAVE_INTERVAL_TICKS` | Otherwise events could be due more often than the interval |
| I2 | `AUTOSAVE_MAX_DEFER_TICKS` < `AUTOSAVE_INTERVAL_TICKS` | Otherwise a deferred save collides with the next interval |
| I3 | `AUTOSAVE_RETRY_TICKS` ≤ `AUTOSAVE_MAX_DEFER_TICKS` / 4 and `AUTOSAVE_MAX_DEFER_TICKS` mod `AUTOSAVE_RETRY_TICKS` = 0 | The cap must land on the retry grid |
| I4 | `AUTOSAVE_GENERATIONS` ≥ 2 | A safe swap and a fallback need two |
| I5 | `MAX_BLOCK_BYTES` ≤ `MAX_SAVE_BYTES` | A block cannot exceed its file |
| I6 | `SAVE_SIZE_ALARM_BYTES` < `MAX_BLOCK_BYTES` | The alarm must be able to fire |
| I7 | `MAX_HEADER_BYTES` ≥ 600 + `MAX_BLOCKS` × 150 (19 800 at the kernel's 128) | A full registry must fit |
| I8 | `LOADING_TARGET_MS` > the Pipeline's black-hold fade (120 ms, cited) | Otherwise the target is below what the player sees |
| I9 | `loss_max_stepback` (F-7) ≤ `LOSS_CEILING_TICKS` | Diagnostic: a change that raises the worst case must be deliberate |
| I10 | `GATE_SOFT_STATES` ∩ {`DOWNED`, `DEAD`} = ∅ | A listed hard state would let the cap write a downed world |

### Cited knobs (owned elsewhere — never restated with another value)

| Knob | Value as cited | Owner and source of truth | Used by |
|---|---|---|---|
| `TICK_RATE` | 60 Hz (registry, set-once) | Kernel | Header check, every tick/second conversion |
| `DOWN_TO_DEATH_TICKS` | 1 800 (registry; owner range 600 – 3 600) | Controller | F-3, F-5, F-7; the stub's observation (SL-7) |
| `W_MIN_WARNING_S` | 10 s (registry; range 4 – 20) | Graph | `attention_tell_block` (F-2) and the `W_MIN_STEPS` derivation below |
| `TELL_MAX_TICKS` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass)* | 1 800 ticks (registry) | Attention | `tell_remaining` range (F-2); the `gate_tell_mode` STRICT bound |
| `W_MIN_STEPS` *(Amended 2026-10-26 — CD-GDD-ALIGN)* | 100 (Graph D.11: `W_MIN_WARNING_S × TICK_RATE / GRAPH_STEP_TICKS`, calculated at boot; range 40 – 200) | Graph | `graph_tell_block` (F-2): `d.warn_elapsed < W_MIN_STEPS` |
| `GRAPH_STEP_TICKS` *(Amended 2026-10-26 — CD-GDD-ALIGN)* | 6 ticks (registry; equals the Graph's hook divisor) | Graph | `graph_tell_remaining` (F-2) |
| `HOST_PEER_ID` | 1 (registry) | Kernel | Single-player identity (SL-20) |
| `SNAPSHOT_BUDGET_MS` | 16.7 ms (kernel GDD value, range 8 – 33; kernel registry candidate) | Kernel | F-5 |
| `MAX_BLOCKS` | 128 (kernel GDD value; kernel registry candidate) | Kernel | F-6, I7, T-5 block table step |
| Graph snapshot and restore cost | ≤ 0.5 ms and ≤ 32 KB; restore + `post_restore` ≤ 2 ms | Graph | F-5, F-6, F-8 |
| `TRANSIT_HOLD` fade | 120 ms | Pipeline | F-8, I8, Game Feel |
| `STEP_BUDGET_MS`, `MAX_DIRTY_KEYS` | 4 ms, 256 (registry) | Kernel | Not Save/Load's; it registers no hook and no dirty key (SL-17) |

### Considered and not made knobs

`QUARANTINE_MAX_FILES` (ruled out: quarantined files are never deleted automatically); a `restore wait for write` timeout (the stub waits for `WRITTEN` or `FAILED` (SL-7), bounded by the writer's failure path for the rename step (`RENAME_RETRY_COUNT` × `RENAME_RETRY_DELAY_MS`); a write stuck in flush or re-read is unbounded in principle (F-5), is reachable only if a write that started inside the 1 800-tick `DOWNED` window has not settled, and is measured by the spike (AC-SL-30, AC-SL-88)); `HOT_COPY_ENABLED` and `PRESTAGE_ON_DOWNED` (recorded alternatives, OQ-23, not MVP rules); `format_epoch`, a separate `SAVE_AUDIO_CUE`, any glyph colour and any per-write glyph or its hold time (rejected or retired: SL-21, SL-24, UI-1); a `GRAPH_TELL_MODE` (not made: the Graph window is the guarantee, so the Graph half has nothing to tune); the playtest-trigger constants `SCOUT_DEATH_WINDOW_TICKS` 5 400 and `QUIT_RELAUNCH_WINDOW_S` 120 and the M1 to M3 thresholds (playtest-protocol values in Edge Cases, calibrated at the first outside build, not data-file knobs). *(Amended 2026-10-26 — CD-GDD-ALIGN)*

## Visual/Audio Requirements

Save/Load owns no art and no sound. Its whole visible footprint is two things drawn by other systems from the reads it publishes (SL-21), plus one thing the player must never see. This section records what is visible, what is audible, who renders it, and which art-bible rules bind it, so the owners of those surfaces inherit the constraints instead of rediscovering them.

**What the player can see.**

| Surface | When | Rendered by | Binding rules |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Failed-write notice** *(Amended 2026-10-26 — CD-GDD-ALIGN)* (a plain, non-modal mark on the suit panel; shape and placement are not designed here) | Only after `AUTOSAVE_FAIL_ESCALATE_COUNT` (3) consecutive failed writes (published as `fail_escalated`, SL-21); persists until the next `WRITTEN` | HUD & Suit Layer (designed — hud-and-suit-layer.md; shape and placement are HS-46's), under art bible 7.0 (the HUD is a suit) | HR-1: it lives on the HUD layer, never dims, fades or flickers with world light, Attention or sanity. It takes **no Flesh** (`#D9A58C`, `#B5705C`) and **no Violet** (`#8A3FD1`, `#B07BE6`): those hues are reserved for the player's sanity leaking into the world (art bible 4.4), and a mark that could take them would be a lie the player could not tell from a leak (SL-24, H5). It is never a hallucination class: a false "failed" would be an unfalsifiable lie on the one channel that must stay true. It sits on an opaque Ground plate (HR-2) and counts inside the HUD's roughly 1 % non-Ground area cap (7.0). It is tied to write health, never to the gate: it looks and behaves identically whether the gate is deferring or not, and it says nothing about when a save was or will be taken |
| **Newest-record line** *(Amended 2026-10-26 — CD-GDD-ALIGN)* (pause panel only) | While the pause panel is open | Chart Table (designed — chart-table-web-map-and-menus.md), through `/ux-design` | One SIM fact: the playtime of the newest generation (`SaveInfo.playtime`), static while paused. No countdown, no "time since", no per-write mark, no gate state. Ground panel with the world undimmed (7.4); no Flesh or Violet; never a hallucination class |
| **Layer-1 black hold** during `LOADING` | From the `DEAD` tick (death stub) or the Continue press to the first `RUNNING` frame; bounded by `LOADING_TARGET_MS` (1 000, provisional) | Pixel-Perfect Render Pipeline (`TRANSIT_HOLD`, a 120 ms fade) | Plain black on layer 1. No death screen, no text, no beat: the art bible's 2.10 "last light going out" sequence is written for the cryo re-wake and belongs to Failure Contract & Re-wake (VS-A, provisional — undesigned); the death stub does **not** render it, and must stay retire-able with no presentation change beyond what VS-A adds |
| **First `RUNNING` frame after a load** | The frame after the hold | Pipeline (camera `CUT` at the pre-load `k_z`), Perception Filter (designed) | The frame must already be filtered for the restored sanity level (SL-24, H4): the black hold covers any rebuild, and a single unfiltered frame is a free oracle about a hallucination. This is a routed request (OQ-11), not a Save/Load render |
| **Menu and pause surfaces**: Save & Quit, Continue, New campaign, the hold-to-confirm "quit without saving", the corrupt-ring, no-loadable-save and incompatible-build messages | On the pause panel and main menu | Chart Table (designed — chart-table-web-map-and-menus.md), through `/ux-design` | Art bible 7.4: the pause panel is a right-third side panel on Ground over the frozen world, and **the world is not dimmed, blurred or desaturated** under it (dimming is a mask on the world, blur is a lens lie, desaturation is the sanity convention 4.4 rejected). Hold-to-confirm fills a **linear bar with a needle-mark**, never a radial wipe (7.3, HR-14). Cuts and short fades of 80 – 120 ms, no slides or bounces (7.4). Selection is the 7.2 bracket plus bezel thickening, never a Tungsten fill (7.4 menu components) |

**What the player never sees.** No save-list **thumbnail** (a screenshot would capture the Perception Filter's lie, Pillar 1), no ring, spinner or percentage (a "saving…" meter would turn persistence into infrastructure in the player's face and contradict "shown in the world, never a meter"), no **death screen** (art bible 7.4 and 2.10: the alternative "a death screen with text" was considered and rejected), no "progress saved" toast, no completion or progress indicator on a campaign (no win state). **No per-write autosave mark of any kind** *(Amended 2026-10-26 — CD-GDD-ALIGN)*: writes are held by the gate (an active tell, a breach phase that is not calm, a seat in `CHANNELLING`, `STAGGERED`, `IN_POD` or `DOWNED`), so a mark that appeared on every write would turn its absence into a HUD signal that danger is building and, once Attention publishes its breach phase, into a hidden Attention meter (P3: "shown in the world, never a meter"). The glyph and `STATUS_WRITTEN_HOLD_TICKS` are retired; only the write-health notice above remains. The campaign list shows only `SaveInfo` facts computed from SIM blocks: campaign id, generation kind, playtime, crew count, `RISKY`, and a depth label (provisional — derived from the node `ring` of `web.view`).

**What the player can hear.** **Nothing, in the MVP.** There is no save chime, no write tick, no failure sting and no load sound. Reason: any sound is attributable by the web (Pillar 3), and silence is the correct cue for an event the player should not perceive at all. If the Audio design later justifies a cue, it enters through the Audio GDD, never as a Save/Load rule, and must not be confusable with a hallucinated sound (Pillar 4). The black hold is silent in this GDD; whether ambient audio ducks across `LOADING` is the Audio and Pipeline owners' call.

**Strings.** The few player-facing strings this system sources (corrupt ring, incompatible build, no loadable save, failed write, quit held) are plain, factual and low-affect, in a ship-log register: no exclamation marks, no emoji, no second-person death phrase, no colour from the sane or insane palette (Player Fantasy, tone). Final copy is the UX designer's and the narrative director's.

**Art bible sections that apply:** 2.10 (Crew loss — explicitly not rendered here), 4.4 (the sanity palette: Flesh and Violet reserved), 7.0 (HUD-as-suit, ~1 % area cap), 7.3 (linear hold-to-confirm bar, three motions only), 7.4 (menus: world not dimmed; no death screen), 7.7 HR-1 (HUD exemption) and HR-14 (gamepad completeness, hold-to-confirm for destructive actions). Retained-screenshot evidence for each touched screen is AC-SL-92 and AC-SL-117.

📌 **Asset Spec** — Visual/Audio requirements are defined. After the art bible is approved, run `/asset-spec system:save-load-and-versioned-format` to produce per-asset visual descriptions, dimensions, and generation prompts from this section.

## Game Feel

The player never feels a save system; they feel what it guarantees (Player Fantasy: trust without a safety net). The feel of this system is therefore a set of **absences and one half-second**, each with an owner and a measurable target. Save/Load owns the timing of the two moments below; it owns no animation, sound or camera move.

**1. A save is imperceptible.** *Feel:* the world runs continuously and the ship "just exists". *Target:* the main-thread cost Save/Load adds to the kernel's capture (`t_encode + t_handoff`, F-5) keeps the main-thread sum `main_hitch_ms` inside one frame at 60 Hz (`SNAPSHOT_BUDGET_MS` 16.7, cited); the file write, flush, re-read and rename run off the critical path (SL-10); no frame within ±2 frames of a capture start exceeds 25 ms (AC-SL-91). *What would feel wrong:* a stutter every three minutes that teaches the player when the game saved — it would turn every autosave into a small event and hand the player a rhythm to exploit — and, for the same reason, any per-write mark: there is none, because a mark shown on every write and held by the gate would make its absence a signal that danger is building (P2, P3) *(Amended 2026-10-26 — CD-GDD-ALIGN)*. *Owner:* the kernel owns capture cost; Save/Load owns the added encode and hand-off; no system owns a glyph, because none exists.

**2. A death is a breath of black, then recognition.** *Feel:* "death is a bitch. It happens." The loss lands in the body of the game, not in an interface: the avatar goes `DOWNED` for 30 seconds (`DOWN_TO_DEATH_TICKS` 1 800, the Controller's grief window and the real tension), then on the `DEAD` tick the stub restores. The player sees layer-1 black for **at least 120 ms** (the `TRANSIT_HOLD` fade; shorter reads as a flash or a glitch) and **at most about 2 s** (longer reads as a crash); `LOADING_TARGET_MS` 1 000 (provisional) is the budget inside that corridor, measured as `restart_ms` (F-8). The first `RUNNING` frame is a hard camera `CUT` at the **pre-load zoom** (`k_z` is kept), so the player's framing is unchanged and recognition is instant: *I am back at the weld. The ship is exactly as I made it. I know what is in that room now.* *What would feel wrong:* a death screen, a sting, a "you died" line, a lights-out beat (2.10 is for the cryo re-wake, VS-A), a zoom reset, a menu asking which save to load, or any penalty beyond the lost minutes — each would turn consequence into ceremony or punishment.

**3. The cost of dying is a number the designer can feel.** The player pays the minutes since the last autosave and nothing else: at the defaults the mean is about 7 200 ticks (2 minutes), the ceiling 19 800 ticks (5.5 minutes) and 23 400 after one conditional step-back (F-7). `AUTOSAVE_INTERVAL_TICKS` is the single lever of how much death hurts (OQ-19): too cheap and "safe farming" returns, which Pillar 5 rejects ("scarcity that pushes inward"); too dear and death becomes a quit. These are playtest-owned numbers, measured by the 3-person kill/continue playtest (AC-SL-94) against the triggers M1 to M3 in Edge Cases, not design of consequence.

**4. The refusal, not the rescue.** The autosave gate (SL-5) is felt only as an absence: the player never wakes into a moment they could not have seen coming, because a world in a `DOWNED` state is never recorded. The player does **not** feel rescued — the save never buys back a mistake (no save-anywhere, SL-4) and the physical world's draws are restored exactly, so the room that killed them kills them again if they walk the same path (SL-23). Hallucination interplay *(Amended 2026-10-26 — CD-GDD-ALIGN)*: a reload is never a cure (H3) and Save/Load adds no window onto truth; whether a false state replays at the same moment is Sanity's decision, with the constraint that death and quit offer no reroll oracle (H2, OQ-37).

**5. Failure is quiet and honest.** A failed write shows the plain `FAILED` notice only after `AUTOSAVE_FAIL_ESCALATE_COUNT` (3) consecutive failures; a corrupt newest generation is a one-line ship-log message and a fall back to the one before it; an incompatible build refuses to touch the campaign. None of these ever frightens: the system's tone is the opposite of the world's.

**Who owns the feel.** Black-hold timing, fade and camera `CUT`: Pixel-Perfect Render Pipeline & Camera (cited). The 30 s `DOWNED` grief window: Player Controller & Input. The failure notice: HUD & Suit Layer (designed — hud-and-suit-layer.md); the pause panel's newest-record line: Chart Table (designed). The re-wake beat that will replace the bare stub: Failure Contract & Re-wake (VS-A, provisional — undesigned). The filter on the first frame: Perception Filter (designed; OQ-11). Save/Load owns the numbers in this section that are its own: `LOADING_TARGET_MS`, `AUTOSAVE_INTERVAL_TICKS`, and `AUTOSAVE_MAX_DEFER_TICKS` (the written-hold of the status read is retired). Evidence: AC-SL-84, AC-SL-91 and AC-SL-93 (retained screenshots and frame-time logs, lead sign-off).

## UI Requirements

Save/Load draws nothing itself. It publishes host-local reads and a host-local API (SL-21); every surface below is **drawn and laid out by another system** (HUD & Suit Layer and Chart Table, both designed — hud-and-suit-layer.md, chart-table-web-map-and-menus.md) under the art bible's HUD-as-suit grammar (7.0), the menu rules (7.4) and the hard requirements HR-1 and HR-14. This section is the requirements list those UX specs inherit. The player-facing vocabulary is fixed: **Continue**, **Save & Quit**, **New campaign**; never "load game", "slot", "checkpoint", "respawn", "save point", "game over", "you died", "progress saved" or "quicksave" (Player Fantasy, tone).

| # | Surface | Data it reads or calls | Requirements | Owner of the layout |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| UI-1 | ~~**Autosave glyph** (suit panel)~~ **RETIRED** *(Amended 2026-10-26 — CD-GDD-ALIGN)* | — | Retired. No per-write glyph in the MVP. The glyph appeared on every write while writes are held by the autosave gate (an active tell, a breach phase that is not calm, a seat in `CHANNELLING`, `STAGGERED`, `IN_POD` or `DOWNED`), so a due autosave with no glyph would tell the HUD wearer that danger was building, a hidden Attention meter against P3 and against "You never see the save". Not renumbered; its replacements are the UI-2 newest-record line and the UI-10 failed-write notice | — |
| UI-2 | **Pause panel: Save & Quit** | `save_and_quit()`; `save_status` | Right-third side panel on Ground over the frozen world, which is **not** dimmed, blurred or desaturated (7.4). Shows one recency fact, the **newest-record line**: the playtime of the newest generation (`SaveInfo.playtime`), a SIM fact that is static while paused — no countdown, no "time since", no gate state, no per-write mark *(Amended 2026-10-26 — CD-GDD-ALIGN)*. One action writes a `QUIT` generation then exits. While any bound seat is `DOWNED` the action writes nothing and routes to UI-3 (`SL_QUIT_NO_WRITE_DOWNED`). A held or failed write keeps the panel and shows the plain `FAILED` flag with retry and UI-3 as the two ways out (SL-6) | Chart Table |
| UI-3 | **Hold-to-confirm "quit without saving"** | Result `SL_QUIT_NO_WRITE_DOWNED`, or a held write (`SAVE_QUIT_TIMEOUT_MS`) | Destructive: fills a **linear bar with a needle-mark**, never a radial wipe (7.3, HR-14); gamepad-complete; the string states that the newest autosave will be used (Continue restores the newest verified generation) | Chart Table |
| UI-4 | **Main menu: Continue / New campaign** | `campaign_info()` (newest `SaveInfo`); `continue_campaign()`; `new_game(world_seed)`; `list_saves()` | One Continue for the one logical autosave — the generation ring is invisible and the player never chooses a generation. `SaveInfo` shows only SIM facts: playtime, crew count, kind, `RISKY`, depth label (provisional). **No thumbnail**, no completion or progress figure, no "last played" derived from wall clock beyond file metadata | Chart Table |
| UI-5 | **New campaign over an existing one** | `retire_campaign(id)`, `delete_campaign(id)` | Default action is **retire** (reversible); **delete** is a separate explicit action under hold-to-confirm on a linear bar (HR-14). Quarantined files are removed only by delete | Chart Table |
| UI-6 | **Load screen / black hold** | `restore_reason`; kernel `LOADING` | Layer-1 black via `TRANSIT_HOLD`; no text, no tip, no progress; no death screen; the first `RUNNING` frame is a camera `CUT` (Pipeline). `restore_reason` is offered to the HUD, Pipeline and Failure Contract as a request; no MVP consumer may alter hallucination content from `DEATH_STUB` (SL-21, SL-24) | Pipeline; Failure Contract (VS-A) |
| UI-7 | **Corrupt-ring notice** | A load that fell back from a CORRUPT generation; on the main menu, `campaign_info().newest_failed_verification` (ASK-CT-5) | One plain ship-log line (placeholder: "Newest record unreadable. Continuing from the one before it."), shown once on the next `RUNNING` frame or on the menu; no alarm styling (SL-11) | Chart Table (main menu, when the flag is set) / HUD & Suit Layer (first `RUNNING` frame otherwise) |
| UI-8 | **`SL_LOAD_NO_LOADABLE_SAVE` screen** | Published reason; shown by the Chart Table as a boot-flow screen; the kernel is not `RUNNING` and no pause is requested (CT-25; in-session death-stub teardown, OQ-42) | A plain statement that no readable record exists, with New campaign and Quit; no file is deleted and no autosave of the dead world occurs (SL-7) | Chart Table |
| UI-9 | **Incompatible-build message** | `INCOMPATIBLE` class; campaign `READ_ONLY` | Placeholder: "This save was made by an incompatible build."; offers Quit and New campaign; never offers to overwrite or "repair" (SL-11, T-9) | Chart Table |
| UI-10 | **Failed-write notice** | `fail_escalated` (true after `AUTOSAVE_FAIL_ESCALATE_COUNT` (3) consecutive failures; `save_status` reads `FAILED` on every one) | Tied to write health only (consecutive failed writes), never to the gate, and the only autosave-related mark on the HUD in the MVP. Plain, persistent, non-modal; names no cause beyond "the game could not write its record"; cleared by the next `WRITTEN` | HUD & Suit Layer (shape and placement, HS-46) |

**Rules that bind every surface.** No thumbnail, no ring or percentage, no meter, no audio cue, no death screen, no palette hue from the sanity channel; all destructive actions are hold-to-confirm on a linear bar; every action is reachable by gamepad (HR-14); strings are plain, factual and low-affect with no exclamation mark; none of these surfaces is ever a hallucination class (Pillar 4: at least one channel of truth remains, and the save UI is that channel). The pause panel's physical look, the hold duration (not a number in the art bible — OQ-32) and the final copy are the Chart Table's `/ux-design` and the narrative director's, not this GDD's.

**Accessibility.** Save/Load adds no timing-critical input: the hold-to-confirm duration must be adjustable by the accessibility options (`design/accessibility-requirements.md`), and the failure notice and the newest-record line must not rely on colour alone (shape and text, per HR-10, no global hue remap).

> **📌 UX Flag — Save/Load & Versioned Format**: This system has UI requirements. In Phase 4 (Pre-Production), run `/ux-design` to create a UX spec for each screen or HUD element this system contributes to **before** writing epics: the failure notice (HUD & Suit Layer; the autosave glyph is retired), and the Save & Quit (with its newest-record line) / hold-to-confirm / Continue / New campaign / no-loadable-save / incompatible-build surfaces (Chart Table). Stories that reference UI should cite `design/ux/[screen].md`, not the GDD directly. Retained screenshots of each screen are the evidence gate (AC-SL-92, AC-SL-117).
